\paragraph{Proof of the signed envelope and recovery.}
Keep the exact original residue:
\[
 r_j=-A+
 \sum_{\rho\in S}
  \Re\!\left(\frac{d_j\rho}{\rho+d_j-1}\right)
                  \operatorname{ord}_{q_j(\rho)}\zeta.
\]
Every retained reference coefficient is nonnegative. Indeed, for
\(\rho=\sigma+i\tau\),
\[
 \Re\!\left(\frac{d\rho}{\rho+d-1}\right)
 =\frac{d\sigma(\sigma+d-1)+d\tau^2}
        {(\sigma+d-1)^2+\tau^2}\ge0
 \qquad(d>1,\ \sigma\ge0).
\]
This proves \(r_j\ge-A\). If \(B_j=0\), all its nonnegative integer
summands vanish, so the full reference contribution vanishes and
\(r_j=-A\).

The strict size inequality and~\eqref{eq:near-identity-bad-budget}
force a clean \(j\in T\). For every row the noise bound and \(r_j\ge-A\)
give \(y_j>-A-1/2\), whence
\(\lfloor-y_j+1/2\rfloor\le A\).
For a clean row, \(|-A-y_j|<1/2\) gives equality.
Taking the finite maximum proves~\eqref{eq:near-identity-panel-decode}.
Finally, \(M+1>|S|/(20\lambda)\) proves the explicit block-size condition,
and monotonicity of \(1/(j+1)\) proves the power bound.

\paragraph{Actual analytic observations and formal scope.}
For an arbitrary analytic strip correction \(G\), use the actual observations
\[
 y_j(t)=\sum_{\rho\in S}
 \Re\!\left(t\,\operatorname{affinePowerReadout}
       (-\zeta'/\zeta+G,d_j)(\rho+t)\right).
\]
The completed finite-packet limit gives \(y_j(t)\to r_j\) as
\(t\to0,\ t\ne0\). For fixed finite \(T\), all the strict half-unit
error inequalities therefore hold simultaneously in a punctured neighborhood
of zero. The floor-maximum decoder is consequently exactly equal to \(A\)
throughout that neighborhood. The set, rectangle, base, and panel are fixed
before taking this limit; no uniform step-size or sampling-rate bound is claimed.

The affine geometry and full signed coefficient inequality are checked in
\texttt{Mathlib.Analysis.Complex.NearIdentityResiduePanel}; the actual zero
budget, contamination count, clean-row existence, decoder, block construction,
power bound, and eventual analytic recovery are checked in
\texttt{Assembly.CarmichaelNearIdentityPanel}.
The preceding fixed-degree real aggregate recovered an index and quadratic
parity. This panel recovers the target total by using several real observations;
it does not recover all the varying reference orders.

The background includes global Li coefficients and Weil functionals
(Lagarias, arXiv:math/0404394), and global inverse-power zero sums
(Sekatskii, \textit{On the Sums over Inverse Powers of Zeroes of the Hurwitz
Zeta-Function}, 2024,
\url{https://www.preprints.org/manuscript/202401.0068/v1/download}).
Here the global budget instead controls collisions across compensation
parameters and gives a finite stable inverse formula. Targeted searches
found no matching near-identity finite-panel statement; priority remains
unestablished. The certified global zero-free boundary remains \(7/8\).

\subsection{Phase multiplicity profiles and the QRH zero-mass budget}
\label{sec:phase-multiplicity-mass}

Write \(m(z)=\operatorname{ord}_z\zeta\), and retain the full residue
\[
 R_d(\rho)=-m(\rho)+\frac{d\rho}{\rho+d-1}m(q_d\rho),
 \qquad q_d\rho=(\rho+d-1)/d.
\]
For \(d>1\) and \(\Im\rho\ne0\), define the normalized phase
\[
 \Phi_d(\rho)=
 \frac{|\rho+d-1|^2}{d(d-1)\Im\rho}\Im R_d(\rho).
\]
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:phase-reference-order}
 \Phi_d(\rho)=m(q_d\rho).
\end{equation}
Indeed,
\[
 \Im\frac{d\rho}{\rho+d-1}
   =\frac{d(d-1)\Im\rho}{|\rho+d-1|^2};
\]
the target term is real and disappears from this component.
No reference order is discarded or assumed zero.

Set \(\rho_{d,t}=\tfrac12+i\,dt\), \(t\ne0\), and
\(P(d,t)=\Phi_d(\rho_{d,t})\). The exact identity becomes
\begin{equation}\label{eq:critical-phase-profile}
 P(d,t)=m\left(1-\frac1{2d}+it\right)\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}.
\end{equation}
Every nonreal \(z\) with \(\tfrac12<\Re z<1\) occurs uniquely, with
\(d=1/(2(1-\Re z))\) and \(t=\Im z\).
For each \(b\ge\tfrac12\) and integer \(M\ge0\), the following are
therefore equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
 \item \(m(z)\le M\) at every nonreal \(z\) with \(b<\Re z<1\);
 \item \(P(d,t)\le M\) whenever \(d>1\), \(t\ne0\), and
       \(b<1-1/(2d)\).
\end{enumerate}
This is an equivalence of the complete multiplicity profile, not only
a Boolean zero detector. In particular, the maintained theorem
\texttt{critical\_phase\_three\_quarters\_subunit\_iff} proves
\[
 [\,P(d,t)<1\text{ for every }d>2,\ t\ne0\,]
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 [\,\zeta(z)\ne0\text{ for every nonreal }
       z\text{ with }3/4<\Re z<1\,].
\]
The integer gap makes the strict bound below one sufficient.
This statement explicitly concerns nonreal zeros; it does not provide
a phase test for real zeros of general Dirichlet \(L\)-functions.

These are actual analytic observations. For any correction \(G\)
analytic on the strip, the scaled compensated readout converges to
\(R_d(\rho)\); its normalized imaginary part converges to
\(m(q_d\rho)\). The maintained declaration is
\texttt{critical\_phase\_readout\_limit}. This is a limit theorem at
each fixed center, not a uniform finite-sample error estimate.

\paragraph{Independent zero mass supplied by the existing QRH theorem.}
The already proved complete positive trace is
\[
 \sum_{\rho\ {\rm distinct}}m(\rho)K(\rho)=C_\zeta,\qquad
 K(\rho)=\Re\frac1{\rho(1-\rho)},\qquad
 C_\zeta=\gamma_E+2-\log(4\pi)\le\frac1{20}.
\]
Its maintained proof already consumes the unconditional \(7/8\)
provider to control the full zero strip and convergence.
Reflection gives \(1/8\le\beta=\Re\rho\le7/8\).
For \(u=(\Im\rho)^2\), \(c=\beta(1-\beta)\),
\[
 K(\rho)=\frac{u+c}{u^2+(1-2c)u+c^2},\qquad
 \frac1{K(\rho)}
   =u+1-3c+\frac{c(4c-1)}{u+c}.
\]
Here \(7/64\le c\le1/4\), so
\(1\le(u+43/64)K(\rho)\).
For every finite set \(S\) of distinct nontrivial zeros with
\(|\Im\rho|\le T\), \(T\ge0\), summing this inequality yields
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qrh-finite-multiplicity-budget}
 \sum_{\rho\in S}m(\rho)
 \le C_\zeta(T^2+43/64)
 \le \frac{T^2+43/64}{20}.
\end{equation}
No cardinality factor is introduced. The maintained count theorem
uses a finite set of distinct xi zeros and their full multiplicities,
including both signs of the ordinate. Xi and zeta multiplicities
agree at these strip zeros.

The QRH theorem also gives \(P(d,t)=0\) for every \(d>4\).
Thus the open interval \(2<d\le4\) is the precise additional phase
range needed for the nonreal \(3/4\) boundary. The independent
quadratic budget above does not give a subunit phase bound there,
nor does it prove simplicity. It is much weaker in height than
classical zero-counting estimates and is recorded as a consumer
of the existing trace, not as an improved zero-density estimate.
Local residue recovery, its global profile equivalence, and an
independent sharp observable estimate are separate mathematical steps.

The generic owner is
\texttt{Mathlib/Analysis/Complex/ResidueReferencePhase.lean};
the actual consumers are in
\texttt{Assembly/CarmichaelPhaseMultiplicity.lean}.
The existing multiplicity-recovery theorems are reused.
The phase specialization and strip-to-count envelope are elementary
consequences, with no claim of established novelty priority.
The certified zero-free boundary remains \(7/8\).

\subsection{The seven-eighths strip bounds localized multiplicity mass}
\label{sec:localized-paired-multiplicity}

The preceding positive global budget can be localized more efficiently.
Let \(F=\xi'/\xi\), and let
\[
 A_r(T)=\frac12\left\{\Re F\left(\tfrac12+r+iT\right)
                   +\Re F\left(\tfrac12+r-iT\right)\right\}.
\]
The installed unconditional \(7/8\) theorem and the functional equation
place every xi zero \(\rho=\beta+i\gamma\) in
\(1/8\le\beta\le7/8\), with its full analytic multiplicity \(m(\rho)\).
The maintained theorem
\texttt{zeta\_multiplicity\_seven\_eighths\_log\_bound} proves
\begin{equation}\label{eq:localized-multiplicity-log-bound}
 \exists C>0\quad
 m(\beta+i\gamma)\le\frac{3\sqrt2}{32}\log|\gamma|+C
 \qquad(0<\beta<1,\ |\gamma|\ge2).
\end{equation}
The constant is independent of the zero. The formal theorem also allows
nonzeros, where the analytic order is zero. The leading coefficient is
approximately \(0.13258\); the additive constant is existential, and no
numerical starting height is supplied.

\paragraph{A jointly positive compensated kernel.}
For real \(x,y\) and \(|x|<r<R\), define
\[
 Q_r(x,y)=\frac12\left\{
 \frac{r-x}{(r-x)^2+y^2}+\frac{r+x}{(r+x)^2+y^2}\right\},
 \qquad K_{r,R}=Q_r-\frac rR Q_R,
\]
and
\[
 D_r=((r-x)^2+y^2)((r+x)^2+y^2).
\]
The complete numerator identity is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:paired-resolvent-numerator}
 K_{r,R}(x,y)=\frac{r(R^2-r^2)}{D_rD_R}
 \left\{(r^2+y^2-x^2)(R^2+y^2-x^2)-4x^2y^2\right\}.
\end{equation}
All four resolvent terms remain in this identity. Set \(k=r^2-2x^2\).
If \(k\ge0\), its expression in braces equals
\[
 ((y-x)^2+k)((y+x)^2+k)
 +(R^2-r^2)(r^2+y^2-x^2)\ge0.
\]
Thus \(K_{r,R}(x,y)\ge0\) for every \(y\) whenever
\(2x^2\le r^2\).
The infinitesimal threshold is sharp: the first product is nonnegative
for every \(y\) if and only if \(2x^2\le r^2\).
For necessity, setting \(y=x\) gives \(r^4-4x^4\).

At the zero's own ordinate,
\[
 K_{r,R}(x,0)=
 \frac{r(R^2-r^2)}{(r^2-x^2)(R^2-x^2)}
 \ge K_{r,R}(0,0)=\frac1r-\frac r{R^2}>0.
\]
Indeed both denominator factors are positive and their product is at
most \(r^2R^2\). These identities, positivity statements, sharpness,
and the central minimum are proved in
\texttt{Mathlib/Analysis/Complex/PairedResolventKernel.lean}.

\paragraph{The actual complete zero trace.}
The genus-one Hadamard formula has the same polynomial constant at
every evaluation point:
\[
 F(s)=B+\sum_\rho m(\rho)\left\{\frac1{s-\rho}+\frac1\rho\right\}.
\]
For each fixed \(r>1/2,T\), subtract the formulas at
\(s_\pm=1/2\pm r+iT\). The constant \(B\) and every reciprocal
correction cancel exactly. Both points are separated from the zero
strip. The inverse-square xi-zero summability theorem therefore
justifies the subtraction and taking real parts. More explicitly,
if \(|s-\rho|\ge\delta>0\), then
\[
 \left|\frac1{s-\rho}+\frac1\rho\right|
 =\frac{|s|}{|\rho|\,|s-\rho|}
 \le \frac{|s|(\delta+|s|)}{\delta|\rho|^2},
\]
using \(|\rho|\le|s-\rho|+|s|\).

The functional equation gives \(F(1-s)=-F(s)\), hence
\[
 A_r(T)=\sum_\rho m(\rho)
 Q_r\left(\beta-\tfrac12,T-\gamma\right).
\]
In particular, with no reindexing or loss of reciprocal terms,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:complete-compensated-xi-trace}
 A_r(T)-\frac rR A_R(T)=
 \sum_\rho m(\rho)
 K_{r,R}\left(\beta-\tfrac12,T-\gamma\right).
\end{equation}
Every sum here is absolutely convergent after the displayed real
pairing, at each fixed \(T\). No simultaneous growing-height
interchange is used.

For \(r>1/2\) and \(r^2\ge9/32\), the \(7/8\) strip ensures
\(2(\beta-1/2)^2\le r^2\). Every term of
\eqref{eq:complete-compensated-xi-trace} is nonnegative.
Charging the entire canonical index fibre of a chosen zero, rather
than one representative, gives the compiled inequality
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qrh-compensated-multiplicity}
 m(\rho)\left(\frac1r-\frac r{R^2}\right)
 \le A_r(\gamma)-\frac rR A_R(\gamma).
\end{equation}
The canonical fibre has cardinality \(m(\rho)\), by the existing
xi--zeta multiplicity equivalence. This is the actual \(7/8\)
consumer, proved as
\texttt{zeta\_multiplicity\_le\_compensated\_xi\_trace}.

\paragraph{Independent bounds with the logarithmic coefficient retained.}
For \(\Re s>1\), the exact shifted-gamma identity is
\[
 F(s)=\frac{\zeta'}{\zeta}(s)+\frac1{s-1}
       -\frac12\log\pi+\frac12\psi(s/2+1).
\]
The shift by one absorbs the separate \(1/s\) term.
The existing Euler-product theorem bounds
\(|\zeta'/\zeta(\sigma+it)|\le1/(\sigma-1)+C_E\)
uniformly in \(t\), for every \(\sigma>1\).
On any fixed closed half-plane \(\Re s\ge a>1\), Cauchy's estimate
on balls of radius \((a-1)/2\), followed by the convex-domain
mean-value inequality, also gives a uniform Lipschitz constant for
\(\zeta'/\zeta\). The pole term has the same property.

For completeness, the sharp gamma estimate is obtained directly
from its convergent series, for \(1\le\Re z\le b\) and
\(|\Im z|\ge1\). Put \(N=\lceil|\Im z|\rceil+1\). Then
\[
 \psi(z)+\gamma_E
 =H_N-\sum_{n<N}\frac1{z+n}
   +\sum_{n\ge N}\left(\frac1{n+1}-\frac1{z+n}\right).
\]
The inverse-resolvent head has norm at most
\(N/|\Im z|\le3\). The full tail has norm at most \(b+2\):
\[
 \left|\frac1{n+1}-\frac1{z+n}\right|
 \le\frac{|z|+1}{(n+1)^2},\qquad
 \sum_{n\ge N}\frac1{(n+1)^2}\le\frac1N,\qquad
 |z|+1\le(b+2)N.
\]
Also \(H_N\le\log|\Im z|+1+\log3\). Consequently
\[
 \Re\psi(z)\le\log|\Im z|
       +b+6+\log3+|\gamma_E|.
\]
For \(\Re z,\Re w\ge1\), subtracting the two complete digamma series
and retaining their common harmonic cancellation gives
\[
 |\psi(z)-\psi(w)|
 \le |z-w|\sum_{n\ge0}\frac1{(n+1)^2}.
\]
These two estimates are compiled in
\texttt{Helpers/RealDigammaHeight.lean}. Their Euler and pole
consumers in \texttt{Helpers/XiVerticalLogDerivative.lean} prove:
for each fixed \(r>1/2\), there exist \(C_0,L\ge0\) such that
\[
 A_r(T)\le\tfrac12\log|T|+C_0\quad(|T|\ge2),\qquad
 A_r(T)-A_R(T)\le L(R-r)\quad(R>r),
\]
uniformly in height. The estimates are independent of an assumed
zero. Absolute estimates are used for these bounded arithmetic and
gamma remainders, after the signed zero trace has been retained.

\paragraph{Cancelling the common scale and taking the endpoint.}
Preserve the subtraction as
\[
 A_r-\frac rR A_R
 =\frac{R-r}{R}A_r+\frac rR(A_r-A_R).
\]
Substitution into \eqref{eq:qrh-compensated-multiplicity} yields
\[
 m(\rho)\left(\frac1r-\frac r{R^2}\right)
 \le\frac{R-r}{R}\left(\tfrac12\log|\gamma|+C_0\right)
     +\frac rR L(R-r).
\]
For \(R>r\), multiplication by \(rR^2/(R-r)>0\) gives
\[
 m(\rho)(R+r)
 \le rR\left(\tfrac12\log|\gamma|+C_0+rL\right).
\]
Both sides are continuous polynomials in \(R\). The right-hand
limit \(R\downarrow r\) therefore proves
\[
 m(\rho)\le\frac r4\log|\gamma|
                 +\frac r2 C_0+\frac{r^2}2L.
\]
Choose \(r=3\sqrt2/8>1/2\), so \(r^2=9/32\), and enlarge the
constant to obtain \eqref{eq:localized-multiplicity-log-bound}.
The maintained general-radius theorem and its radical specialization
are in \texttt{Assembly/CarmichaelLocalizedMultiplicity.lean}.

This bounds the multiplicity mass of each zero through an independently
controlled signed observable. It supplies neither simplicity nor a
smaller zero-free boundary. The classical unconditional order
\(m(\rho)=O(\log|\gamma|)\) is not claimed as new; see A.~Ivi\'c,
\emph{On the multiplicity of zeros of the zeta-function},
arXiv:math/0501434. The contribution here is the proved compensated
strip-to-coefficient argument and its consumption of the installed
\(7/8\) strip. Novelty priority for the coefficient is unestablished.

\subsection{Exact finite-radius optimization of the multiplicity coefficient}
\label{sec:sharp-finite-resolvent}

The preceding sufficient condition loses information when the two radii
are unequal. Keeping their full dependence gives an exact classification.
For \(0\le w<r<R\), put
\[
 a=\sqrt{r^2-w^2},\qquad b=\sqrt{R^2-w^2}.
\]
Then the maintained theorem \texttt{pairedResolventStrip\_nonneg\_iff} proves
\begin{equation}\label{eq:sharp-finite-strip}
 \bigl[K_{r,R}(x,y)\ge0\quad\text{for all }|x|\le w,
                       \ y\in\mathbb R\bigr]
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad a+b\ge2w.
\end{equation}
This includes every imaginary displacement; no zero phase is discarded.

\paragraph{Proof of the exact strip condition.}
Let \(H(x,y)\) denote the square-bracket numerator in
\eqref{eq:paired-resolvent-numerator}. Its boundary value is
\[
 H(w,y)=(y^2-ab)^2+\bigl((a+b)^2-4w^2\bigr)y^2.
\]
Moreover, for every \(|x|\le w\),
\[
 H(x,y)-H(w,y)
 =(w^2-x^2)(r^2+R^2+6y^2-x^2-w^2)\ge0.
\]
The denominator and the prefactor in
\eqref{eq:paired-resolvent-numerator} are positive. Thus \(a+b\ge2w\)
implies positivity on the whole strip. Conversely, \(a,b>0\), and the
explicit choice \(x=w\), \(y=\sqrt{ab}\) gives
\[
 H(w,\sqrt{ab})=ab\bigl((a+b)^2-4w^2\bigr).
\]
It is negative if \(a+b<2w\). This proves both directions, including
an explicit counterexample when the condition fails. The algebraic
identities, monotonicity across the strip, equivalence and negative
witness are formalized in \texttt{PairedResolventSharpStrip.lean}.

\paragraph{The complete constrained optimizer for width \(3/8\).}
Restrict to the compensated two-line class with weight \(r/R\), and to
\(1/2\le r<R\). Define
\[
 \ell=\frac{\sqrt7}{8},\qquad
 R_* = \frac{\sqrt{13-3\sqrt7}}4,\qquad
 c(r,R)=\frac{rR}{2(r+R)},\qquad
 c_* = \frac{\sqrt{13-3\sqrt7}}
              {4(2+\sqrt{13-3\sqrt7})}.
\]
Among every pair satisfying \eqref{eq:sharp-finite-strip} for
\(w=3/8\), the maintained optimizer proves
\begin{equation}\label{eq:sharp-finite-coefficient}
 c(r,R)\ge c_*,\qquad
 c(r,R)=c_*\ \Longleftrightarrow\ r=1/2,\ R=R_*.
\end{equation}
The ordering of the radii removes the swapped equality case.

Here is the optimization proof. Write
\(u=\sqrt{r^2-w^2}\), \(v=\sqrt{R^2-w^2}\). Then
\[
 \ell\le u<v,\qquad u+v\ge2w=\frac34,
 \qquad g(t)=\frac1{\sqrt{w^2+t^2}}.
\]
On \([\ell,\infty)\), \(g\) is strictly decreasing and strictly convex:
\[
 g'(t)=-t(w^2+t^2)^{-3/2},\qquad
 g''(t)=(2t^2-w^2)(w^2+t^2)^{-5/2}>0,
 \qquad 2\ell^2-w^2=\frac5{64}>0.
\]
For a strictly decreasing strictly convex function on this interval,
\[
 g(u)+g(v)\le g(\ell)+g(2w-\ell),
\]
with equality precisely at the two endpoints in the displayed order.
Indeed, if \(v\ge2w-\ell\), monotonicity gives the result, strictly
unless both coordinates are endpoints. Otherwise both coordinates
lie between the endpoints. Apply their strict chord inequalities and
add: the constraint \(u+v\ge2w\), together with
\(g(\ell)>g(2w-\ell)\), bounds the resulting chord sum by the endpoint
sum. This also gives strictness in the interior. These statements,
including the equality case, are formalized for general strictly
convex decreasing functions in \texttt{ReciprocalRadiusConvexity.lean}.
Finally,
\[
 g(u)+g(v)=\frac1r+\frac1R
 \le 2+\frac1{R_*},\qquad
 c(r,R)=\frac1{2(1/r+1/R)}.
\]
The positive-denominator comparison proves
\eqref{eq:sharp-finite-coefficient}. The exact endpoint identities and
unique optimizer are formalized in \texttt{PairedResolventOptimizer.lean}.
Optimality here concerns this two-line compensation class; it is not
an optimality claim among all zero observables or multiplicity bounds.

\paragraph{Euler-admissible approximation and the actual zero bound.}
The endpoint \(r=1/2\) itself is outside the independent Euler estimate.
For each \(\varepsilon>0\), set
\[
 d=\min\left\{\frac{R_*-1/2}{2},\varepsilon\right\},\qquad
 r=\frac12+d,\qquad R=R_*.
\]
Then \(1/2<r<R\). Increasing the inner radius preserves
\eqref{eq:sharp-finite-strip}, and the exact rational comparison gives
\[
 c(r,R_*)\le c(1/2,R_*)+\frac{r-1/2}{2}<c_*+\varepsilon.
\]
This entire approximation, with its quantified positive epsilon,
is formalized; it is not a finite numerical test.

For these fixed radii the canonical xi zero enumeration and
\eqref{eq:sharp-finite-strip} give the finite mass inequality
\[
 \sum_{j\in S}K_{r,R}(\beta_j-1/2,T-\gamma_j)
 \le A_r(T)-\frac rR A_R(T)
\]
for every finite subset \(S\) of the enumeration, which includes all
multiplicity copies. The same-height minimum charges the full fiber
of a zero \(\rho\) by \(m(\rho)(1/r-r/R^2)\). Retaining the exact signed
compensation and the independent bounds on \(A_r\) and \(A_r-A_R\)
therefore proves, for \(|\gamma|\ge2\),
\[
 m(\rho)(r+R)
 \le rR\left(\frac12\log|\gamma|+C_0+rL\right).
\]
Consequently the maintained theorem
\texttt{zeta\_multiplicity\_seven\_eighths\_sharp\_log\_bound} proves
\begin{equation}\label{eq:sharp-global-multiplicity}
 \forall\varepsilon>0\ \exists C_\varepsilon>0\ \forall\rho:
 \quad 0<\Re\rho<1,\ |\Im\rho|\ge2
 \ \Longrightarrow\quad
 m(\rho)\le(c_*+\varepsilon)\log|\Im\rho|+C_\varepsilon.
\end{equation}
The radii are chosen after epsilon and before the zero height. The
constant may diverge as epsilon decreases: no assertion of
\(c_*\log|\gamma|+O(1)\) is made. The closed-form coefficient is about
\(0.132354\), compared with \(3\sqrt2/32\approx0.132583\) above.
The weighted mass and fixed-radius analytic bound are formalized in
\texttt{Helpers/XiSharpMultiplicity.lean}; the actual installed \(7/8\)
specialization is in \texttt{Assembly/CarmichaelSharpMultiplicity.lean}.
The unconditional zero-free boundary remains \(7/8\); this theorem
bounds multiplicity mass, and does not prove simplicity or RH.
Novelty priority for the coefficient remains unestablished.

\subsection{A three-line kernel strictly improves the multiplicity coefficient}
\label{sec:three-line-multiplicity}

The sharp constant \(c_*\) above belongs to the compensated two-line
class \(K_{r,R}\). It is not a lower bound for general signed finite
combinations of reflected resolvents. We now give an explicit strict
improvement using three lines.

\paragraph{Theorem.}
Put
\begin{align*}
 (a_1,a_2,a_3)&=\left(\frac{5001}{10000},\frac7{10},\frac65\right),\\
 (c_1,c_2,c_3)&=\left(\frac{1209493}{1000000},
             -\frac{1059063}{1000000},\frac{56907}{500000}\right),\\
 g&=\sum_{j=1}^3\frac{c_j}{a_j}
       =\frac{7004201657}{7001400000},\qquad S=\sum_{j=1}^3c_j,\\
 c_3^{\rm new}&=\frac{S}{2g}
       =\frac{4625194854}{35021008285}
       =0.1320691516\ldots.
\end{align*}
There is a fixed explicitly defined \(C_3\ge0\) such that every
\(w\) with \(0<\Re w<1\), \(T=|\Im w|\ge2\), satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:three-line-global-multiplicity}
 \operatorname{ord}_w\zeta
       \le c_3^{\rm new}\log T+C_3
       \le\frac{1321}{10000}\log T+C_3.
\end{equation}
The exact strict comparison is
\[
 c_3^{\rm new}<\frac{1321}{10000}<c_*.
\]
This uses the installed unconditional \(7/8\) strip, rather than RH.

\paragraph{The full signed kernel.}
For real \(u,v\), write
\[
 \mathcal R_a(u,v)=\frac12\left(
 \frac{a-u}{(a-u)^2+v^2}+\frac{a+u}{(a+u)^2+v^2}\right),\qquad
 K_3(u,v)=\sum_{j=1}^3 c_j\mathcal R_{a_j}(u,v).
\]
The two geometric statements proved below are
\begin{align}
 K_3(u,v)&>0&&(|u|\le3/8,\ v\in\mathbb R),\label{eq:three-line-positive}\\
 K_3(u,0)&\ge g&&(|u|\le1/2).\label{eq:three-line-charge}
\end{align}
All imaginary displacements and all cross terms are retained. The first
horizontal moment is \(\sum_{j=1}^3c_ja_j=1001493/10^{10}>0\).
The single-line trace identities are absolutely convergent, so this
retained tail is included in the assembly.

\paragraph{Exact polynomial proof of positivity.}
Let \(W=9/64\), and define the explicit rational polynomials
\begin{align*}
 d_j(x,y)&=(a_j^2+y-x)^2+4xy,\\
 N(x,y)&=\sum_{i=1}^3 c_i a_i(a_i^2+y-x)
           \prod_{\substack{1\le j\le3\\j\ne i}}d_j(x,y),\\
 Q(q,y)&=N(W-q,y)=\sum_{i=0}^5\sum_{j=0}^5Q_{ij}q^iy^j.
\end{align*}
Coefficients outside the degree-five support are zero. These definitions
specify every coefficient by rational arithmetic from the displayed
radii and weights.
Set
\[
 \alpha=-Q_{14}>0,\qquad \eta=-Q_{22}>0,\qquad\delta=-Q_{41}>0,
\]
and let \(B(y)=\sum_{j=0}^5b_jy^j\), where \(b_j=Q_{0j}\) for
\(j\ne4\), and \(b_4=Q_{04}-\alpha W\).
Exact expansion gives the identity
\begin{align*}
 Q(q,y)=B(y)
 &+q\{Q_{10}+(Q_{11}-\delta W^3)y
                   +(Q_{12}-\eta W)y^2+Q_{13}y^3\}\\
 &+\delta q(W-q)(W^2+Wq+q^2)y+\eta q(W-q)y^2\\
 &+q^2(Q_{20}+Q_{21}y+Q_{23}y^3)
       +q^3(Q_{30}+Q_{31}y+Q_{32}y^2)\\
 &+Q_{40}q^4+Q_{50}q^5+\alpha(W-q)y^4.
\end{align*}
Every coefficient in the braces and in the remaining correction terms
is strictly positive. For \(0\le q\le W\), \(y\ge0\), these corrections
are therefore nonnegative.

It remains to check \(B(y)>0\). Here \(b_2,b_3,b_4,b_5>0\).
Put \(p_0=B(1/4)\), \(p_1=B'(1/4)\), and
\[
 R(y)=b_3(y+1/2)+b_4(y^2+y/2+3/16)
             +b_5(y^3+y^2/2+3y/16+1/16).
\]
Then \(R(y)\ge0\) for \(y\ge0\), and the exact identity
\[
 4b_2B(y)=\{2b_2(y-1/4)+p_1\}^2
      +(4b_2p_0-p_1^2)+4b_2(y-1/4)^2R(y)
\]
proves strict positivity. The unscaled rational remainder is
\[
 p_0-\frac{p_1^2}{4b_2}
 =\frac{42582294214966263093549136221118761182085742929055283}
 {952122753923491722551436902400000000000000000000000000000}>0.
\]
This is the same certificate after division by the positive integer
scale used in Lean. Its polynomial identity, coefficient signs and
strict discriminant are checked exactly, without a frequency grid.

For \(x=u^2\), \(y=v^2\), the denominator product is positive because
\(|u|\le3/8<a_j\), and
\[
 K_3(u,v)\prod_{j=1}^3 d_j(u^2,v^2)=N(u^2,v^2).
\]
Taking \(q=W-u^2\) now proves \eqref{eq:three-line-positive}.

\paragraph{The full zero-fiber charge.}
The same-height statement has the larger domain \(|u|\le1/2\).
To see this, put
\[
 s=\sum_{j=1}^3a_j^2,\quad
 p=\sum_{1\le i<j\le3}a_i^2a_j^2,\quad
 \tau=\sum_{j=1}^3c_ja_j,\quad
 \nu=\sum_{j=1}^3c_ja_j^3,
\]
\[
 A_0=\nu-s\tau+gp,\quad A_1=\tau-gs,\quad
 R_0=A_0+A_1/4+g/16>0,\quad R_1=-A_1-g/2>0.
\]
Direct rational algebra gives
\[
 \{K_3(u,0)-g\}\prod_{j=1}^3(a_j^2-u^2)
   =u^2\{R_0+R_1(1/4-u^2)+g(1/4-u^2)^2\}\ge0.
\]
Every denominator factor is positive, proving
\eqref{eq:three-line-charge}. This argument applies to every point
in the open critical strip, including points of analytic order zero.

\paragraph{The complete analytic consumer and its constant.}
Use the same \(A_a(t)\) as in the preceding trace theorem. Absolute
summability and the exact single-line identity give
\[
 B_3(T):=\sum_{j=1}^3c_jA_{a_j}(T)
 =\sum_{p\in\mathcal Z_\xi}
 K_3(\Re\rho_p-1/2,T-\Im\rho_p),
\]
where \(\mathcal Z_\xi\) is the canonical zero enumeration with every
multiplicity copy included. The installed strip puts every summand in
\eqref{eq:three-line-positive}. Applying the finite-subset inequality
to the entire fiber of \(w\), then using
\eqref{eq:three-line-charge}, yields
\[
                  \operatorname{ord}_w\zeta\,g\le B_3(\Im w).
\]

For completeness the additive constant is fixed as follows. Let
\(\sigma=1/2+a_1=10001/10000\),
\(E(a)=|\zeta'(a)/\zeta(a)|\) for real \(a>1\), and
\begin{align*}
 H&=\frac1{\sigma-1}+E(\sigma)+\frac{|\log\pi|}{2}
       +\frac{\sigma+8+|\log3|+|\gamma_E|}{2},\\
 L&=\frac{2E(1+(\sigma-1)/2)}{(\sigma-1)/2}
       +\frac1{(\sigma-1)^2}
       +\frac14\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac1{(n+1)^2},\\
 \mathcal R&=|c_2||a_2-a_1|+|c_3||a_3-a_1|,
       \qquad C_3=\frac{SH+L\mathcal R}{g}\ge0.
\end{align*}
Here \(\gamma_E\) is Euler's constant. These finite constants are the
maintained \texttt{xiExplicitHeight} and \texttt{xiExplicitLipschitz}
values. The independent Euler and gamma estimates already proved give
\[
 A_{a_1}(T)\le\tfrac12\log|T|+H\quad(|T|\ge2),\qquad
 |A_a(T)-A_{a_1}(T)|\le L|a-a_1|\quad(a\ge a_1).
\]
Retain the signed main term before estimating the differences:
\begin{align*}
 B_3(T)&=S A_{a_1}(T)
       +c_2\{A_{a_2}(T)-A_{a_1}(T)\}
       +c_3\{A_{a_3}(T)-A_{a_1}(T)\}\\
 &\le \frac S2\log|T|+SH+L\mathcal R.
\end{align*}
In particular the negative weight \(c_2\) remains in \(S\).
Dividing by \(g>0\) proves
\eqref{eq:three-line-global-multiplicity}. The strict comparison with
\(c_*\) is also checked algebraically; for example
\(\sqrt7<53/20\) gives the required rational lower bound on
\(\sqrt{13-3\sqrt7}\).

The maintained proofs are in
\texttt{Helpers/ThreeLineResolventCertificate.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/XiThreeLineMultiplicity.lean}, and
\texttt{Assembly/CarmichaelThreeLineMultiplicity.lean}.
This is a strict improvement over the completed two-line leading
coefficient. The additive constant has not been optimized for numerical
simplicity, and the effective window in the next subsection is unchanged.
The certified zero-free boundary remains \(7/8\). Priority beyond this
explicit comparison has not been established.

\subsection{A joint multiplicity budget and horizontal rigidity}
\label{sec:horizontal-multiplicity}

The same signed three-line kernel controls more than an individual
multiplicity.  It charges horizontal displacement from the critical line
by a definite positive amount.  Retain the exact radii, signed weights,
charge \(g\), coefficient \(c_3\), and constant \(C_3\) from the preceding
subsection, and put
\[
 \kappa=\frac{13}{2},\qquad \lambda=\frac{\kappa}{g},\qquad
 B(T)=c_3\log|T|+C_3.
\]
For a finite set \(W\) of distinct complex points in the open critical
strip, all with imaginary part \(T\), define
\[
 N_W=\sum_{w\in W}m(w),\qquad
 M_W=\sum_{w\in W}m(w)(\Re w-\tfrac12)^2,
 \qquad m(w)=\operatorname{ord}_w\zeta.
\]
A nonzero value of \(\zeta\) has order zero, so it is unnecessary to
assume separately that every point in \(W\) is a zero.

\paragraph{Theorem.}
Unconditionally, for every such finite \(W\) and every \(|T|\ge2\),
\begin{equation}
 N_W+\lambda M_W\le B(T).
 \label{eq:horizontal-multiplicity-budget}
\end{equation}
In particular, every critical-strip point \(w\) with \(|\Im w|\ge2\)
satisfies
\begin{equation}
 m(w)\bigl(1+\lambda(\Re w-\tfrac12)^2\bigr)
 \le B(\Im w).
 \label{eq:horizontal-multiplicity-profile}
\end{equation}
If \(N_W\ge(1-\eta)B(T)\), then
\begin{equation}
 \lambda M_W\le\eta B(T).
 \label{eq:horizontal-multiplicity-rigidity}
\end{equation}
Thus a collection whose multiplicity mass is near the available ceiling
must have a small multiplicity-weighted horizontal second moment.
The statement concerns the explicitly specified finite support \(W\);
no unqualified sum over all complex numbers is used.

\paragraph{Exact quadratic charge.}
Write \(s=\sum_j a_j^2\), \(p=\sum_{i<j}a_i^2a_j^2\),
\(d=\prod_j a_j^2\), and \(\tau=\sum_jc_ja_j\), and set
\[
 D(x)=\prod_{j=1}^3(a_j^2-x),\qquad
 G_0=d\sum_{j=1}^3\frac{c_j}{a_j^3},\qquad G_1=\tau-gs.
\]
The exact rational identity
\[
 (K_3(u,0)-g)D(u^2)=u^2(G_0+G_1u^2+gu^4)
\]
follows by putting the three resolvents over their common denominator.
Define the rational constants
\[
 A=G_0-\kappa d,\qquad C=\kappa s-g,\qquad
 B_0=G_1+\kappa p-\frac{C}{4}.
\]
For the stated radii and weights, exact rational arithmetic proves
\(A>0\), \(B_0>0\), and \(C>0\).  The complete retained difference is
\begin{align*}
 &(K_3(u,0)-g-\kappa u^2)D(u^2)\\
 &\quad=u^2\left(A+B_0u^2+C u^2(\tfrac14-u^2)+\kappa u^6\right).
\end{align*}
If \(|u|\le1/2\), every term on the right is nonnegative and every
factor of \(D(u^2)\) is positive, including at \(|u|=1/2\), since
\(a_1=5001/10000>1/2\).  Consequently
\begin{equation}
 K_3(u,0)\ge g+\frac{13}{2}u^2\qquad(|u|\le1/2).
 \label{eq:horizontal-kernel-charge}
\end{equation}
This proof keeps the signed second weight inside the full rational
identity.  It does not estimate the three main resolvents separately.

\paragraph{Full multiplicity-fiber proof.}
For each \(w\in W\), the canonical xi divisor index has a finite fiber
of exactly \(m(w)\) elements, by the proved xi--zeta order identity.
The fibers of distinct points are disjoint.  Let \(S_W\) be their finite
union.  Every index in \(S_W\) has ordinate \(T\), and hence
\eqref{eq:horizontal-kernel-charge} gives
\[
 gN_W+\kappa M_W
 \le\sum_{q\in S_W}
 K_3(\Re\rho_q-\tfrac12,T-\Im\rho_q).
\]
The actual unconditional canonical strip is
\(|\Re\rho_q-1/2|\le3/8\).  The previously proved joint kernel
positivity on this strip and absolute summability allow the finite
sum to be bounded by the complete signed trace \(B_3(T)\).  The full
signed analytic upper bound from the preceding subsection is
\[
 B_3(T)\le g\bigl(c_3\log|T|+C_3\bigr)=gB(T).
\]
Combining these inequalities and dividing by \(g>0\) proves
\eqref{eq:horizontal-multiplicity-budget}.  Taking \(W=\{w\}\) proves
\eqref{eq:horizontal-multiplicity-profile}.  Subtracting the assumed
lower bound for \(N_W\) proves
\eqref{eq:horizontal-multiplicity-rigidity}.  This charges every
multiplicity copy and retains the same analytic constant \(C_3\).

\paragraph{Comparison and formal status.}
The old mass-only consequence was \(N_W\le B(T)\).  The new bound adds
the nonnegative term \(\lambda M_W\), with the fixed explicit
\(\lambda=13/(2g)>0\).  A point at horizontal distance at least
\(a\ge0\) therefore pays a multiplicative factor at least
\(1+\lambda a^2\) in the individual bound.
The global logarithmic coefficient remains
\(c_3=4625194854/35021008285\); the numerical simplicity window and
the zero-free boundary are unchanged.
All twelve new declarations, including the uniform polynomial charge,
disjoint multiplicity fibers, finite-set budget, individual profile,
and rigidity theorem, have real Lean proofs.  No finite zero samples
or numerical completion of an unresolved term enter the argument.
No priority over the external multiplicity literature is asserted.

\subsection{Sharp nonlinear horizontal geometry of a zero cluster}
\label{sec:nonlinear-horizontal-geometry}

Retain the preceding exact radii \(a_j\), signed coefficients \(c_j\),
charge \(g\), and complete budget \(B(T)=c_3\log|T|+C_3\).
For \(0\le x\le9/64\), define
\[
 \Phi(x)=\sum_{j=1}^3\frac{c_ja_j}{a_j^2-x},\qquad
 L(z)=\sum_{j=1}^3\frac{c_ja_j}{(a_j^2-z)^2}.
\]
The exact same-height identity is \(K_3(u,0)=\Phi(u^2)\) for
\(|u|\le3/8\), and \(\Phi(0)=g\).  Put
\begin{align*}
 \ell=L(0)&=\frac{9127021347318871247}{1372823364650976000}
             =6.648358108\ldots,\\
 \sigma=\sum_{j=1}^3\frac{c_j}{a_j^5}
   &=\frac{8724025982765199771011463287}{269181019586315111382051840}
             =32.409513851\ldots.
\end{align*}
In particular, \(\ell>13/2\) and \(\sigma>0\).

For a finite same-ordinate set \(W\) as above, suppose its total
multiplicity \(N_W\) is positive.  Write
\[
 x_w=(\Re w-\tfrac12)^2,\qquad
 \mu_W=\frac{M_W}{N_W},\qquad
 M_{4,W}=\sum_{w\in W}m(w)x_w^2,\qquad
 V_W=\sum_{w\in W}m(w)(x_w-\mu_W)^2.
\]
Points of order zero make no contribution.

\paragraph{Theorem (nonlinear joint geometry).}
Unconditionally, for every such finite \(W\) at every \(|T|\ge2\),
\(0\le\mu_W\le9/64\) and
\begin{equation}
 N_W\Phi(\mu_W)+\sigma V_W\le gB(T).
 \label{eq:nonlinear-horizontal-budget}
\end{equation}
Consequently,
\begin{equation}
 gN_W+\ell M_W+\sigma M_{4,W}\le gB(T).
 \label{eq:quartic-horizontal-budget}
\end{equation}
The left side of \eqref{eq:quartic-horizontal-budget} exceeds the
previous \(gN_W+(13/2)M_W\) by exactly
\[
 (\ell-13/2)M_W+\sigma M_{4,W}\ge0,
\]
and the improvement is strict whenever \(M_W>0\).
Every term in the old analytic upper bound, including \(C_3\), is retained.

\paragraph{Proof of the signed tangent estimate.}
All denominators below are positive on the closed square
\(0\le x,z\le w_0:=9/64\).  The exact identity
\begin{equation}
 \Phi(x)-\Phi(z)-L(z)(x-z)=H(x,z)(x-z)^2,
 \qquad
 H(x,z)=\sum_{j=1}^3
 \frac{c_ja_j}{(a_j^2-x)(a_j^2-z)^2}
 \label{eq:nonlinear-horizontal-tangent}
\end{equation}
follows by clearing each summand's denominators.  The negative second
coefficient remains inside \(H\).
We prove the uniform, sharp floor
\begin{equation}
 H(x,z)\ge\sigma\quad(0\le x,z\le9/64).
 \label{eq:nonlinear-horizontal-remainder-floor}
\end{equation}
Here sharp means that \(\sigma\) is the largest constant lower bound
for \(H\) on this square: \(H(0,0)=\sigma\).

The following exact certificate proves the floor without a grid test.
Set
\[
 B_j(x,z)=(a_j^2-x)(a_j^2-z)^2,\qquad
 D(x,z)=\prod_{j=1}^3B_j(x,z),\qquad
 U(x,z)=\sum_{j=1}^3c_ja_j\!\prod_{k\ne j}B_k(x,z).
\]
Then \(HD=U\).  Write
\[
 P(X,Z)=U(w_0X,w_0Z)-\sigma D(w_0X,w_0Z)
       =\sum_{k=0}^{3}\sum_{l=0}^{6}p_{kl}X^kZ^l.
\]
For \(0\le i\le3\), \(0\le j\le6\), define the exact rational numbers
\[
 \beta_{ij}=\sum_{k=0}^{i}\sum_{l=0}^{j}p_{kl}
       \frac{\binom{i}{k}}{\binom{3}{k}}
       \frac{\binom{j}{l}}{\binom{6}{l}}.
\]
With the positive integer
\[
 \mathcal S=743392281267463253945801192243200000000000000000000000000000000000,
\]
the integers \(A_{ij}=\mathcal S\beta_{ij}\binom{3}{i}\binom{6}{j}\)
satisfy \(A_{00}=0\); the remaining twenty-seven are positive.
For completeness, their row minima are
\[
 \begin{array}{c|c|r}
 i&\text{indices included}&\min A_{ij}\\\hline
0 & 1\le j\le6 & 50887375665861953576851511269639925474434771031682231601158402048 \\
1 & 0\le j\le6 & 81448516441444037642357369142454676442889767037974549895550337024 \\
2 & 0\le j\le6 & 126567896707499144913908861729990991379108675655992874202228681267 \\
3 & 0\le j\le6 & 37530468824846874813723019000311986185373140352803836208877888247 \\
 \end{array}
\]
The coefficient formula specifies every entry, and exact expansion gives
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal SP(X,Z)=
 \sum_{i=0}^{3}\sum_{j=0}^{6}A_{ij}
 X^i(1-X)^{3-i}Z^j(1-Z)^{6-j}.
 \label{eq:nonlinear-horizontal-bernstein}
\end{equation}
Every term on the right is nonnegative on the unit square.  Since
\(\mathcal S>0\) and \(D>0\), this proves
\eqref{eq:nonlinear-horizontal-remainder-floor}.  The full integer
identity, signs and rational tangent identity are checked in Lean.
It follows that
\begin{equation}
 \Phi(z)+L(z)(x-z)+\sigma(x-z)^2\le\Phi(x).
 \label{eq:nonlinear-horizontal-strong-tangent}
\end{equation}

\paragraph{Joint cancellation and equality configurations.}
For any finite collection of squared horizontal coordinates \(x_r\)
in \([0,9/64]\), with mean \(\mu\), summing
\eqref{eq:nonlinear-horizontal-strong-tangent} gives
\begin{equation}
 n\Phi(\mu)+\sigma\sum_r(x_r-\mu)^2\le\sum_r\Phi(x_r),
 \label{eq:nonlinear-horizontal-centered}
\end{equation}
because the entire linear term is exactly
\(L(\mu)\sum_r(x_r-\mu)=0\).  No separate absolute estimate is applied
to these deviations.  Moreover,
\[
 \sum_r\Phi(x_r)=n\Phi(\mu)
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 x_r=\mu\ \text{for every index }r.
\]
Indeed, \(\sigma>0\) forces every square to vanish in the forward
direction, and the reverse direction is immediate.
Thus \(n\Phi(\mu)\) is the exact minimum profile cost at prescribed
mass and mean.  This sharpness statement concerns the kernel cost;
it makes no attainment assertion about the analytic trace ceiling.

Apply this finite identity to the canonical multiplicity copies of the
points of \(W\).  The existing weighted-fiber transport identifies
\(n=N_W\), the mean with \(\mu_W\), and the squared deviation sum
with \(V_W\).  The proved seven-eighths strip puts every canonical
squared horizontal coordinate in \([0,9/64]\), which also proves the
stated bound on \(\mu_W\).  The complete trace inequality is
\[
 \sum_r K_3(\Re\rho_r-\tfrac12,0)
 \le\mathcal T_3(T)\le gB(T).
\]
Combining it with \eqref{eq:nonlinear-horizontal-centered} proves
\eqref{eq:nonlinear-horizontal-budget} with all multiplicity copies
and compensation terms retained.

Finally, put \(z=0\) in \eqref{eq:nonlinear-horizontal-strong-tangent}:
\(\Phi(x)\ge g+\ell x+\sigma x^2\).  The exact variance balance is
\[
 V_W=M_{4,W}-2\mu_WM_W+\mu_W^2N_W
     =M_{4,W}-N_W\mu_W^2.
\]
Substituting these two identities into
\eqref{eq:nonlinear-horizontal-budget} proves
\eqref{eq:quartic-horizontal-budget} and the displayed comparison.
The global pointwise logarithmic coefficient, numerical simplicity
window and zero-free boundary are unchanged.
For comparison with bounds obtained from zeta values and short
intervals, see
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1706.08268}{Ivi\'c's multiplicity analysis}.
The present bounds constrain the joint horizontal geometry of a finite
same-height cluster.

\subsection{Parity strengthens the full nonlinear horizontal budget}
\label{sec:parity-horizontal-geometry}

Retain \(\Phi,g,\sigma,B(T),N_W,M_W,M_{4,W},V_W\) from the
preceding subsection, and put
\[
 \Psi(x)=\Phi(x)-\sigma x^2,\qquad 0\le x\le 9/64.
\]
The reflection used here preserves the ordinate:
\(r(w)=1-\overline w\).  Reflection closure of \(W\), rather than an
arbitrary selection of zeros at height \(T\), is essential.
The elementary parity principle is classical; the refinement here is its
explicit positive cost in the signed three-line nonlinear budget.

\paragraph{Theorem (parity-refined nonlinear cluster budget).}
\label{thm:parity-horizontal-budget}
Let \(|T|\ge2\), and let \(W\) be a finite subset of the open critical
strip with every point of ordinate \(T\), closed under \(r\).
Give each point its actual zeta order \(m(w)\); points of order zero
contribute nothing.  Suppose that \(N=N_W=\sum_{w\in W}m(w)\) is odd.
If \(N=1\), then \(M_W=M_{4,W}=0\).  If \(N>1\), put \(M=M_W\).
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:odd-horizontal-budget}
 0\le M\le\frac9{64}(N-1),\qquad
 \sigma M_{4,W}+g+(N-1)\Psi\!\left(\frac M{N-1}\right)
 \le gB(T).
\end{equation}
Equivalently, with \(\mu=M/N\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:full-horizontal-parity-cost}
 N\Phi(\mu)+\sigma V_W+\Pi_N(M)\le gB(T),
\quad
 \Pi_N(M)=g+(N-1)\Psi\!\left(\frac M{N-1}\right)-N\Psi(\mu).
\end{equation}
On \(N>1\), \(0\le M\le(9/64)(N-1)\), the correction satisfies
\(\Pi_N(M)\ge0\), with strict inequality if \(M>0\), and
\(\Pi_N(0)=0\).  Thus the improvement retains the entire earlier
quartic variance charge; it does not trade that charge for parity.
\paragraph{Proof.}
First, reflection preserves the actual order without any RH hypothesis.
The identity \(\zeta(\overline w)=\overline{\zeta(w)}\) gives equality
of the orders at \(w\) and \(\overline w\): conjugation commutes with
successive derivatives in this identity, so the first nonzero Taylor
coefficient has the same index.  Analyticity and finite order hold in the
open strip.  The completed-xi functional equation identifies orders at
\(\overline w\) and \(1-\overline w\); its nonvanishing local factors
identify these xi orders with zeta orders.  Therefore
\(m(r(w))=m(w)\).

Consider the finite signed product
\[
 P_W=\prod_{w\in W}(\Re w-\tfrac12)^{m(w)}.
\]
Reindexing by the involution \(r\), including all multiplicities, gives
\(P_W=(-1)^NP_W\).  Oddness forces \(P_W=0\), hence a central point
has positive order.  A zero exponent cannot account for the vanishing
factor, since \(0^0=1\).  The existing weighted-fiber transport therefore
provides an actual central multiplicity copy \(a\) in the canonical
finite xi index \(S\).  We have \(|S|=N\) and, for
\(x_i=(\Re\rho_i-1/2)^2\), \(x_a=0\),
\[
  \sum_{i\in S}x_i=M,\qquad
  \sum_{i\in S}x_i^2=M_{4,W}.
\]
If \(N=1\), this central copy is the entire multiplicity index, so both
moments vanish.  No quotient with denominator \(N-1\) is used in this case.

For \(N>1\), erase only that one copy and write
\(R=S\setminus\{a\}\), \(|R|=N-1\),
\(z=M/(N-1)\).  The unconditional seven-eighths strip gives
\(0\le x_i\le9/64\).  Consequently \(0\le z\le9/64\) and the stated
upper bound for \(M\).  The exact tangent remainder from the preceding
subsection is
\[
 \Psi(x)-\Psi(z)-(L(z)-2\sigma z)(x-z)
   =(H(x,z)-\sigma)(x-z)^2\ge0.
\]
It is strictly positive when \(x\ne z\): the already proved positive
Bernstein certificate gives \(H(x,z)>\sigma\) if \(x>0\) or \(z>0\);
the only remaining point is \((0,0)\), where \(x=z\).
Summing the tangent inequality over \(R\), its linear terms cancel
because \(\sum_{i\in R}x_i=(N-1)z\).  Restoring the erased central copy
and using \(\Psi(0)=g\) gives
\[
 g+(N-1)\Psi(z)\le\sum_{i\in S}\Psi(x_i).
\]
Add \(\sigma\sum_{i\in S}x_i^2\) to obtain the left side of
\eqref{eq:odd-horizontal-budget}, bounded by \(\sum_{i\in S}\Phi(x_i)\).
The exact common-height kernel identity and the complete signed trace
bound give
\[
 \sum_{i\in S}\Phi(x_i)
 \le\mathcal T_3(T)\le gB(T).
\]
All copies and the original additive constant are retained.

The previous variance identity gives
\[
 N\Phi(\mu)+\sigma V_W=\sigma M_{4,W}+N\Psi(\mu),
\]
which proves \eqref{eq:full-horizontal-parity-cost}.
For the correction, apply the residual tangent inequality at \(\mu\)
to the two coordinates \(0,z\), with weights \(1,N-1\).
The linear terms cancel since \((N-1)z=N\mu=M\), proving
\(\Pi_N(M)\ge0\).  If \(M>0\), then \(\mu>0\), so the tangent
inequality at coordinate zero is strict and \(\Pi_N(M)>0\).
At \(M=0\) the displayed formula and \(\Psi(0)=g\) give zero directly.
Every denominator has been restricted to its positive domain.
\paragraph{Sharp finite-spectrum geometry.}
These statements concern abstract reflected finite multisets of
coordinates \(u_i\in[-3/8,3/8]\), with natural multiplicities.
For fixed total mass \(N\) and \(M=\sum_i u_i^2\), the minimum of
\(\sum_i\Phi(u_i^2)\) is
\[
 \begin{cases}
  N\Phi(M/N),&N=2k,\ k\ge1,\ 0\le M\le(9/64)N,\\
  g+(N-1)\Phi(M/(N-1)),&N=2k+1,\ k\ge1,
       \ 0\le M\le(9/64)(N-1).
 \end{cases}
\]
For even \(N\), strict Jensen equality requires all squared coordinates
to equal \(M/N\).  At \(M>0\) reflection then forces precisely \(k\)
copies at each of \(\pm\sqrt{M/N}\), which attains the minimum.
For odd \(N>1\), erase a central copy as above.  Strict Jensen equality
requires every remaining squared coordinate to equal \(M/(N-1)\).
At \(M>0\), this is exactly one central copy and \(k\) copies at each
of \(\pm\sqrt{M/(N-1)}\), again attaining the stated minimum.
The compiled odd and even model identities also verify that the full
quartic charges cancel exactly in these optimizing configurations.
If \(M=0\), every positive-multiplicity copy is central; the named
support points coalesce, rather than giving distinct zeros.
Mass one has only this central configuration.  The optimizer and equality
statements follow from the compiled finite Jensen equivalence and the
compiled central-copy optimizer theorem.  No attainment by actual zeta
zeros of the analytic trace ceiling \(gB(T)\) is claimed.
The logarithmic coefficient, effective simplicity window and global
seven-eighths boundary are unchanged.

\subsection{A sharp cubic law for the parity correction}
\label{sec:cubic-parity-geometry}

The preceding theorem proves that the parity correction is positive.
Its first surviving scale, after the entire quartic charge has been
removed, is cubic in the second horizontal moment.  This scale has a
sharp explicit coefficient for the fixed signed three-line profile.
Define
\[
 \tau=\sum_{j=1}^3\frac{c_j}{a_j^7}
 =\frac{37413639865876325411850661752657392635}
 {263902928706162612461624933697712128}
 =141.77046101498\ldots,
 \qquad A_N=\frac{2N-1}{N^2(N-1)^2}.
\]

\paragraph{Theorem (sharp cubic parity cost).}
For every real \(N>1\) and \(0\le M\le(9/64)(N-1)\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:sharp-cubic-parity-cost}
 \Pi_N(M)\ge\tau A_NM^3,
\end{equation}
with strict inequality when \(M>0\) and equality at \(M=0\).
For each fixed \(N>1\), the coefficient \(\tau\) is optimal:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cubic-parity-optimality}
 \bigl[\,\forall M\in[0,(9/64)(N-1)],\quad
        \Pi_N(M)\ge cA_NM^3\,\bigr]
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad c\le\tau.
\end{equation}
This is an exact statement about the existing fixed parity functional;
it does not optimize over other filters or assert that actual zeros
attain every admissible moment.

\paragraph{Proof.}
Retain every signed pole and introduce the rational tail
\[
 R(x)=\sum_{j=1}^3\frac{c_j}{a_j^5(a_j^2-x)}.
\]
The scalar identity, valid for \(a\ne0\), \(a^2\ne x\), is
\[
 \frac{a}{a^2-x}=\frac1a+\frac{x}{a^3}
       +\frac{x^2}{a^5}+\frac{x^3}{a^5(a^2-x)}.
\]
Summing it with the actual signed weights yields exactly
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cubic-profile-tail}
 \Phi(x)=g+\ell x+\sigma x^2+x^3R(x),\qquad
 \Psi(x)=g+\ell x+x^3R(x),\qquad R(0)=\tau.
\end{equation}
All denominators are positive on \(x\le9/64\).
For \(x,y\le9/64\), the exact divided difference is
\[
 R(y)-R(x)=(y-x)D(x,y),\qquad
 D(x,y)=\sum_{j=1}^3\frac{c_j}
 {a_j^5(a_j^2-x)(a_j^2-y)}.
\]
We prove \(D(x,y)>0\) jointly, including its negative second term.
Put \(d_j=a_j^5(a_j^2-x)(a_j^2-y)>0\).
Since \(0<a_1<a_2\), we have \(d_1\le d_2\).
The actual coefficients satisfy \(c_1>0\), \(c_3>0\) and
\(c_1+c_2=0.150430>0\).  Consequently
\[
 c_1d_2+c_2d_1=(c_1+c_2)d_1+c_1(d_2-d_1)>0,
\]
so \(c_1/d_1+c_2/d_2>0\); the third term is positive as well.
Thus \(R\) is strictly increasing on the full interval in question,
and \(R(x)\ge\tau\) on \([0,9/64]\).
No pole contribution has been discarded or bounded separately by its
absolute value.

Write \(q=N-1>0\), \(x=M/N\), \(z=M/q\).
Then \(0\le x\le z\le9/64\).  Substitution of
\eqref{eq:cubic-profile-tail} into the complete parity cost cancels
its constant and linear terms and gives
\begin{align}
 \Pi_N(M)&=A_NM^3\,\Gamma_N(M),\label{eq:cubic-parity-factorization}\\
 \Gamma_N(M)&=\frac{N^2R(z)-q^2R(x)}{2N-1}
 =R(x)+\frac{N^2}{2N-1}\bigl(R(z)-R(x)\bigr).
 \label{eq:cubic-parity-normalized}
\end{align}
The last expression is at least \(\tau\), and is greater than
\(\tau\) when \(M>0\), since then \(x>0\).
As \(A_N>0\), this proves \eqref{eq:sharp-cubic-parity-cost}.
For every fixed \(N>1\), the rational expression is continuous at
\(M=0\), with
\[
 \Gamma_N(0)=\tau,\qquad
 \lim_{M\to0}\Gamma_N(M)=\tau.
\]
If \(c>\tau\), continuity supplies a positive admissible \(M\)
with \(\Gamma_N(M)<c\).  Concretely, take half the minimum of the
continuity radius and \((9/64)(N-1)\).  Since \(A_NM^3>0\),
\eqref{eq:cubic-parity-factorization} violates the proposed bound
with coefficient \(c\).  Every \(c\le\tau\) works by
\eqref{eq:sharp-cubic-parity-cost}.  This proves the full quantified
optimality statement, including the endpoint \(M=0\).

\paragraph{Actual zero-cluster consequence.}
Under the hypotheses of the parity-refined cluster theorem, with
odd actual multiplicity \(N>1\), retain the entire earlier charge
and define its trace slack by
\[
 \Delta_W(T)=gB(T)-\bigl(N\Phi(M/N)+\sigma V_W\bigr).
\]
The full signed consumer is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cubic-parity-zeta-budget}
 N\Phi(M/N)+\sigma V_W+\tau A_NM^3\le gB(T),
\end{equation}
and hence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cubic-parity-inverse}
 \tau(2N-1)M^3\le N^2(N-1)^2\Delta_W(T).
\end{equation}
If \(M>0\), then \(\Delta_W(T)>\tau A_NM^3\).
These follow by substituting the scalar bound into
\eqref{eq:full-horizontal-parity-cost}; every compensation and variance
term remains present.  Equation \eqref{eq:cubic-parity-inverse} makes
small trace slack an explicit cubic constraint on the horizontal second
moment.  Central multiple zeros have \(M=0\), so this theorem alone
does not establish their simplicity.  Mass one remains covered by the
separate central-copy theorem, without division by \(N-1\).
The global logarithmic coefficient, certified numerical simplicity
window and seven-eighths boundary are unchanged.

\subsection{A two-moment minimum and an even-multiplicity gap}
\label{sec:moment-ratio-lattice}

The fourth horizontal moment can be used jointly with the second, rather
than only as an additive correction.  The underlying moment-optimization
principle is classical; see, for general moment methods,
\href{https://www.mit.edu/~dbertsim/papers/MomentProblems/Optimal-inequalities-in-probability-theory-A-convex-optimization-approach-SIAM15.pdf}
{Bertsimas and Popescu's convex-optimization framework}.
Here the conclusion is an explicit minimum for the fixed signed
three-line kernel, together with a quantitative restriction coming from
actual integer multiplicities and same-height reflection.

Define the size-biased profile
\[
 F(x)=\sum_{j=1}^3\frac{c_j}{a_j(a_j^2-x)},\qquad
 \Phi(x)=g+xF(x).
\]
The second identity is exact.  All denominators are positive on
\([0,1/4]\), since \(a_1=0.5001>1/2\).
For a finite common-height critical-strip set \(W\), retain the actual
orders \(m(w)\), put \(x_w=(\Re w-1/2)^2\), and write
\[
 N=\sum_{w\in W}m(w),\quad
 M=\sum_{w\in W}m(w)x_w,\quad
 Q=\sum_{w\in W}m(w)x_w^2.
\]
Suppose \(N>0\) and \(M>0\).  Then \(Q>0\), so the ratios
\[
 t=\frac QM,\qquad \kappa=\frac{M^2}{Q}=\frac Mt
\]
are well defined and positive.  The unconditional canonical strip bound
gives \(t\le9/64\); the generic moment inequality below needs only
\(t\le1/4\).  Define
\begin{align}
 \mathcal B_2(N,M,Q)
   &=Ng+MF(t)=Ng+\kappa\bigl(\Phi(t)-g\bigr),
       \label{eq:moment-ratio-base}\\
 \mathcal E_W&=\sum_{w\in W}m(w)x_w(x_w-t)^2,\qquad
 K=\sum_{\substack{w\in W\\1/2\le x_w/t}}m(w).
       \label{eq:moment-ratio-threshold}
\end{align}
The threshold is inclusive, and \(K\) counts total order, not distinct
points.

\paragraph{Theorem (joint moment bound and even-mass gap).}
Let \(|T|\ge2\), suppose every point of \(W\) has ordinate \(T\),
and suppose \(W\) is closed under \(w\mapsto1-\overline w\).  Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:moment-ratio-strong-bound}
 \mathcal B_2(N,M,Q)+\mathcal E_W\le gB(T).
\end{equation}
The integer \(K\) is even, and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:moment-ratio-lattice-cross}
 t^3(\kappa-K)^2\le2N\mathcal E_W
 \le2N\bigl(gB(T)-\mathcal B_2(N,M,Q)\bigr).
\end{equation}
In particular, if \(\kappa\) is an odd integer,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:moment-ratio-odd-gap}
 \mathcal B_2(N,M,Q)+\frac{t^3}{2N}\le gB(T).
\end{equation}
All denominators in these normalized statements are positive.
The coefficient of the rounding penalty is a certified positive value;
we assert no optimality for that coefficient.

For nonnegative real weights, the continuous two-moment minimum is
exactly \(\mathcal B_2\).  Equality in
\(\sum_{w\in W}m(w)\Phi(x_w)\ge\mathcal B_2\) requires every
positive-weight displaced coordinate to equal \(t\); zero coordinates
are allowed.  For actual reflection-closed zero clusters this equality
forces \(\kappa=K\), an even natural number.  These equality assertions
refer to the finite kernel sum, not attainment of the analytic ceiling
\(gB(T)\).

\paragraph{Proof: the signed curvature estimate.}
The exact tangent identity for \(F\) is
\[
 F(x)-F(z)-F'(z)(x-z)=H_1(x,z)(x-z)^2,\qquad
 H_1(x,z)=\sum_{j=1}^3
 \frac{c_j}{a_j(a_j^2-x)(a_j^2-z)^2}.
\]
We prove \(H_1(x,z)>1\) for \(0\le x,z\le1/4\) while retaining
the negative second-pole contribution.  Put
\(d_j=a_j(a_j^2-x)(a_j^2-z)^2\).
Every \(d_j\) is positive, and
\[
 d_1\le d_2\le a_2^7=0.0823543
 <c_1+c_2=0.150430.
\]
Because \(c_1>0\),
\[
 c_1d_2+c_2d_1-d_1d_2
 =(c_1+c_2-d_2)d_1+c_1(d_2-d_1)>0.
\]
Thus \(c_1/d_1+c_2/d_2>1\), and \(c_3/d_3>0\) proves the claim.
Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{eq:moment-ratio-strong-tangent}
 F(x)\ge F(z)+F'(z)(x-z)+(x-z)^2.
\end{equation}
This estimates the complete signed rational expression; it does not
replace its negative pole by an absolute-value bound.

\paragraph{Proof: size bias, equality and the sharp continuous minimum.}
The finite weighted variance identity gives
\[
 NQ-M^2=N\sum_{w\in W}m(w)(x_w-M/N)^2\ge0.
\]
Since \(M>0\) and \(N>0\), this also proves \(Q>0\).
The elementary bound \(x_w^2\le(1/4)x_w\) yields \(t\le1/4\).
For the sharper \(9/64\) endpoint, transport \(M,Q\) to the canonical
multiplicity copies, apply the proved seven-eighths strip to every copy,
and use the same pointwise inequality with \(9/64\).
No strip assertion is required for points of order zero.

The weights \(p_w=m(w)x_w/M\) form a probability law with mean
\(\sum_{w\in W}p_wx_w=t\).
Apply \eqref{eq:moment-ratio-strong-tangent} at \(z=t\), multiply by
\(m(w)x_w\), and sum.  All linear compensation terms cancel exactly:
\(\sum_{w\in W}m(w)x_w(x_w-t)=Q-tM=0\).
Using \(\Phi(x)=g+xF(x)\), we obtain
\[
 \sum_{w\in W}m(w)\Phi(x_w)
 \ge Ng+MF(t)+\mathcal E_W.
\]
The complete finite-profile trace bound is
\(\sum_{w\in W}m(w)\Phi(x_w)\le gB(T)\), proving
\eqref{eq:moment-ratio-strong-bound}.

If the finite kernel sum equals \(\mathcal B_2\), the nonnegative
energy must vanish.  Every term with \(m(w)>0\) and \(x_w>0\)
then has \(x_w=t\).  Conversely, that support condition gives equality
by the exact profile identity.  For arbitrary real moment data with
\(M,Q>0\), \(M^2\le NQ\), and \(Q\le M/4\), take the two atoms
\[
 (x,\text{weight})=(0,N-\kappa),\qquad(t,\kappa).
\]
Their weights are nonnegative, and their mass, first moment and second
moment are respectively \(N,M,Q\), since
\(\kappa t=M\) and \(\kappa t^2=Q\).
Their kernel cost is exactly \(\mathcal B_2\).  This proves sharpness
of the continuous minimum and describes its complete equality support.
Actual integer multiplicities impose the additional restriction below.

\paragraph{Proof: the rounding energy and parity.}
Put \(y_w=x_w/t\), and let \(b(y)=1\) for \(y\ge1/2\), and
\(b(y)=0\) otherwise.  For every \(y\ge0\),
\[
 (y-b(y))^2\le2y(y-1)^2.
\]
For \(y\ge1/2\), the difference is
\((2y-1)(y-1)^2\ge0\); for \(y<1/2\), it is
\(y(1-2y)(2-y)\ge0\).
Write \(\delta_w=y_w-b(y_w)\) and
\(\bar\delta=N^{-1}\sum_{w\in W}m(w)\delta_w\).
The full weighted variance identity retains every cross term:
\[
 N\sum_{w\in W}m(w)\delta_w^2
 -\left(\sum_{w\in W}m(w)\delta_w\right)^2
 =N\sum_{w\in W}m(w)(\delta_w-\bar\delta)^2\ge0.
\]
Now \(\sum_{w\in W}m(w)y_w=M/t=\kappa\),
\(\sum_{w\in W}m(w)b(y_w)=K\), and
\[
 t^3\sum_{w\in W}m(w)y_w(y_w-1)^2=\mathcal E_W.
\]
Combining these identities with the pointwise rounding inequality proves
\(t^3(\kappa-K)^2\le2N\mathcal E_W\).

Same-height reflection preserves both \(x_w\) and actual order.
The threshold subset is therefore reflection closed and contains no
central point, because a central coordinate gives \(y_w=0\).
If its total order \(K\) were odd, the already proved odd-order theorem
would force a positive-order central point in that subset, a contradiction.
Thus \(K\) is even, without assuming RH.  This proves
\eqref{eq:moment-ratio-lattice-cross}.
If \(\kappa\) is an odd integer, it differs from the even integer
\(K\), so \((\kappa-K)^2\ge1\), yielding
\eqref{eq:moment-ratio-odd-gap}.
Finally, under equality in the finite continuous minimum, each
positive-weight \(y_w\) is zero or one.  Hence \(y_w=b(y_w)\)
for every contributing point and \(\kappa=K\).

\paragraph{Comparison with the complete earlier charges.}
This joint estimate retains the preceding nonlinear, quartic and parity
information.  Let \(L>0\), \(r=M/L\), and suppose
\(M^2\le LQ\), \(t=Q/M\le9/64\).
Then \(0<r\le t\le9/64\).  The cubic tail from the previous subsection
satisfies \(R(r)\ge\tau>0\) and \(R(t)\ge R(r)\).
Using \(F(t)=\ell+\sigma t+t^2R(t)\) and
\(\Psi(r)=g+\ell r+r^3R(r)\), the exact difference is
\begin{align*}
 \mathcal B_2-&\bigl[\sigma Q+(N-L)g+L\Psi(M/L)\bigr]\\
 &=M\bigl[(t^2-r^2)R(r)+t^2(R(t)-R(r))\bigr]\ge0.
\end{align*}
It is strictly positive if \(M^2<LQ\).
Taking \(L=N\) proves domination of the full earlier charge
\(N\Phi(M/N)+\sigma V_W\).
For odd \(N>1\), remove the compulsory central canonical copy.
Its second and fourth moments are zero, and the weighted variance identity
on the remaining \(N-1\) copies gives \(M^2\le(N-1)Q\).
Taking \(L=N-1\) therefore proves domination of the entire odd-parity
charge \(\sigma Q+g+(N-1)\Psi(M/(N-1))\), with the stated strictness.
Every old signed term is retained in these comparisons.
The case \(M=0\) remains the central configuration and is outside the
ratio definitions used here.  The global logarithmic coefficient,
certified simplicity window and seven-eighths boundary are unchanged.

\subsection{Compensating rounding with the exact second moment}
\label{sec:compensated-moment-lattice}

The preceding squared rounding estimate has a denominator proportional
to the total order \(N\).  An exact cancellation removes that loss.
Put
\[
 \delta=1-\frac{\sqrt2}{2},\qquad
 b_\delta(y)=\begin{cases}1&y\ge\delta,\\0&y<\delta.\end{cases}
\]
Thus \(0<\delta<1/2\), \(\delta>1/4\), and
\(2\delta^2-4\delta+1=0\).  Retain the definitions
\(N,M,Q,t=Q/M,\kappa=M^2/Q,\mathcal B_2\) and
\(\mathcal E=\sum_{\rho\in W}m_\rho x_\rho(x_\rho-t)^2\)
from the preceding subsection.

\paragraph{Theorem (compensated parity charge).}
Let \(W\) be a finite subset of the open critical strip, all of whose
points have imaginary part \(T\), where \(|T|\ge2\).
Suppose \(W\) is closed under \(\rho\mapsto1-\overline\rho\), and
\(N>0\), \(M>0\).  Count every point with its actual analytic order
\(m_\rho\), and define
\[
 K_\delta=\sum_{\substack{\rho\in W\\x_\rho/t\ge\delta}}m_\rho.
\]
Then \(K_\delta\) is an even nonnegative integer and
\begin{equation}
 \delta t^3|\kappa-K_\delta|\le\mathcal E,\qquad
 \mathcal B_2+\delta t^3|\kappa-K_\delta|\le gB(T).
 \label{eq:compensated-moment-charge}
\end{equation}
In particular, if \(\kappa\) is an odd integer,
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal B_2+\delta t^3\le gB(T).
 \label{eq:compensated-moment-odd}
\end{equation}
For \(N\ge2\), this penalty is strictly larger than
\(t^3/(2N)\), the preceding squared rounding penalty.

\paragraph{Proof of the exact compensation.}
The following argument holds for any finite nonnegative weights
\(a_i\) and nonnegative \(y_i\) satisfying
\[
 \sum_i a_i y_i^2=\sum_i a_i y_i.
\]
Write \(S=\sum_i a_i y_i\), \(K=\sum_i a_i b_\delta(y_i)\), and
\(E_0=\sum_i a_i y_i(y_i-1)^2\).  Before estimating anything, cancel
the full second-moment identity:
\begin{equation}
 S-K=\sum_i a_i
 \bigl[y_i-b_\delta(y_i)-y_i(y_i-1)\bigr]
 =\sum_i a_i\bigl[2y_i-y_i^2-b_\delta(y_i)\bigr].
 \label{eq:compensated-round-ledger}
\end{equation}
This identity retains the signed linear error and every quadratic term.
For all \(y\ge0\),
\begin{equation}
 -y(y-1)^2\le
 \delta\bigl[2y-y^2-b_\delta(y)\bigr]\le y(y-1)^2.
 \label{eq:compensated-round-pointwise}
\end{equation}
Indeed, for \(y\ge\delta\) the middle bracket is
\(-(y-1)^2\); the lower bound uses \(y\ge\delta\), and the upper
bound follows from nonnegativity.  For \(0\le y<\delta\) it is
\(y(2-y)\ge0\).  Its upper bound follows from the exact factorization
\[
 (y-1)^2-\delta(2-y)
   =(\delta-y)(2-2\delta-y)\ge0.
\]
Multiplying \eqref{eq:compensated-round-pointwise} by each \(a_i\),
summing the two signed inequalities and only then using
\eqref{eq:compensated-round-ledger} gives
\[
 -E_0\le\delta(S-K)\le E_0,\qquad \delta|S-K|\le E_0.
\]
There is no Cauchy--Schwarz step or factor \(N\).

For the actual cluster, take \(a_i=m_\rho\) and \(y_i=x_\rho/t\).
The definitions give
\[
 \sum_{\rho\in W}m_\rho y_\rho^2
  =\frac Q{t^2}
  =\frac M t
  =\kappa
  =\sum_{\rho\in W}m_\rho y_\rho,
 \qquad t^3E_0=\mathcal E.
\]
Since \(t>0\), the normalized bound proves the first inequality in
\eqref{eq:compensated-moment-charge}.  The threshold set is closed
under reflection.  Its fixed points have \(x_\rho=0\) and are excluded
because \(\delta t>0\).  Reflection preserves the actual analytic
order, so its total order \(K_\delta\) is even.  Equivalently, the
maintained threshold-order theorem applies at ratio \(2\delta t\),
since \(x/(2\delta t)\ge1/2\) is exactly \(x/t\ge\delta\).
The already proved full signed profile bound
\(\mathcal B_2+\mathcal E\le gB(T)\) now gives the second inequality.
Odd \(\kappa\) has distance at least one from the even integer
\(K_\delta\), proving \eqref{eq:compensated-moment-odd}.
Finally \(1/(2N)\le1/4<\delta\) when \(N\ge2\), and \(t^3>0\).

\paragraph{Threshold and constant.}
More generally, replacing \(\delta\) by \(d\) in both the coefficient
and the threshold gives the pointwise certificate whenever
\[
 0\le d\le\tfrac12,\qquad 2d^2-4d+1\ge0.
\]
On this interval the latter condition is equivalent to \(d\le\delta\).
At the fixed threshold \(\delta\), the lower pointwise inequality
evaluated at \(y=\delta\) forces any admissible coefficient to be at
most \(\delta\).  The stronger sharpness statement below concerns
the full moment identity and natural even weights, rather than merely
this pointwise argument.

\paragraph{Theorem (sharpness for even integer-weight moment models).}
For every integer \(n\ge1\), set
\[
 a_n=\frac{2n+1-\sqrt{n(2n+1)}}{2(n+1)},\qquad
 b_n=1+\frac{1/2-a_n}{n}.
\]
Place weight \(2\) at \(a_n\) and weight \(2n\) at \(b_n\).  Then
\[
 \delta\le a_n<\tfrac12,\qquad 1<b_n<\tfrac32,\qquad
 2a_n+2nb_n=2a_n^2+2nb_n^2=2n+1.
\]
The effective mass is the odd integer \(2n+1\), the rounded mass is
the even integer \(2n+2\), and the discrepancy has absolute value one.
Its energy
\[
 E_n=2a_n(a_n-1)^2+2nb_n(b_n-1)^2
\]
satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \delta\le E_n\le\delta+\frac1n.
 \label{eq:compensated-moment-sharp}
\end{equation}
Consequently every \(c>\delta\) fails as a universal lower bound
\(E_0\ge c\) for odd effective mass, even when every weight is a natural
even integer.

\paragraph{Proof of sharpness.}
Squaring the defining radical gives the exact relation
\[
 n(2a_n-a_n^2-\tfrac12)=(\tfrac12-a_n)^2.
\]
The radical lies strictly between \(n\) and \(2n+1\), giving
\(0<a_n<1/2\).  Factoring the quadratic against its root \(\delta\)
then gives \(a_n\ge\delta\) and
\[
 n(a_n-\delta)\le\tfrac14.
\]
The definition of \(b_n\), followed by the displayed quadratic
relation, proves both moment identities.  Both atoms lie above
the threshold, so their rounded mass is \(2+2n\).
The energy expansion is
\[
 E_n=2a_n(a_n-1)^2+
       \frac{2(1/2-a_n)^2}{n}
       +\frac{2(1/2-a_n)^3}{n^2}.
\]
Here \(2(a_n-1)^2\le1\), \(2(1/2-a_n)^2\le1/2\), and
\(2(1/2-a_n)^3\le1/4\).  Hence
\[
 E_n\le a_n+\frac1{2n}+\frac1{4n^2}
 \le\delta+\frac1{4n}+\frac1{2n}+\frac1{4n^2}
 \le\delta+\frac1n.
\]
The compensated lower bound gives \(E_n\ge\delta\).
For \(c>\delta\), choose a natural \(n>\max\{1,1/(c-\delta)\}\)
to obtain \(E_n<c\).  This proves
\eqref{eq:compensated-moment-sharp} and the claimed optimality.

These sharpness examples are finite moment models; they are not
asserted to be actual zeta zero configurations.  The sharp coefficient
concerns the energy inequality, not optimality of the complete rational
kernel penalty.  The complete \(B(T)\), its logarithmic coefficient
and additive constant are unchanged.  The central case \(M=0\) remains
outside the moment-ratio statement; this theorem gives neither a larger
certified simplicity window nor a new zero-free boundary.

\subsection{The full signed-kernel parity penalty}
\label{sec:full-kernel-parity}

The energy bound in the preceding subsection can retain its exact
rational curvature.  Write
\[
 \mathcal H(x,z)=
 \sum_{j=1}^3\frac{c_j}{r_j(r_j^2-x)(r_j^2-z)^2},
 \qquad
 \tau=\mathcal H(0,0)=\sum_{j=1}^3\frac{c_j}{r_j^7},
 \qquad
 C(t)=\delta\mathcal H(\delta t,t),
\]
where \(r=(5001/10000,7/10,6/5)\) and \(c\) is the fixed signed
three-pole weight vector.  In particular
\[
 \tau=
 \frac{37413639865876325411850661752657392635}
      {263902928706162612461624933697712128}
 \approx141.77.
\]
The symbol \(\mathcal H\) here is the tangent remainder of the
size-biased profile \(F\), rather than that of \(\Phi\).

\paragraph{Theorem (full kernel charge and sharp universal cubic constant).}
Let \(W,T,N,M,Q,t,\kappa,\mathcal B_2,K_\delta\) satisfy the
actual-zero hypotheses of the preceding compensated parity theorem.
Then \(0<t\le9/64\), \(K_\delta\) is even, and
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal B_2+C(t)t^3|\kappa-K_\delta|\le gB(T),
 \qquad C(t)>\delta\tau>\delta.
 \label{eq:full-kernel-parity}
\end{equation}
If \(\kappa\) is an odd integer, this gives
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal B_2+C(t)t^3\le gB(T),\qquad
 \mathcal B_2+\delta\tau t^3\le gB(T).
 \label{eq:full-kernel-parity-odd}
\end{equation}
The same lower profile charge holds for every finite even integer-weight
moment model with \(0\le x_i\le1/4\), \(0<t\le9/64\),
\(\sum a_i x_i^2=t\sum a_i x_i\), and odd normalized count
\(\sum a_i x_i/t\).  The constant \(\delta\tau\) is the largest
universal cubic coefficient over this finite-model class.

\paragraph{The signed curvature floor.}
We first retain the complete rational remainder.  For
\(x\le y\le1/4\) and \(z\le1/4\),
\[
 \mathcal H(y,z)-\mathcal H(x,z)
 =(y-x)\sum_{j=1}^3
 \frac{c_j}{r_j(r_j^2-x)(r_j^2-y)(r_j^2-z)^2}.
\]
The signed sum on the right is positive.  To see this without dropping
the negative second pole, its first denominator is no larger than its
second; both are positive.  Their reciprocals \(q_1,q_2\) therefore
satisfy \(q_1\ge q_2>0\), and the third reciprocal satisfies \(q_3>0\).
Since \(c_1>0\), \(c_1+c_2>0\), and \(c_3>0\),
\[
 c_1q_1+c_2q_2+c_3q_3
  =c_1(q_1-q_2)+(c_1+c_2)q_2+c_3q_3>0.
\]
Thus \(\mathcal H\) is increasing in its first argument.
The already proved cubic divided difference
\[
 D(u,v)=\sum_{j=1}^3
 \frac{c_j}{r_j^5(r_j^2-u)(r_j^2-v)}>0
 \quad(u,v\le9/64)
\]
also gives the exact anchor identity
\[
 \mathcal H(0,z)-\tau=z\{D(z,z)+D(0,z)\}.
\]
Consequently \(\mathcal H(x,z)\ge\tau\) for
\(0\le x\le1/4\), \(0\le z\le9/64\), and the inequality is strict
when \(z>0\).  Taking \(x=\delta t,z=t\) proves
\(C(t)>\delta\tau\).  The rational value above has \(\tau>1\).

\paragraph{The coupled low-threshold comparison.}
Fix \(0\le t\le9/64\) and \(0\le y\le\delta\), and put
\[
 A=2-2\delta-y,\quad D_0=2-y-\delta,\quad L=2t\delta D_0,
 \quad P_j=r_j^2A-L,\quad
 \mathcal D_j=r_j(r_j^2-t)^2(r_j^2-ty)(r_j^2-t\delta).
\]
All \(\mathcal D_j\) are positive.
The bounds \(\delta<3/10\), \(D_0\le(4/3)A\) give
\[
 A>0,\qquad 0\le L\le(9/80)A,\qquad
 \tfrac12r_j^2A\le P_j\le r_j^2A,\qquad P_j>0.
\]
For every \(u\ge0\),
\[
 \tfrac32(r_1^2-u)\le r_2^2-u.
\]
Applying this to the four retained denominator factors gives
\(\mathcal D_2\ge(81/16)\mathcal D_1\ge4\mathcal D_1\).
Also \(r_2^2\le2r_1^2\), so
\[
 P_2\mathcal D_1
 \le r_2^2A\mathcal D_1
 \le2r_1^2A\mathcal D_1
 \le\tfrac12r_1^2A\mathcal D_2
 \le P_1\mathcal D_2.
\]
Hence \(P_1/\mathcal D_1\ge P_2/\mathcal D_2>0\), and the same
signed-pair argument yields
\[
 L_{\rm low}(t,y):=\sum_{j=1}^3c_jP_j/\mathcal D_j>0.
\]
The exact numerator factorization using
\(2\delta^2-4\delta+1=0\) is
\[
 (y-1)^2\mathcal H(ty,t)
  -\delta(2-y)\mathcal H(t\delta,t)
   =(\delta-y)L_{\rm low}(t,y)\ge0.
\]
Every numerator, reciprocal and signed pole remains in this identity.

\paragraph{From the pointwise comparison to the complete profile.}
For \(t>0\), \(t\le9/64\), \(y\ge0\), and \(ty\le1/4\), the low
comparison just proved and the high comparison from monotonicity give
\begin{equation}
 -y(y-1)^2\mathcal H(ty,t)
 \le C(t)\{2y-y^2-b_\delta(y)\}
 \le y(y-1)^2\mathcal H(ty,t).
 \label{eq:full-kernel-pointwise}
\end{equation}
For the high case \(y\ge\delta\), the middle bracket is
\(-(y-1)^2\) and
\(y\mathcal H(ty,t)\ge\delta\mathcal H(t\delta,t)=C(t)\).
For the low case its upper bound is the preceding exact
factorization multiplied by \(y\); its lower bound is nonnegative
against a nonpositive left side.

Now let \(a_i\ge0\), \(0\le x_i\le1/4\), and
\(\sum a_i x_i^2=t\sum a_i x_i\).
Set \(y_i=x_i/t\).  Sum the two signed inequalities
\eqref{eq:full-kernel-pointwise} with weights \(a_i\), and use the exact
compensated ledger
\[
 \sum_i a_i(y_i-b_\delta(y_i))
   =\sum_i a_i(2y_i-y_i^2-b_\delta(y_i)).
\]
After rescaling,
\[
 C(t)t^3\left|\frac{\sum_i a_ix_i}{t}
             -\sum_i a_i b_\delta(x_i/t)\right|
 \le\sum_i a_ix_i(x_i-t)^2\mathcal H(x_i,t).
\]
Independently, the exact \(F\)-tangent identity gives
\begin{equation}
 \sum_i a_i\Phi(x_i)
   =g\sum_i a_i+F(t)\sum_i a_ix_i
      +\sum_i a_ix_i(x_i-t)^2\mathcal H(x_i,t).
 \label{eq:full-kernel-weighted-ledger}
\end{equation}
The entire slope term is
\(F'(t)\{\sum a_ix_i^2-t\sum a_ix_i\}=0\).
Thus the displayed charge is a lower bound for the complete profile
sum, including its constant and linear terms.

Apply this result to the actual orders \(a_i=m_\rho\).
The canonical strip supplies \(t\le9/64\), while the full signed
profile upper bound supplies \(\sum m_\rho\Phi(x_\rho)\le gB(T)\).
The constant and linear part of
\eqref{eq:full-kernel-weighted-ledger} is precisely \(\mathcal B_2\).
Reflection makes \(K_\delta\) even, proving
\eqref{eq:full-kernel-parity}.  Odd \(\kappa\) has distance at least
one from \(K_\delta\), proving \eqref{eq:full-kernel-parity-odd}.
For even integer weights the threshold mass is even directly, giving
the finite-model version.

\paragraph{Sharpness of the universal cubic coefficient.}
Use the previously proved atoms \(a_n,b_n\), with weights \(2,2n\).
For \(n\ge1\) and \(0<t\le1/16\), put \(x_1=ta_n,x_2=tb_n\);
both coordinates lie in \([0,1/4]\).  Their moments are exactly
\[
 M=t(2n+1),\qquad Q=t^2(2n+1),\qquad \kappa=2n+1.
\]
Define their full kernel defect by
\[
 \Delta_n(t)=2\Phi(ta_n)+2n\Phi(tb_n)
   -\{(2+2n)g+t(2n+1)F(t)\}.
\]
The braces are exactly their moment-ratio minimum \(\mathcal B_2\).
Without discarding any compensation term,
\[
 \Delta_n(t)=t^3J_n(t),\qquad
 J_n(t)=2a_n(a_n-1)^2\mathcal H(ta_n,t)
       +2nb_n(b_n-1)^2\mathcal H(tb_n,t).
\]
For each fixed \(n\), \(J_n\) is continuous at zero and
\(J_n(0)=\tau E_n\), where \(\delta\le E_n\le\delta+1/n\).

Given any \(c>\delta\tau\), first choose a natural \(n\ge1\) with
\(E_n<c/\tau\), using the already proved sharp even-weight energy
family.  Continuity then supplies a positive \(t\le1/16\) with
\(J_n(t)<c\), so \(\Delta_n(t)<ct^3\).
This is a counterexample to every larger universal cubic coefficient
within the stated finite even-weight model class.  Combined with the
general lower bound, it proves optimality of \(\delta\tau\).

The sharpness models are not asserted to be realized by zeta zeros.
The complete budget \(B(T)\), its logarithmic coefficient and additive
constant, the numerical simplicity window and the installed \(7/8\)
zero-free boundary are unchanged.  The strengthened contribution is the
horizontal parity penalty; the central case \(M=0\) remains outside
this moment-ratio statement.

\subsection{Exact optimality at every fixed positive scale}
\label{sec:fixed-scale-kernel-optimality}

The preceding sharpness statement lets the scale tend to zero.  The
stronger conclusion below fixes the scale first.  Write
\[
 C(t)=\delta\mathcal H(t\delta,t),\qquad
 a_n=\frac{2n+1-\sqrt{n(2n+1)}}{2(n+1)},\qquad
 b_n=1+\frac{1/2-a_n}{n}
\]
and, for each integer \(n\ge1\), put
\[
 \begin{split}
 J_n(t)={}&2a_n(a_n-1)^2\mathcal H(ta_n,t)\\
 &+2n b_n(b_n-1)^2\mathcal H(tb_n,t),\\
 \Delta_n(t)={}&2\Phi(ta_n)+2n\Phi(tb_n)
 -(2+2n)g-t(2n+1)F(t).
 \end{split}
\]
All three signed poles in \(\mathcal H\) are retained.

\paragraph{Theorem (exact fixed-scale parity cost).}
For every fixed \(0<t\le9/64\),
\[
 J_n(t)\ge C(t),\qquad
 \lim_{n\to\infty}J_n(t)=C(t),\qquad
 \Delta_n(t)=t^3J_n(t).
\]
Consequently, for every real \(c\),
\[
 \bigl[c\,t^3\le\Delta_n(t)\text{ for every integer }n\ge1\bigr]
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad c\le C(t).
\]
These models have even integral weights \(2,2n\), odd effective mass
\(2n+1\), and even threshold count \(2n+2\).

\paragraph{Proof.}
The previously established model bounds give
\[
 \delta\le a_n<\tfrac12,\quad
 1<b_n<\tfrac32,\quad
 n(a_n-\delta)\le\tfrac14,\quad
 2a_n+2nb_n=2a_n^2+2nb_n^2=2n+1.
\]
Thus \(0\le ta_n,tb_n\le1/4\); in fact
\(tb_n<27/128<1/4\).  The exact tangent-remainder identity,
applied to the two model radii and added with their weights, gives
\(\Delta_n=t^3J_n\).  Its linear tangent term cancels because
\[
 2a_n(a_n-1)+2nb_n(b_n-1)=0.
\]
To obtain the lower bound, use the lower half of the signed
pointwise kernel comparison at \(a_n,b_n\).  Both threshold
indicators equal one.  Their compensated sum is exactly
\[
 2(2a_n-a_n^2-1)+2n(2b_n-b_n^2-1)=-1.
\]
The resulting inequality is \(-J_n(t)\le-C(t)\).

For the limit, it is useful to avoid any separate norm estimate.
Define a two-variable continuous extension
\[
 \begin{split}
 G_t(a,u)={}&2a(a-1)^2\mathcal H(ta,t)\\
 &+2\bigl(1+(\tfrac12-a)u\bigr)(\tfrac12-a)^2u\,
 \mathcal H\bigl(t(1+(\tfrac12-a)u),t\bigr).
 \end{split}
\]
For \(n\ge1\), exact algebra gives \(G_t(a_n,1/n)=J_n(t)\).
Every denominator at \((a,u)=(\delta,0)\) is nonzero:
the relevant factors are \(r_j^2-t\delta\) and \(r_j^2-t\),
both positive for the stated range of \(t\).
Hence \(G_t\) is continuous there, with its signed coefficients intact.
The identity \(2(1-\delta)^2=1\) yields
\[
 G_t(\delta,0)=\delta\mathcal H(t\delta,t)=C(t).
\]
Moreover \(0\le a_n-\delta\le1/(4n)\), so
\((a_n,1/n)\to(\delta,0)\).  This proves the asserted limit.
In the Lean proof the convergence is stated explicitly: for every
\(\varepsilon>0\) there is an integer \(N\ge1\) such that
\(|J_n(t)-C(t)|<\varepsilon\) for every \(n\ge N\).

If \(c>C(t)\), choose such an \(n\) with
\(|J_n(t)-C(t)|<c-C(t)\).  Then
\(\Delta_n(t)<c\,t^3\), contradicting the proposed uniform
larger coefficient.  Conversely, \(c\le C(t)\), the lower bound
and \(t^3>0\) give the required inequality for every \(n\).

This is optimality within the abstract finite-model class with radii
in \([0,1/4]\).  Its total weight \(2+2n\) grows without bound.
No realization by actual zeta zeros is asserted, and a fixed-height
multiplicity budget may impose additional restrictions.
There is also an explicit endpoint distinction: since \(b_n>1\),
\(tb_n>t\) for every \(t>0\).  At \(t=9/64\) these sharpness models
therefore exceed the canonical \(7/8\)-strip radius bound \(x\le9/64\).
The theorem does not claim sharpness in that narrower endpoint class.
The complete height budget, numerical simplicity window and
\(7/8\) boundary remain unchanged.

\subsection{Finite-effective-mass stability of the parity penalty}
\label{sec:finite-mass-parity-stability}

The preceding optimality theorem permits the effective mass to tend
to infinity.  At finite effective mass there is an additional
quantitative cost.  Set
\[
 \gamma=\delta-\frac14=\frac{(1-2\delta)^2}{4}>0,
 \qquad
 \delta=1-\frac1{\sqrt2}.
\]
The coefficient \(\gamma\) below is a proved stability coefficient;
its optimality is not asserted.

\paragraph{Theorem (compensated moment stability).}
Let \(S\) be finite, \(a_i,y_i\ge0\), and suppose
\[
 M=\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i=\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i^2>0.
\]
Define
\[
 K=\sum_{i\in S}a_i\,1_{\{y_i\ge\delta\}},\quad
 D=M-K,\quad E=\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i(y_i-1)^2.
\]
Then
\begin{equation}
 M(E-\delta|D|)\ge\gamma D^2,\qquad
 E\ge\delta|D|+\frac{\gamma D^2}{M}.
 \label{eq:finite-mass-stability}
\end{equation}
In particular, if \(M\) is an odd integer and every weight \(a_i\)
is an even nonnegative integer, then
\[
 E\ge\delta+\frac{\gamma}{M}.
\]
If additionally \(M\le B\), the last lower bound is at least
\(\delta+\gamma/B>\delta\).

\paragraph{Proof.}
Write
\[
 f(y)=y(y-1)^2,\quad
 g(y)=2y-y^2-1_{\{y\ge\delta\}},\quad
 h(y)=y(y-1),\quad A=1-2\delta.
\]
The two exact moment identities give
\[
 \sum_{i\in S}a_i h(y_i)=0,\qquad
 \sum_{i\in S}a_i g(y_i)=D.
\]
For \(\sigma\in\{-1,1\}\), define the coupled residual and transport
\[
 R_\sigma(y)=f(y)-\sigma\delta g(y),\qquad
 k_\sigma(y)=h(y)+\sigma A g(y).
\]
The key pointwise certificate, valid for every \(y\ge0\), is
\begin{equation}
 k_\sigma(y)^2\le4yR_\sigma(y).
 \label{eq:parity-transport-square}
\end{equation}
Here is a full verification, retaining the threshold jump.

For \(y\ge\delta\), one has
\[
 \begin{array}{ll}
 R_+(y)=(y+\delta)(y-1)^2,&
 k_+(y)=(2\delta y+1-2\delta)(y-1),\\
 R_-(y)=(y-\delta)(y-1)^2,&
 k_-(y)=2(1-\delta)(y-\delta)(y-1).
 \end{array}
\]
The identity \(2\delta^2-4\delta+1=0\) gives
\[
 0\le2\delta y+1-2\delta\le2y,\qquad
 4(1-\delta)^2=2.
\]
Thus the positive-sign square is at most
\(4y^2(y-1)^2\le4yR_+(y)\), while
\(k_-(y)^2=2(y-\delta)R_-(y)\le4yR_-(y)\).

For \(0\le y<\delta\), the positive-sign expressions factor as
\[
 R_+(y)=y(\delta-y)(2-2\delta-y),\qquad
 k_+(y)=2\delta y(y-\delta).
\]
Since \(\delta^2\le1\) and
\(\delta-y\le2-2\delta-y\), this also proves the certificate.
For the negative sign, write
\[
 R_-(y)=yq(y),\quad
 k_-(y)=yv(y),\quad
 q(y)=(y-1)^2+\delta(2-y),\quad
 v(y)=2(1-\delta)y+4\delta-3.
\]
One has
\[
 q(y)-1=(\delta-y)(2-y)\ge0,\qquad -2\le v(y)\le0,
\]
using \(1/4<\delta<1/2\) and its quadratic identity.
Consequently \(v(y)^2\le4\le4q(y)\), proving
\eqref{eq:parity-transport-square} in the remaining case.

For every real \(u\), the square certificate implies
\[
 yu^2-u k_\sigma(y)+R_\sigma(y)\ge0.
\]
At \(y=0\) the expression vanishes.  At \(y>0\), multiply it by
\(4y\): it is the sum of
\((2yu-k_\sigma(y))^2\) and
\(4yR_\sigma(y)-k_\sigma(y)^2\), both nonnegative.
Multiplying by the weights and summing the complete expression gives
\[
 0\le Mu^2-\sigma A D u+E-\sigma\delta D.
\]
No transport or compensation cross term has been separated.
Choose \(u=\sigma A D/(2M)\), then choose
\(\sigma D=|D|\).  This yields
\[
 E-\delta|D|\ge\frac{A^2D^2}{4M}
 =\frac{\gamma D^2}{M},
\]
as required.  In the integral parity case, \(K\) is even and \(M\)
is odd, so \(|D|\ge1\) and \(D^2\ge1\).  Finally
\(0<M\le B\) implies \(\gamma/M\ge\gamma/B>0\).

\paragraph{Theorem (complete actual-height consumer).}
Let \(W\) be a finite same-height, reflection-closed critical-strip
