zero cluster at \(|T|\ge2\), with \(N>0,M_1>0\), and let
\[
 t=\frac{Q}{M_1},\quad \kappa=\frac{M_1^2}{Q},\quad
 \mathcal B_2=gN+M_1F(t),\quad
 D=\kappa-K_\delta .
\]
Then \(0<t\le9/64\), \(K_\delta\) is even,
\(0<\kappa\le B(T)\), and
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal B_2+\tau t^3
   \left(\delta|D|+\frac{\gamma D^2}{\kappa}\right)
 \le gB(T).
 \label{eq:actual-parity-stability}
\end{equation}
If \(\kappa\) is an odd integer, both
\[
 \mathcal B_2+\tau t^3
   \left(\delta+\frac{\gamma}{\kappa}\right)\le gB(T),
 \qquad
 \mathcal B_2+\tau t^3
   \left(\delta+\frac{\gamma}{B(T)}\right)\le gB(T)
\]
hold.  Combining the latter with the full signed-kernel theorem gives
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal B_2+t^3
 \max\left\{C(t),\,\tau\left(\delta+\frac{\gamma}{B(T)}\right)\right\}
 \le gB(T).
 \label{eq:combined-parity-stability}
\end{equation}

\paragraph{Proof.}
Apply \eqref{eq:finite-mass-stability} with actual analytic-order
weights and normalized radii \(y=x/t\), where
\(x=(\Re w-1/2)^2\).  The normalized first and second moments
are both \(\kappa\).  The exact signed-kernel ledger is
\[
 \sum_{w\in W}m_w\Phi(x_w)
 =\mathcal B_2+
   \sum_{w\in W}m_w x_w(x_w-t)^2\mathcal H(x_w,t).
\]
The previously proved signed-kernel floor
\(\mathcal H(x,t)\ge\tau\), valid on the entire retained domain,
and the exact cubic rescaling give
\eqref{eq:actual-parity-stability} after applying the full trace
upper budget.  The floor includes the negative pole coefficient;
it is not an unsigned replacement of that kernel.
The existing moment feasibility inequality gives \(\kappa\le N\);
the cluster-window theorem at window width zero gives \(N\le B(T)\).
Reflection gives the evenness of \(K_\delta\).
The odd case follows from \(|D|\ge1\), and the maximum combines
two proved complete lower charges against the same upper budget.

\paragraph{A nonempty regime of strict improvement.}
For every fixed \(B>0\), there is an \(\eta_B>0\) such that
\[
 0<t<\eta_B
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 C(t)<\tau\left(\delta+\frac{\gamma}{B}\right).
\]
Indeed \(C\) is continuous at zero, \(C(0)=\tau\delta\), and
\(\tau\gamma/B>0\).  This neighborhood statement is also proved
in Lean.  Thus \eqref{eq:combined-parity-stability} is strictly
stronger than the earlier full-kernel odd charge in a proved
small-scale regime at every fixed positive height budget.
There is no conflict with fixed-scale sharpness: those extremizing
models require unbounded effective mass.

The result assumes a nonzero horizontal moment and an odd integral
effective mass for its odd-gap corollaries.  It neither asserts that
actual zeros realize these moment configurations nor treats a
central cluster with \(M_1=0\).  It supplies an additional
multiplicity-geometry constraint; no larger numerical simplicity
window, global simplicity theorem, or change to the \(7/8\) boundary
is claimed.

\subsection{An explicit parameter range for the parity gain}
\label{sec:effective-parity-threshold}

The continuity argument in the preceding subsection can be made
effective without replacing the signed kernel by an unsigned norm.
Keep \(\gamma=\delta-1/4\), \(\tau=\mathcal H(0,0)\), and
\(C(t)=\delta\mathcal H(t\delta,t)\).

\paragraph{Theorem (explicit coefficient margin).}
For every \(B>0\) and \(0\le t\le1/16\) with \(1024Bt\le1\),
\begin{equation}
 C(t)+\frac3B
 <\tau\left(\delta+\frac{\gamma}{B}\right).
 \label{eq:explicit-parity-coefficient-margin}
\end{equation}
In particular the interval
\[
 0<t\le\min\left\{\frac1{16},\frac1{1024B}\right\}
\]
is an explicit nonempty range of improvement.  The numerical
constants are conservative; optimality is not claimed.

\paragraph{Proof.}
For \(r>0\), introduce the exact coordinate quantities
\[
 \begin{split}
 \mathcal N_r(d,t)&=(d+2)r^4-(2d+1)r^2t+dt^2,\\
 \mathcal D_r(d,t)&=r^7(r^2-dt)(r^2-t)^2,\\
 e(c,r,d,t)&=\frac{c}{r(r^2-dt)(r^2-t)^2}-\frac{c}{r^7}.
 \end{split}
\]
If \(r^2-dt,r^2-t>0\), direct algebra gives
\begin{equation}
 e(c,r,d,t)\mathcal D_r(d,t)=tc\,\mathcal N_r(d,t).
 \label{eq:coordinate-increment-identity}
\end{equation}
For \(0\le d\le3/10\), \(0\le t\le1/16\), and \(r\ge1/2\),
all denominators here are positive: \(dt\le3/160\),
\(r^2-dt\ge37/160\), and \(r^2-t\ge3/16\).

For the fixed radii and signed coefficients, the following entirely
rational estimates suffice:
\[
 \begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
 j&\text{denominator estimate}
   &\text{numerator estimate}&e(c_j,r_j,d,t)\\ \hline
 1&\mathcal D_{r_1}\ge1/16000
   &c_1\mathcal N_{r_1}\le3/16&\le3000t\\
 2&\mathcal D_{r_2}>0
   &\mathcal N_{r_2}\ge0,\ c_2<0&\le0\\
 3&\mathcal D_{r_3}\ge1
   &c_3\mathcal N_{r_3}\le1&\le t .
 \end{array}
\]
Here are the details behind the bounds.
For the first coordinate,
\[
 r_1^7\ge1/128,\qquad r_1^2-dt\ge37/160,\qquad
 r_1^2-t\ge3/16,
\]
so \(\mathcal D_{r_1}\ge1/16000\).
Also \(t^2\le1/256\), \(dt^2\le3/2560\), and the subtracted
middle term of \(\mathcal N_{r_1}\) is nonnegative.
Using the exact rational value \(r_1=5001/10000\) gives
\(\mathcal N_{r_1}\le3/20\), hence
\(c_1\mathcal N_{r_1}\le3/16\).
For the second coordinate,
\[
 (2d+1)r_2^2t\le(8/5)r_2^2/16,\qquad dt^2\ge0;
\]
substitution of \(r_2=7/10\) gives
\(\mathcal N_{r_2}\ge0\).
For the third coordinate, \(r_3=6/5\) gives
\(r_3^7,r_3^2-dt,r_3^2-t\ge1\), and the same
numerator calculation gives \(\mathcal N_{r_3}\le5\), hence
\(c_3\mathcal N_{r_3}\le1\).
The last column follows from
\eqref{eq:coordinate-increment-identity}, positivity of the
denominator and \(t\ge0\).

Apply the estimates at \(d=\delta<3/10\).
The signed origin is retained exactly:
\[
 \tau=\sum_{j=1}^3\frac{c_j}{r_j^7},\qquad
 C(t)-\delta\tau=\delta\sum_{j=1}^3e(c_j,r_j,\delta,t).
\]
Consequently
\begin{equation}
 C(t)-\delta\tau\le3001\delta t\le1024t
 \qquad(0\le t\le1/16).
 \label{eq:explicit-kernel-increment}
\end{equation}
In particular the negative second-pole increment has its actual
sign; its signed contribution to \(\tau\) has not been removed.

Finally, \(\tau>128\) follows from its exact rational formula.
The threshold identity and \(\delta>1/4\) imply
\(\delta>9/32\), so \(\gamma>1/32\) and
\[
 \tau\gamma>4.
\]
Together with \(1024Bt\le1\), equation
\eqref{eq:explicit-kernel-increment} gives
\[
 BC(t)+3\le B\delta\tau+4
 <B\delta\tau+\tau\gamma.
\]
Division by the positive \(B\) proves
\eqref{eq:explicit-parity-coefficient-margin}.

\paragraph{Theorem (complete effective height gap).}
Let \(W\) be a finite same-height, reflection-closed critical-strip
zero cluster at \(|T|\ge2\), with \(N>0\) and nonzero horizontal
moment.  Use the preceding definitions of \(t,\kappa,\mathcal B_2\)
and the complete budget \(B(T)\).  If \(\kappa\) is an odd integer
and
\[
 t\le\min\left\{\frac1{16},\frac1{1024B(T)}\right\},
\]
then
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal B_2+C(t)t^3+\frac{3t^3}{B(T)}<gB(T).
 \label{eq:effective-actual-parity-gap}
\end{equation}
Thus, under the same cluster and scale hypotheses, if the left
side is at least \(gB(T)\), the effective mass cannot be an odd
integer.

\paragraph{Proof.}
The existing feasibility and height estimates give
\(0<\kappa\le B(T)\), hence \(B(T)>0\).
The displayed scale condition implies \(0<t\le1/16\) and
\(1024B(T)t\le1\).  Multiply
\eqref{eq:explicit-parity-coefficient-margin} by \(t^3>0\),
add \(\mathcal B_2\), and apply the previously proved complete
finite-height charge
\[
 \mathcal B_2+\tau t^3\left(\delta+\frac{\gamma}{B(T)}\right)
 \le gB(T).
\]
This proves \eqref{eq:effective-actual-parity-gap}.
The odd-mass obstruction is its direct contrapositive.

This makes the earlier improvement range explicit in the installed
height budget and normalized moment.  The added charge is more
than \(3t^3/B(T)\) throughout the stated range.  The theorem still
requires a positive horizontal moment and odd integral effective
mass; it does not assert that a zero cluster lies in this range.
No enlargement of the numerical simplicity window, proof of
global simplicity, or improvement of the \(7/8\) boundary is claimed.

\subsection{A joint inverse band and robust recovery of the even order}
\label{sec:joint-inverse-parity}

The finite-mass estimate has a consequence for every positive real
effective mass, without an oddness or integrality assumption on that
mass.  It gives a nonlinear inverse band, which can be combined with
the stronger full-kernel linear band rather than replacing it.

Write \(\gamma=\delta-1/4>0\).  For \(M>0\), \(S\ge0\), define
\[
 R(M,S)=
 \frac{2MS}{\delta M+\sqrt{(\delta M)^2+4\gamma MS}},
 \qquad
 R_{\rm joint}(M,S,c)=\min\{R(M,S),S/c\}\quad(c>0).
\]
The rationalized formula for \(R\) has a positive denominator,
including at \(S=0\).

\paragraph{Theorem (inverse compensated parity cost).}
For \(M>0,S\ge0\), a discrepancy \(D\) satisfying
\[
 \delta|D|+\frac{\gamma D^2}{M}\le S
\]
obeys \(|D|\le R(M,S)\).  Moreover,
\begin{equation}
 R(M,S)<1
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 S<\delta+\frac{\gamma}{M}.
 \label{eq:inverse-parity-unit-threshold}
\end{equation}
For \(S>0,c>0\), the exact comparison with the linear radius is
\begin{equation}
 R(M,S)<S/c
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \gamma S>Mc(c-\delta).
 \label{eq:inverse-parity-crossover}
\end{equation}
At \(S=0\) both radii are zero.
Consequently
\[
 S<\max\{c,\delta+\gamma/M\}
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad R_{\rm joint}(M,S,c)<1.
\]

\paragraph{Proof.}
The underlying inversion works more generally for \(a\ge0,b>0,z\ge0\):
\[
 \rho(a,b,z)=\frac{2z}{b+\sqrt{b^2+4az}}.
\]
Its denominator is positive, \(\rho\ge0\), and direct multiplication
using \((\sqrt{b^2+4az})^2=b^2+4az\) gives
\[
 a\rho^2+b\rho=z.
\]
On the nonnegative half-line the cost \(ax^2+bx\) is strictly
increasing, since
\[
 (ay^2+by)-(ax^2+bx)=(y-x)\bigl(a(x+y)+b\bigr).
\]
Thus, for \(x,r\ge0\),
\[
 ax^2+bx\le z\iff x\le\rho,\qquad
 \rho<r\iff z<ar^2+br.
\]
These statements include \(a=0\); the proof never divides by \(a\).

Apply this with \(a=\gamma,b=\delta M,z=MS,x=|D|\).
Multiplication of the compensated cost by the positive \(M\)
proves the inverse bound.  Taking \(r=1\) proves
\eqref{eq:inverse-parity-unit-threshold}.
For \(r=S/c>0\), use the exact identity
\[
 \gamma(S/c)^2+\delta M(S/c)-MS
 =\frac{S}{c^2}\bigl(\gamma S-Mc(c-\delta)\bigr).
\]
The factor \(S/c^2\) is positive, proving
\eqref{eq:inverse-parity-crossover}.
If \(S=0\), the numerator defining \(\rho\) vanishes.
Finally, if the maximum in the displayed threshold is \(c\),
then \(S/c<1\); if it is \(\delta+\gamma/M\), equation
\eqref{eq:inverse-parity-unit-threshold} gives \(R<1\).
Taking the minimum proves the joint statement.

\paragraph{Theorem (actual joint inverse geometry).}
Let \(W\) be a finite same-height, reflection-closed critical-strip
zero cluster at \(|T|\ge2\), with \(N>0\) and positive horizontal
moment.  Use the previously established quantities
\(t,\kappa,\mathcal B_2,K_\delta,\tau,C(t)\), and put
\[
 L=gB(T)-\mathcal B_2,\qquad
 S=\frac{L}{\tau t^3},\qquad c=\frac{C(t)}{\tau}.
\]
Then \(S\ge0\), \(c>0\), \(K_\delta\) is an even natural number, and
\begin{equation}
 |\kappa-K_\delta|
 \le R_{\rm joint}(\kappa,S,c).
 \label{eq:actual-joint-inverse-band}
\end{equation}
If \(R_{\rm joint}(\kappa,S,c)<1\), exactly one even natural number
lies in this closed band.  In particular this unique recovery holds
whenever
\begin{equation}
 S<\max\left\{c,\delta+\frac{\gamma}{\kappa}\right\}.
 \label{eq:actual-joint-recovery-threshold}
\end{equation}

\paragraph{Proof.}
The established moment bounds give \(t>0,\kappa>0\);
the signed kernel gives \(C(t)>0\), and \(\tau>0\).
The two complete height-budget inequalities imply, for
\(d=|\kappa-K_\delta|\),
\[
 \delta d+\frac{\gamma d^2}{\kappa}\le S,\qquad cd\le S.
\]
The first of these also proves \(S\ge0\), since both terms of
its left side are nonnegative.  Thus nonnegativity of the slack is
a consequence of the complete signed consumer, not an extra
assumption about its definition.
The inverse cost theorem gives \(d\le R(\kappa,S)\), while the
second inequality gives \(d\le S/c\).
This proves \eqref{eq:actual-joint-inverse-band}, with both
previously proved constraints retained.
Reflection supplies the evenness of \(K_\delta\).

For uniqueness, if two even natural numbers \(a,b\) satisfy
\(|\kappa-a|,|\kappa-b|\le r<1\), then
\[
 |a-b|\le|\kappa-a|+|\kappa-b|<2.
\]
Distinct even integers are separated by at least two, hence \(a=b\).
The actual \(K_\delta\) supplies existence in the band.
Equation \eqref{eq:actual-joint-recovery-threshold} forces \(r<1\)
by the preceding inverse theorem.

The strict unit-radius condition cannot be replaced by a closed
unit-radius condition using lattice information alone: both \(2\)
and \(4\) are even and have distance one from \(3\).
This counterexample is also proved in Lean.  It is a statement
about the abstract even lattice, not an assertion that both
candidates are realized by actual zero clusters.

\paragraph{Theorem (an explicit increase in recoverable slack).}
Under the same cluster hypotheses, suppose additionally
\[
 t\le\min\left\{\frac1{16},\frac1{1024B(T)}\right\}.
\]
Then the closed allowance
\begin{equation}
 L\le\left(C(t)+\frac3{B(T)}\right)t^3
 \label{eq:expanded-inverse-recovery-allowance}
\end{equation}
already guarantees the unique even order in
\eqref{eq:actual-joint-inverse-band}.  No integrality or parity
assumption on \(\kappa\) is required.

\paragraph{Proof.}
The existing height estimate gives \(0<\kappa\le B(T)\).
The explicit coefficient margin proved in the preceding subsection
gives
\[
 C(t)+3/B(T)<\tau\bigl(\delta+\gamma/B(T)\bigr).
\]
Multiply by \(t^3>0\), combine with
\eqref{eq:expanded-inverse-recovery-allowance}, and divide by
\(\tau t^3>0\).  The result is
\[
 S<\delta+\frac{\gamma}{B(T)}
 \le\delta+\frac{\gamma}{\kappa}
 \le\max\{c,\delta+\gamma/\kappa\}.
\]
Thus \eqref{eq:actual-joint-recovery-threshold} applies.
The inequality involving reciprocal masses uses \(\gamma>0\);
in Lean it is obtained from the existing finite-budget cost
provider at unit discrepancy, preserving its exact hypotheses.

The full-kernel linear band alone gives unique recovery from
\(L<C(t)t^3\).  In the explicit scale range,
\eqref{eq:expanded-inverse-recovery-allowance} includes an additional
closed slack interval of length \(3t^3/B(T)>0\).
The new joint band is never larger than the former linear band;
equation \eqref{eq:inverse-parity-crossover} specifies exactly when
the quadratic information makes it strictly smaller.

The recovered quantity is an aggregate analytic-order count
selected by the horizontal threshold.  The theorem concerns
stability of that count.  Individual simplicity and the central
case with zero horizontal moment are outside its conclusion.
The numerical simplicity window and the \(7/8\) boundary remain
unchanged, and no actual realization of any particular slack
configuration is asserted.

\subsection{The sharp signed stability coefficient}
\label{sec:sharp-parity-stability}

The preceding finite-mass inequality can be strengthened without changing
its linear term or discarding either compensation term.  Write
\[
 d=\delta=1-\frac{\sqrt2}{2},\qquad A=1-2d,\qquad
 C=2(1-d)=\sqrt2,\qquad
 \Gamma=1-3d=\frac{3\sqrt2-4}{2}.
\]
Thus \(A,C,\Gamma>0\), \(A^2=C\Gamma\), and
\[
 \Gamma=2C(d-\tfrac14)=2\sqrt2(d-\tfrac14).
\]
The new quadratic coefficient is approximately \(0.12132034\);
the preceding coefficient was approximately \(0.04289322\).
The linear coefficient remains \(d\).

\paragraph{Theorem (sharp real-weight compensated stability).}
Let \(S\) be finite, and let \(a_i,y_i\ge0\), for \(i\in S\), satisfy
\[
 M=\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i
   =\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i^2>0.
\]
Define
\[
 K=\sum_{i\in S}a_i{\bf1}_{y_i\ge d},\qquad
 D=M-K,\qquad E=\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i(y_i-1)^2.
\]
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:sharp-compensated-stability}
 E\ge d|D|+\Gamma\,\frac{D^2}{M}.
\end{equation}
The coefficient \(\Gamma\) cannot be increased over this class of
nonnegative real weights.

\paragraph{Proof of the bound.}
Put \(v(y)=2y-y^2-{\bf1}_{y\ge d}\), \(h(y)=y(y-1)\), and, for
\(\sigma\in\{1,-1\}\), put
\[
 R_\sigma(y)=y(y-1)^2-\sigma d\,v(y),\qquad
 k_\sigma(y)=h(y)+\sigma A\,v(y).
\]
The full compensated residual is nonnegative.  For \(y\ge d\),
\[
 R_+(y)=(y+d)(y-1)^2,\qquad
 R_-(y)=(y-d)(y-1)^2.
\]
For \(0\le y<d\),
\[
 R_+(y)=y(d-y)(C-y),\qquad
 R_-(y)=y(y-1)^2+d\,y(2-y),
\]
which are also nonnegative.

The key extra sign is \(k_\sigma(y)\le0\) for \(0\le y\le1\).
Indeed, below \(d\) the two transports are
\[
 k_+(y)=2dy(y-d),\qquad
 k_-(y)=y(Cy+4d-3).
\]
Here \(Cd=A\), so \(Cy+4d-3\le2d-2<0\).
On \(d\le y\le1\), they are
\[
 k_+(y)=(2dy+A)(y-1),\qquad
 k_-(y)=C(y-d)(y-1),
\]
and both are nonpositive.

For \(y\ge1\) the sharper squared estimate is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:sharp-transport-square}
 k_\sigma(y)^2\le Cy^2R_\sigma(y).
\end{equation}
For the positive sign, \(0\le2dy+A\le y\) and \(C(y+d)\ge1\),
so the displayed factorization proves the estimate.
For the negative sign its difference factors as
\[
 Cy^2R_-(y)-k_-(y)^2
   =C(y-d)(y-1)^3(y-A)\ge0.
\]
Consequently, for every \(u\ge0\) and \(y\ge0\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:sharp-one-sided-packet}
 R_\sigma(y)-u k_\sigma(y)+\frac C4y^2u^2\ge0.
\end{equation}
For \(y\le1\), each retained term is nonnegative.
For \(y>1\), multiply by \(Cy^2>0\) and use the exact identity
\[
 Cy^2\left(R_\sigma-u k_\sigma+\frac C4y^2u^2\right)
 =\left(\frac C2y^2u-k_\sigma\right)^2
   +Cy^2R_\sigma-k_\sigma^2.
\]
This preserves the signed transport rather than bounding its magnitude
on the entire domain.

The moment constraint gives
\[
 \sum_{i\in S}a_i h(y_i)=0,\qquad
 \sum_{i\in S}a_i v(y_i)=D.
\]
Summing the complete packet~\eqref{eq:sharp-one-sided-packet} therefore yields
\[
 0\le\frac C4 M u^2-u\sigma A D+E-\sigma dD.
\]
Choose \(\sigma D=|D|\) and \(u=2A|D|/(CM)\ge0\).
The resulting inequality is
\[
 E\ge d|D|+\frac{A^2}{C}\frac{D^2}{M}
       =d|D|+\Gamma\frac{D^2}{M},
\]
as required.

\paragraph{Proof of sharpness.}
For any \(z>1\), take two coordinates \(d,z\) with respective weights
\[
 w=\frac{2z(z-1)}A,\qquad1.
\]
Both weights are positive and both coordinates belong to the threshold
set.  The moment relation follows from \(2d(1-d)=A\):
\[
 wd^2+z^2=wd+z=M.
\]
For this model,
\[
 M=z(Cz-A),\qquad
 D=-(z-1)((C+2)z-1)<0,\qquad
 E-d|D|=(z-d)(z-1)^2.
\]
The identities \(Cd=A\), \(Ad=\Gamma\),
\(\Gamma(C+2)=A\), and \(A(C+2)=C\) imply
\[
 (Cz-A)(z-d)=\Gamma((C+2)z-1)^2.
\]
Hence the exact excess ratio is
\[
 \frac{M(E-d|D|)}{D^2}=\Gamma z.
\]
Given \(c>\Gamma\), choose \(z=(1+c/\Gamma)/2\).
Then \(1<z<c/\Gamma\), so this profile violates the bound with
\(c\) in place of \(\Gamma\).  This proves real-weight sharpness.
This proof concerns real weights.  Section~\ref{sec:even-integral-parity}
settles the even integral case; actual-zero sharpness is not asserted.

\paragraph{The actual signed consumer.}
Retain the preceding actual-cluster hypotheses, including the same-height
reflection condition and \(M_1>0\), and put
\(t=Q/M_1\), \(\kappa=M_1^2/Q\), and \(d_0=|\kappa-K_\delta|\).
Rescale \(y_w=(\Re w-\tfrac12)^2/t\), with weights equal to the
actual analytic orders.  The exact second-moment relation holds.
Applying~\eqref{eq:sharp-compensated-stability}, multiplying by
\(\tau t^3\), retaining the full signed kernel charge, and using the
installed height budget gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:actual-sharp-parity-stability}
 \mathcal B_2+\tau t^3
   \left(\delta d_0+\Gamma\frac{d_0^2}{\kappa}\right)
 \le gB(T),\qquad K_\delta\in2\mathbb N.
\end{equation}
Compared with the preceding stability consumer, its additional retained
charge is exactly
\[
 \tau t^3\bigl(\Gamma-(\delta-\tfrac14)\bigr)\frac{d_0^2}{\kappa}
 \ge0,
\]
and is positive when \(d_0>0\).  The full-kernel linear inequality remains
available as an additional constraint.
The new scalar, weighted, sharpness and actual-cluster assertions are
proved in the maintained Lean modules
\texttt{SharpCompensatedParityStability} and
\texttt{CarmichaelSharpParityStability}.
This is a specialized sharp moment inequality within the general
\href{https://epubs.siam.org/doi/10.1137/S1052623401399903}
{moment-optimization framework of Bertsimas and Popescu}.
It does not provide an independent global small-value-area estimate,
extend the certified numerical simplicity window, or improve the
installed \(7/8\) boundary.  Central clusters with \(M_1=0\) remain
outside this consumer.

\subsection{Equality rigidity and recovery at the closed endpoint}
\label{sec:sharp-parity-rigidity}

The sharp coefficient has a stronger equality statement.  Use a finite
support \(I\), and retain the profile hypotheses
of~\eqref{eq:sharp-compensated-stability}, including
\(M>0\), and write \(\Gamma=1-3\delta\).

\paragraph{Theorem (exact equality classification).}
One has
\[
 E=\delta|D|+\Gamma D^2/M
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \text{every coordinate with positive weight belongs to }\{0,1\}.
\]
In particular every equality profile has \(D=E=0\), and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:sharp-parity-strict}
 D\ne0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 E>\delta|D|+\Gamma D^2/M.
\end{equation}
Thus the preceding two-atom sharpness sequence approaches the best
coefficient through strict inequalities.

\paragraph{Proof.}
Denote the left side of the pointwise packet
\eqref{eq:sharp-one-sided-packet} by
\[
 \mathcal Q_\sigma(y,u)
   =R_\sigma(y)-u k_\sigma(y)+Cy^2u^2/4.
\]
For \(0<y\le1\) and \(u>0\), the last term is positive while
\(R_\sigma\ge0\) and \(-u k_\sigma\ge0\).  Hence
\(\mathcal Q_\sigma(y,u)>0\).
For \(y>1\), the squared certificate is strict:
\[
 k_\sigma(y)^2<Cy^2R_\sigma(y).
\]
For the positive sign this follows from
\((2\delta y+A)^2\le y^2\), \(C(y+\delta)>1\), and
\((y-1)^2>0\).
For the negative sign the exact difference is
\(C(y-\delta)(y-1)^3(y-A)>0\).
The square-completion identity from the preceding proof therefore gives
\(\mathcal Q_\sigma(y,u)>0\) for \(y>1\) as well.

Since \(M=\sum_{i\in I}a_i y_i>0\), at least one summand has both
\(a_i>0\) and \(y_i>0\).  The complete weighted packet consequently has
strictly positive sum whenever \(u>0\).
For \(D\ne0\), the previous optimizer
\(u=2A|D|/(CM)\) is positive.  Substitution proves
\eqref{eq:sharp-parity-strict}.

Suppose equality holds.  Strictness forces \(D=0\), so equality becomes
\(E=0\).  Each summand \(a_i y_i(y_i-1)^2\) is nonnegative.
A positive-weight coordinate must therefore be \(0\) or \(1\).
Conversely, for such a profile,
\({\bf1}_{y_i\ge\delta}=y_i\) at every positive-weight coordinate,
and \(y_i(y_i-1)^2=0\).  Thus \(D=E=0\) and equality holds.
Zero-weight coordinates are unrestricted.

\paragraph{Theorem (actual recovery at the closed endpoint).}
Retain the actual-cluster hypotheses of
\eqref{eq:actual-sharp-parity-stability}.  Put
\[
 L=gB(T)-\mathcal B_2,\qquad S=\frac{L}{\tau t^3},
 \qquad \kappa=M_1^2/Q>0.
\]
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:closed-parity-recovery}
 S\le\delta+\frac{\Gamma}{\kappa}
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 |\kappa-K_\delta|<1.
\end{equation}
Consequently \(K_\delta\) is the unique even natural number at distance
strictly less than \(1\) from \(\kappa\).
The premise includes equality in its slack threshold and places no
integrality or oddness assumption on \(\kappa\).

\paragraph{Proof.}
If \(d_0=|\kappa-K_\delta|>0\), strict weighted stability, multiplication by
the positive \(\tau t^3\), the full signed kernel charge, and the weak
height-budget upper bound give the strict actual inequality
\[
 \mathcal B_2+\tau t^3
   \left(\delta d_0+\Gamma d_0^2/\kappa\right)<gB(T).
\]
Thus \(\delta d_0+\Gamma d_0^2/\kappa<S\).
If \(d_0\ge1\), positivity of \(\delta,\Gamma,\kappa\) gives
\[
 S>\delta d_0+\Gamma d_0^2/\kappa
   \ge\delta+\Gamma/\kappa,
\]
contradicting the premise.
The case \(d_0=0\) is immediate.
The actual \(K_\delta\) is even.  Two distinct even natural numbers are
separated by at least \(2\), so two of them cannot both lie at distance
strictly less than \(1\) from the same real \(\kappa\).

The strict arithmetic inequality is essential at this endpoint.
A closed radius-one lattice band alone can contain two even integers:
both \(2\) and \(4\) are at distance \(1\) from \(3\).
The theorem proves that the actual profile lies in the open band,
even under the closed slack assumption.
The full-kernel linear recovery condition remains available.

The equality classification, strict scaling and charge transfers, and
closed-endpoint actual recovery are proved in
\texttt{CompensatedParityRigidity} and
\texttt{CarmichaelParityRigidity}.
This improves an aggregate even-order recovery criterion.  It supplies
neither an independent global small-value-area estimate nor a larger
numerical simplicity window.  The positive-horizontal-moment hypothesis
is unchanged.

\subsection{The complete coefficient classification for even integral weights}
\label{sec:even-integral-parity}

The sharp coefficient remains optimal after restricting the weights to
even natural numbers.  This is stronger than sharpness over arbitrary
nonnegative real weights.  It uses a different family from the earlier
models with exactly odd effective mass.

For \(c\in\mathbb R\), let \(\operatorname{Adm}_{\mathrm{even}}(c)\)
mean that, for every finite support \(I=\{1,\ldots,m\}\), every
\(a_i\in2\mathbb N\), and every \(y_i\ge0\) satisfying
\[
 M=\sum_{i\in I}a_i y_i=\sum_{i\in I}a_i y_i^2>0,
\]
one has
\[
 E=\sum_{i\in I}a_i y_i(y_i-1)^2
 \ge \delta|D|+cD^2/M,\qquad
 D=M-\sum_{i\in I}a_i{\bf1}_{y_i\ge\delta}.
\]
Zero weights are allowed; the positive-mass hypothesis excludes the
degenerate zero denominator.

\paragraph{Theorem (complete even-integral coefficient classification).}
For every real \(c\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:even-integral-classification}
 \operatorname{Adm}_{\mathrm{even}}(c)
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad c\le\Gamma=1-3\delta.
\end{equation}
For every \(c>\Gamma\), two positive even natural weights already suffice
to violate the proposed bound.  There are no unspecified asymptotic
constants in the witness rule below.

\paragraph{The exact family.}
Put \(A=1-2\delta>0\) and \(C=2(1-\delta)=\sqrt2\).
For any natural \(n\ge1\), set
\[
 z_n=\frac{1+\sqrt{1+2A/n}}2.
\]
Take the two coordinates \(\delta,z_n\) with weights \(2,2n\).
The radical gives the exact identity
\[
 z_n(z_n-1)=\frac A{2n}>0.
\]
Since \(z_n>0\), this implies \(z_n>1\).
Moreover,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:even-integral-excess}
 0<z_n-1<\frac A{2n},
\end{equation}
because \(z_n(z_n-1)-(z_n-1)=(z_n-1)^2>0\).

Let \(M_n,D_n,E_n\) denote the resulting mass, discrepancy and energy:
\[
 \begin{split}
 M_n&=2\delta+2nz_n,\\
 D_n&=M_n-(2+2n),\\
 E_n&=2\delta(\delta-1)^2+2nz_n(z_n-1)^2.
 \end{split}
\]
Both coordinates are selected by the threshold convention
\(y\ge\delta\).  The mass is positive and the moment identity holds:
\[
 2\delta^2+2nz_n^2=M_n.
\]
One way to check every term is to use the previously proved real model.
Its lower weight is
\[
 w(z_n)=\frac{2z_n(z_n-1)}A=\frac1n.
\]
Scaling that complete model by \(2n\) produces exactly the weights
\(2,2n\).  Mass, discrepancy and signed energy all scale by the same
positive factor.  Its exact ratio therefore gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:even-integral-exact-ratio}
 M_n(E_n-\delta|D_n|)=\Gamma z_nD_n^2.
\end{equation}
The discrepancy has the additional exact description
\begin{equation}\label{eq:even-integral-discrepancy}
 D_n=\frac A{z_n}-C,\qquad -C<D_n<-1.
\end{equation}
Indeed \(2n(z_n-1)=A/z_n\), and \(C-A=1\).
Thus these even-weight profiles lie outside the unit-distance band.
They do not replace the earlier family whose effective mass was
constrained to be exactly odd.

\paragraph{Proof of the classification.}
If \(c\le\Gamma\), the compiled real-weight theorem gives
\(E\ge\delta|D|+\Gamma D^2/M\).
Since \(D^2/M\ge0\), this implies the inequality with coefficient \(c\)
on the entire even-natural-weight class.

Conversely, let \(c>\Gamma\) and put \(q=c/\Gamma>1\).
Choose any natural number satisfying the explicit bound
\begin{equation}\label{eq:even-integral-witness}
 n>\max\left\{1,\frac A{2q(q-1)}\right\}.
\end{equation}
Such an \(n\) exists by the Archimedean property.
The denominator is positive, and the bound implies
\[
 z_n(z_n-1)=\frac A{2n}<q(q-1).
\]
The function \(u(u-1)\) is strictly increasing for \(u\ge1\):
its difference at \(v\ge u\ge1\) is
\((v-u)(v+u-1)\).
Consequently \(z_n<q\).
The exact ratio~\eqref{eq:even-integral-exact-ratio}, together with
\(M_n>0\) and \(D_n\ne0\), now gives
\[
 E_n<\delta|D_n|+cD_n^2/M_n.
\]
This contradicts admissibility and proves the converse for every
\(c>\Gamma\).

The finite weights, nonnegative coordinates, complete finite-sum
admissibility predicate, uniform witness and equivalence are proved in
\texttt{EvenIntegralParityOptimality}.
This settles the optimal coefficient in the even integral moment class.
It does not assert that these profiles are realized by zeta zeros, or
that the same coefficient is optimal after imposing a fixed bounded
height budget.  The installed global simplicity and zero-free-boundary
conclusions are unchanged.

\subsection{The sharp directional coefficient region}
\label{sec:directional-parity-stability}

Retaining the sign of the discrepancy gives a stronger positive branch.
Write
\[
 A=1-2\delta,\qquad
 \beta_+=A^2(1+\delta)=\Gamma+4\delta^3>\Gamma,
 \qquad D_+=\max(D,0).
\]
For \(M>0\), define
\[
 \mathcal C(M,D)=\delta|D|+
 \begin{cases}
   \beta_+D^2/M,&D\ge0,\\
   \Gamma D^2/M,&D<0.
 \end{cases}
\]
In particular,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:directional-exact-gain}
 \mathcal C(M,D)
 =\delta|D|+\Gamma D^2/M+4\delta^3D_+^2/M.
\end{equation}

\paragraph{Theorem (sharp directional stability).}
For every finite support \(I\), nonnegative real weights \(a_i\), and
coordinates \(y_i\ge0\) with
\[
 M=\sum_{i\in I}a_i y_i=\sum_{i\in I}a_i y_i^2>0,
 \quad
 D=M-\sum_{i\in I}a_i{\bf1}_{y_i\ge\delta},
 \quad
 E=\sum_{i\in I}a_i y_i(y_i-1)^2,
\]
one has \(E\ge\mathcal C(M,D)\).
More generally, keep the linear coefficient \(\delta\) fixed and use
quadratic coefficient \(c_+\) on \(D\ge0\) and \(c_-\) on \(D<0\).
This inequality holds for every such real-weight profile if and only if
\begin{equation}\label{eq:directional-coefficient-region}
 c_+\le\beta_+,\qquad c_-\le\Gamma.
\end{equation}

\paragraph{Proof of the positive bound.}
Use the complete compensated residual \(R_+\) and transport \(k_+\)
from the preceding sections, and put \(q=1+\delta>0\).
For \(y\ge1\), the exact polynomial identity
\[
 \begin{split}
 &y^2(y+\delta)-q(2\delta y+A)^2\\
 &\qquad=(y-1)\bigl(y^2+q(1-4\delta^2)y+qA^2\bigr)\ge0
 \end{split}
\]
has only nonnegative factors on the stated domain.
The factorizations
\(R_+=(y+\delta)(y-1)^2\) and
\(k_+=(2\delta y+A)(y-1)\) imply
\[
 q k_+(y)^2\le y^2R_+(y).
\]
For every \(u\ge0\) and \(y\ge0\), it follows that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:directional-positive-packet}
 R_+(y)-quk_+(y)+\frac q4 y^2u^2\ge0.
\end{equation}
On \(0\le y\le1\), use \(R_+\ge0\) and \(k_+\le0\).
For \(y>1\), the exact square completion is
\[
 qy^2\left(R_+-quk_++\frac q4 y^2u^2\right)
   =\left(\frac q2y^2u-qk_+\right)^2+
      q\bigl(y^2R_+-qk_+^2\bigr)\ge0.
\]
The moment identities retain both compensation terms:
\[
 \sum_{i\in I}a_iR_+(y_i)=E-\delta D,\qquad
 \sum_{i\in I}a_i k_+(y_i)=AD.
\]
Summing the complete packet gives
\[
 0\le E-\delta D-quAD+\frac q4Mu^2.
\]
For \(D\ge0\), choose \(u=2AD/M\ge0\).
Then \(E\ge\delta D+qA^2D^2/M\), with \(qA^2=\beta_+\).
For \(D<0\), apply the already proved symmetric sharp coefficient
\(\Gamma\).  This proves the full directional inequality and
\eqref{eq:directional-exact-gain}.

\paragraph{Positive-side sharpness.}
Let \(0<e<1/2\), and set
\[
 u=\delta-e^2,\qquad z=1+e,\qquad
 w=\frac{z(z-1)}{u(1-u)}.
\]
Since \(\delta>1/4\), one has \(0<u<\delta<1\), \(z>1\), and \(w>0\).
Take two coordinates \(u,z\) with real weights \(w,1\).
The exact moment equation is
\[
 wu^2+z^2=wu+z=M.
\]
Only the upper coordinate is selected, so \(D=M-1>0\).
Direct calculation gives
\[
 M=\frac{z(z-u)}{1-u},\qquad
 D=\frac{e(z+1-u)}{1-u},\qquad E=ze(z-u).
\]
Define the polynomial
\[
 P(e)=1-\delta^2+(3-4\delta)e+(3-2\delta)e^2+2e^3+e^4.
\]
The threshold equation \(2\delta^2-4\delta+1=0\) gives the exact identity
\[
 z(z-u)(1-u)-\delta(z+1-u)=eP(e).
\]
Consequently,
\[
 E-\delta D=\frac{e^2P(e)}{1-u},\qquad
 M(E-\delta D)=R(e)D^2,
 \quad
 R(e)=\frac{z(z-u)P(e)}{(z+1-u)^2}.
\]
This reduced ratio is continuous at \(e=0\); its denominator there is
\((2-\delta)^2>0\).  Its value is
\[
 R(0)=\frac{(1-\delta)(1-\delta^2)}{(2-\delta)^2}
      =A^2(1+\delta)=\beta_+,
\]
where \(1-\delta=A(2-\delta)\).
Thus every \(c_+>\beta_+\) admits some \(0<e<1/2\) with \(R(e)<c_+\).
Since \(M,D>0\), that profile satisfies
\[
 E<\delta D+c_+D^2/M.
\]
This proves positive-side necessity for every proposed larger coefficient.
The reduced ratio is a continuous extension after cancellation; no
value of the raw \(0/0\) expression is used.

\paragraph{Completion of the coefficient classification.}
The preceding even-integral family has \(D_n<0\) and violates every
coefficient \(c_->\Gamma\), independently of \(c_+\).
Together with the positive-side family, this proves both necessary
conditions in~\eqref{eq:directional-coefficient-region}.
Conversely, \(D^2/M\ge0\), so decreasing the applicable coefficient
preserves the directional inequality.  This proves sufficiency for
every real pair \((c_+,c_-)\).
The optimal-pair statement is for real weights.  The positive-side
counterexample weights are not asserted to be even integers or realized
analytic orders of zeta zeros.

\paragraph{The complete actual signed gain.}
Retain the actual finite-cluster hypotheses, including \(|T|\ge2\),
same height, reflection closure, \(N>0\), and \(M_1>0\).
Put \(t=Q/M_1>0\), \(\kappa=M_1^2/Q>0\), and
\(D=\kappa-K_\delta\).
The exact rescaling of the moment profile, the full signed kernel
charge, and the installed height budget give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:actual-directional-signed-gain}
 \mathcal B_2+\tau t^3
   \left(\delta|D|+\Gamma D^2/\kappa\right)
   +\tau t^3\,\frac{4\delta^3D_+^2}{\kappa}
 \le gB(T),\qquad K_\delta\in2\mathbb N.
\end{equation}
Every compensation term is retained.  The added charge is positive
when \(D>0\), and its exact coefficient is independent of the height
and the chosen finite cluster.  The full-kernel linear constraint
remains available.

The packet certificate, weighted bound, complete real-weight coefficient
region, counterexamples, and actual signed gain are proved in
\texttt{DirectionalParityStability},
\texttt{DirectionalParityOptimality}, and
\texttt{CarmichaelDirectionalParity}.
This improves the signed finite-cluster bound while retaining the
positive-horizontal-moment hypothesis.  It supplies neither an
independent global small-value-area estimate nor a new numerical
simplicity window or zero-free boundary.

\subsection{An optimal shift enlarges the closed recovery budget}
\label{sec:shifted-parity-recovery}

The asymmetric coefficient region has a quantitative consequence that is lost
if the rounding center is kept at the effective mass.  Throughout this
subsection write
\[
 d=\delta,\qquad \gamma=\Gamma,\qquad
 b_+=\beta_+=(1-2d)^2(1+d),\qquad g_0=b_+-\gamma=4d^3>0.
\]
For every \(\kappa>0\) put
\[
 f_c(u)=du+\frac{c}{\kappa}u^2,\qquad
 b=2d\kappa+4\gamma,\qquad
 x=\frac{2b}{b+\sqrt{b^2+4g_0b}},\qquad S_*=f_{b_+}(x).
\]
All denominators here are positive.  The shift and allowance depend on
\(\kappa\); no finite restriction on that parameter is imposed.

\paragraph{Theorem (optimal shifted closed-budget recovery).}
One has \(0<x<1\) and
\begin{equation}
 g_0x^2+bx=b,\qquad
 S_*=f_{b_+}(x)=f_\gamma(2-x)
   >d+\frac{\gamma}{\kappa}.
 \label{eq:shifted-parity-matching}
\end{equation}
For \(0\le r\le2\),
\begin{equation}
 \min\{f_{b_+}(r),f_\gamma(2-r)\}\le S_*,
 \qquad\text{with equality exactly when }r=x.
 \label{eq:shifted-parity-optimum}
\end{equation}
Thus \(S_*\) is the uniquely optimal endpoint allowance obtained from these
two costs by using a band \(D\in(r-2,r)\) of width two containing zero.
This optimality statement does not claim that no further arithmetic
information could yield a stronger recovery theorem.

For an actual finite same-height reflection-closed strip cluster, retain the
hypotheses \(|T|\ge2\), \(N>0\), \(M_1>0\), and the definitions
\(t=Q/M_1\), \(\kappa=M_1^2/Q\), and
\(K=K_\delta\in2\mathbb N\).  Write
\(D=\kappa-K\) and let \(\mathcal B_2\) denote the same full moment-ratio
charge as above.  The closed raw budget
\begin{equation}
 gB(T)-\mathcal B_2\le \tau t^3 S_*
 \label{eq:shifted-parity-raw-budget}
\end{equation}
implies
\begin{equation}
 -(2-x)<D<x,\qquad
 \left|(\kappa+1-x)-K\right|<1.
 \label{eq:shifted-parity-recovered-order}
\end{equation}
In particular, \(K\) is the unique even natural number in the open unit
neighborhood of the shifted center \(\kappa+1-x\).  The permitted raw budget
in \eqref{eq:shifted-parity-raw-budget} is strictly larger than the previous
centered allowance \(\tau t^3(d+\gamma/\kappa)\).

\paragraph{Proof of strict directional stability.}
The endpoint inclusion requires a strict inequality, rather than a weak
bound followed by rounding.  In the positive-direction certificate from
\eqref{eq:directional-positive-packet}, the high-coordinate factorization is
\[
 y^2(y+d)-(1+d)(2dy+1-2d)^2
 =(y-1)\{y^2+(1+d)(1-4d^2)y+(1-2d)^2(1+d)\}.
\]
For \(y>1\) the right side is strictly positive, since \(0<d<1/2\).
Consequently, after multiplication by \((y-1)^2\),
\[
 (1+d)k_+(y)^2<y^2R_+(y).
\]
Square completion shows that the full positive packet is strictly positive
on this range.  For \(0<y\le1\) and \(u>0\), its term
\((1+d)y^2u^2/4\) is strictly positive, while
\(R_+(y)\ge0\) and \(- (1+d)u k_+(y)\ge0\).
Therefore the packet is strictly positive at every positive coordinate
when \(u>0\).

A finite nonnegative profile with \(M>0\) has at least one index with
\(a_i y_i>0\).  Summing the packets retains a strictly positive term.
The exact compensation identities remain
\[
 \sum_i a_iR_+(y_i)=E-dD,\qquad
 \sum_i a_i k_+(y_i)=(1-2d)D,\qquad
 \sum_i a_i y_i^2=M.
\]
When \(D>0\), the optimizing tilt \(u=2(1-2d)D/M\) is strictly positive,
so the resulting bound is \(E>dD+b_+D^2/M\).  When \(D<0\), the already
proved sharp rigidity theorem gives \(E>d|D|+\gamma D^2/M\).
Positive rescaling and the full signed kernel ledger preserve strictness.
For the actual cluster this proves, whenever \(D\ne0\),
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal B_2+\tau t^3\mathcal C(\kappa,D)<gB(T),
 \label{eq:shifted-parity-strict-consumer}
\end{equation}
where \(\mathcal C\) is the complete directional cost, including both
compensation contributions.  No separate norm estimate is used.

\paragraph{Proof of the root, optimality, and recovery.}
Here \(b>0\) and \(g_0>0\).  The previously proved rationalized quadratic
inverse gives \(g_0x^2+bx=b\).  Positivity of the displayed quotient gives
\(x>0\), and comparison with the quadratic at one gives \(x<1\).
A direct identity retains every term:
\[
 \kappa\{f_{b_+}(x)-f_\gamma(2-x)\}
 =g_0x^2+bx-b=0.
\]
Since \(f_\gamma\) is strictly increasing on the nonnegative reals and
\(2-x>1\), this also proves the strict gain in
\eqref{eq:shifted-parity-matching}.  For \(r<x\), the first endpoint cost
is strictly below \(f_{b_+}(x)\); for \(r>x\), the second is strictly below
\(f_\gamma(2-x)\).  At \(r=x\) both equal \(S_*\), proving
\eqref{eq:shifted-parity-optimum} with its uniqueness assertion.

If \(D\ge x\), then \(D>0\), and the strict consumer and the closed
budget would give
\[
 S_*\le f_{b_+}(D)=\mathcal C(\kappa,D)
 <\frac{gB(T)-\mathcal B_2}{\tau t^3}\le S_*,
\]
a contradiction.  If \(D\le x-2\), the same argument uses
\(f_\gamma(-D)\ge f_\gamma(2-x)=S_*\).  Both denominators are positive
because \(t,\tau>0\).  The case \(D=0\) is already inside the required
band.  This proves \eqref{eq:shifted-parity-recovered-order}.  Two distinct
even natural numbers differ by at least two, so an open interval of length
two contains at most one of them; the actual even order supplies existence.

The twenty new theorem bodies, including the strict scalar and weighted
transfers, the unique optimal shift, and the actual recovery statement, are
implemented in \texttt{Helpers/DirectionalParityRigidity.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/ShiftedParityRecovery.lean}, and
\texttt{Assembly/CarmichaelShiftedParity.lean}.  This is an exact increase
of the allowable recovery slack.  It does not enlarge the certified numerical
simplicity window or prove global simplicity.  In particular, the hypothesis
\(M_1>0\) excludes clusters supported entirely on the critical line; their
individual multiplicities require the separate observable estimates.

\subsection{The directional region is unchanged by even integral weights}
\label{sec:even-directional-region}

The positive-side sharpness construction above used real weights.  This is
a genuine distinction from an even integral multiplicity constraint: a
small real weight need not itself be a permitted multiplicity.  Nevertheless,
the full directional coefficient region survives that restriction exactly.

\paragraph{Theorem (even-integral directional classification).}
For every pair of real coefficients \(c_+,c_-\), the inequality
\begin{equation}
 E\ge \delta|D|+
 \begin{cases}c_+D^2/M,&D\ge0,\\c_-D^2/M,&D<0\end{cases}
 \label{eq:even-directional-classification-bound}
\end{equation}
holds for every finite nonnegative equal-moment profile of positive mass
whose weights are even natural numbers if and only if
\begin{equation}
 c_+\le\beta_+,\qquad c_-\le\Gamma.
 \label{eq:even-directional-classification-region}
\end{equation}
Equivalently, admissibility in this two-coefficient class for all even-natural
weights is the same as admissibility for all nonnegative real weights.
Moreover, for every \(c>\beta_+\), there is an integer \(N>0\) such that
for every integer \(n\ge N\) a counterexample to the positive branch exists
with weights exactly \((2,2n)\).  These models have positive discrepancy and
mass greater than \(2n\).

\paragraph{Proof by exact weight tuning.}
Use the already proved positive model, with
\[
 u(e)=\delta-e^2,\qquad z(e)=1+e,\qquad
 W(e)=\frac{z(e)(z(e)-1)}{u(e)(1-u(e))},\qquad 0<e<1/2.
\]
Its real weights are \((W(e),1)\), its high coordinate alone is selected,
and its exact identities include
\[
 W(e)u(e)^2+z(e)^2=M(e),\qquad D(e)=M(e)-1>0,
 \qquad M(e)\{E(e)-\delta D(e)\}=R(e)D(e)^2.
\]
The reduced ratio \(R\) is continuous at zero and
\(R(0)=\beta_+\).  Fix \(c>\beta_+\).  Choose \(0<r<1/2\) sufficiently
small that \(R(e)<c\) for every \(0\le e\le r\).  This follows from the
proved continuity; the endpoint \(r\) is chosen strictly inside the resulting
neighborhood.  On \([0,r]\) one has \(0<u(e)<1\), so \(W\) is continuous,
\(W(0)=0\), and \(W(r)>0\).

Choose an integer \(N>\max\{1,1/W(r)\}\).  For every integer \(n\ge N\),
\(0<1/n<W(r)\).  The intermediate value theorem supplies
\(e_n\in[0,r]\) with \(W(e_n)=1/n\).  Since \(W(0)=0\), this parameter
is positive.  Therefore
\[
 0<e_n\le r<1/2,\qquad (2n)W(e_n)=2,\qquad R(e_n)<c.
\]
Multiplying both original weights by \(2n\) gives the required even
natural weights \((2,2n)\), with exactly preserved moment and threshold
identities.  More explicitly,
\[
 M_n=2u(e_n)+2nz(e_n)=2nM(e_n),\quad
 D_n=M_n-2n=2nD(e_n)>0,\quad E_n=2nE(e_n).
\]
There is no rounding or approximation of a weight.  The exact signed ratio
scales as
\[
 M_n\{E_n-\delta D_n\}=R(e_n)D_n^2<cD_n^2.
\]
Because \(M_n>0\), this is precisely
\(E_n<\delta D_n+cD_n^2/M_n\).  Also \(u(e_n)>0\) and \(z(e_n)>1\)
give \(M_n>2n\).  This proves positive necessity at every sufficiently large
integer scale.  The previously proved negative-discrepancy family with
weights \((2,2n)\) proves \(c_-\le\Gamma\).  Sufficiency is the existing
real-weight directional theorem applied to the natural weights after casting
them to nonnegative reals.  This proves both directions of
\eqref{eq:even-directional-classification-region}.

The sixteen new theorem bodies are implemented in
\texttt{Helpers/EvenDirectionalWeightTuning.lean} and
\texttt{Helpers/EvenDirectionalParityOptimality.lean}.  The intermediate
value step, exact scaled moments, positive discrepancy, all sufficiently
large weight scales, and the complete coefficient region are formalized.
The countermodels are finite moment profiles, not claimed realizations as
zeta zero clusters.  Thus the theorem shows exactly that even-integral
weights alone cannot increase either universal directional coefficient.
It leaves room for additional arithmetic constraints on actual clusters.
The full actual signed bound, the numerical simplicity window, and the
certified \(7/8\) boundary are unchanged.

\subsection{Explicit nonlinear costs beyond both sharp quadratics}
\label{sec:nonlinear-threshold-cost}

The sharp quadratic coefficients do not exhaust the information in the two
moment constraints.  Cubic dual packets retain an additional positive cost
on both discrepancy signs.  Polynomial dual certificates belong to the
classical moment-optimization framework.\footnote{D.~Bertsimas and I.~Popescu,
\emph{Optimal Inequalities in Probability Theory: A Convex Optimization
Approach}, SIAM J. Optim. 15 (2005), 780--804,
DOI \texttt{10.1137/S1052623401399903}.}
The result here supplies explicit compensated threshold costs and their
complete signed detector consumer.  Standalone priority for the abstract
moment inequalities is not asserted.

\paragraph{Theorem (two nonlinear threshold costs).}
Let \(S\) be finite, let \(a_i,y_i\ge0\), and suppose
\[
 M=\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i=\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i^2>0,\qquad
 K=\sum_{i\in S}a_i\mathbf1_{y_i\ge d},\qquad
 E=\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i(y_i-1)^2,\qquad 0<d<1.
\]
Then \(K>0\), \(dK<M\), and \(E\ge\mathcal F_d(M,K)\), where
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal F_d(M,K)=
 \begin{cases}
 M(1-d)(z_+-1),&K\le M,\\[2pt]
 \displaystyle\frac{dM(1-d)(K-M)}{M-dK},&K>M,
 \end{cases}
 \qquad
 z_+=\frac{d+\sqrt{d^2+4(1-d)M/K}}2.
 \label{eq:nonlinear-threshold-cost}
\end{equation}
All denominators in this statement are positive.  No assertion about the
global infimum over profiles is needed for this lower-bound theorem.

\paragraph{Proof of the admissible domain.}
There is an index with \(a_i>0\) and \(y_i>d\): otherwise every positive
weight would have \(y_i\le d\), giving
\(M=\sum a_i y_i^2\le d\sum a_i y_i=dM<M\).
That index contributes positively to \(K\).  At every index,
\(y_i-d\mathbf1_{y_i\ge d}\ge0\), and the displayed index contributes
strictly positively to its weighted sum.  Hence
\(M-dK=\sum_{i\in S}a_i(y_i-d\mathbf1_{y_i\ge d})>0\).

\paragraph{Proof of both costs, with the moment compensations retained.}
For a parameter \(z\ge1\), put
\[
 b=-\frac{(z-d)^2}{2z-d}<0,\qquad c=2z+b-d>d.
\]
The positive packet is
\[
 Q_+(y)=y^3-(2z+b)y^2+(z^2+2bz)y-bz^2\mathbf1_{y\ge d}.
\]
For \(0\le y<d\) it equals \(y(y-d)(y-c)\ge0\); for \(y\ge d\) it equals
\((y-z)^2(y-b)\ge0\).  The denominator \(2z-d\) is positive, and
\[
 c-d=\frac{(z-d)(3z-d)}{2z-d}>0.
\]
The negative packet is
\[
 Q_-(y)=y^3-(2z+d)y^2+(z^2+2dz)y-dz^2\mathbf1_{y\ge d}.
\]
On the selected side it equals \((y-d)(y-z)^2\ge0\).  On the unselected
side it equals \(y\{(y-d)(y-2z)+z^2\}\ge0\), since both factors in the
product are nonpositive.

The exact finite summation identity, for arbitrary real \(p,q,r\), is
\begin{align*}
 &\sum_{i\in S}a_i
    (y_i^3-py_i^2+qy_i-r\mathbf1_{y_i\ge d})\\
 &\quad=E-(p-q-1)M-rK
      +(2-p)\left(\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i^2-M\right)
      =E-(p-q-1)M-rK.
\end{align*}
Thus neither threshold subtraction nor either moment contribution is
discarded.  For the positive packet this gives
\[
 E\ge (2z+b-z^2-2bz-1)M+bz^2K.
\]
When \(K\le M\), the root \(z=z_+\) is at least one and satisfies
\(Kz(z-d)=(1-d)M\).  The exact algebraic difference between the right side
and \(M(1-d)(z-1)\), multiplied by \(2z-d\), is
\[
 z(z-d)\{(1-d)M-Kz(z-d)\}=0.
\]
This proves the first branch of \eqref{eq:nonlinear-threshold-cost}.

For the negative packet the summed bound is
\[
 E\ge(2z+d-z^2-2dz-1)M+dz^2K.
\]
When \(K\ge M\), choose \(z=M(1-d)/(M-dK)\ge1\).  It satisfies
\(dzK=M(z-1+d)\), and the difference between the right side and
\(dz(K-M)\) is exactly
\[
 (z-1)\{dzK-M(z-1+d)\}=0.
\]
The second branch follows, including the balanced value at \(K=M\).

\paragraph{Theorem (the surviving gain at the installed threshold).}
Now set \(d=\delta\), \(\gamma=\Gamma\), and \(\beta=\beta_+\).  On
\(M>0\), \(K>0\), \(dK<M\), one has the exact identity
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal F_d(M,K)=\mathcal C(M,M-K)+G(M,K),\qquad G\ge0,
 \qquad K\ne M\Longrightarrow G>0,
 \label{eq:nonlinear-threshold-exact-gain}
\end{equation}
where \(\mathcal C\) is the full directional quadratic cost already proved.
The gain is explicit.  For \(K\le M\), with \(z=z_+\),
\[
 R(z)=\frac{(1-d)z(z^2-d^2)}{(z+1-d)^2},\qquad
 G(M,K)=\frac{(R(z)-\beta)(M-K)^2}{M}.
\]
For \(U=K-M>0\),
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal F_d(M,K)=dU+\gamma\frac{U^2}{M}
   +\frac{d^3U^3}{(1-d)M(M-dK)},\qquad
 G(M,K)=\frac{d^3U^3}{(1-d)M(M-dK)}>0.
 \label{eq:nonlinear-threshold-negative-gain}
\end{equation}

\paragraph{Proof of the exact gain.}
The threshold equation gives
\(\gamma=d^2/(1-d)\) and
\(\beta(2-d)^2=(1-d)(1-d^2)\).  Direct division proves
\eqref{eq:nonlinear-threshold-negative-gain} with its positive denominator.
For the positive branch the root relation gives
\[
 M=\frac{Kz(z-d)}{1-d},\qquad
 M-K=\frac{K(z-1)(z+1-d)}{1-d},\qquad
 \mathcal F_d(M,K)-d(M-K)=K(z-1)^2(z+d).
\]
Consequently
\(M\{\mathcal F_d(M,K)-d(M-K)\}=R(z)(M-K)^2\), proving its gain formula.
Finally, put
\[
 P(z)=(1-d)z^2+(1-d-\beta)z+\beta(1-d)^2.
\]
Since \(1-d-\beta=2d(1-2d^2)>0\), one has \(P(z)>0\) for \(z\ge1\).
The exact factorization is
\[
 R(z)-\beta=\frac{(z-1)P(z)}{(z+1-d)^2}.
\]
For \(K<M\), the root relation forces \(z>1\), so this gain is strictly
positive.  At \(K=M\) its factor \((M-K)^2\) vanishes.  This proves
\eqref{eq:nonlinear-threshold-exact-gain} on both signs.  The higher-order
gain is compatible with the previously proved sharpness of both quadratic
coefficients.

\paragraph{Theorem (complete actual signed consumer).}
For a finite same-height reflection-closed actual strip cluster, retain
\(|T|\ge2\), \(N>0\), \(M_1>0\), \(t=Q/M_1\),
\(\kappa=M_1^2/Q\), and its actual even threshold order \(K\).
The normalized coordinates \(y_i=x_i/t\),
\(x_i=(\operatorname{Re}\rho_i-1/2)^2\), satisfy both moment constraints
with mass \(\kappa\).  In particular \(K>0\) and \(dK<\kappa\).
The full moment-ratio charge remains
\(\mathcal B_2=gN+\Phi_{\rm sb}(t)M_1\), with the same complete fixed
rational profile \(\Phi_{\rm sb}\) as above.  One has
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal B_2+\tau t^3\mathcal F_d(\kappa,K)
 =\mathcal B_2+\tau t^3\mathcal C(\kappa,\kappa-K)
       +\tau t^3G(\kappa,K)\le gB(T).
 \label{eq:nonlinear-threshold-actual-signed}
\end{equation}
The added charge is strictly positive whenever \(\kappa\ne K\).

\paragraph{Proof of the actual transfer.}
Normalization gives the exact energy identity
\[
 \sum_{i\in S}a_i x_i(x_i-t)^2
   =t^3\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i(y_i-1)^2
   \ge t^3\mathcal F_d(\kappa,K).
\]
The unchanged full kernel ledger, its proved remainder floor \(\tau>0\),
and the profile upper bound then give
\(\mathcal B_2+\tau t^3\mathcal F_d(\kappa,K)\le gB(T)\).
Substitution of \eqref{eq:nonlinear-threshold-exact-gain} proves
\eqref{eq:nonlinear-threshold-actual-signed}; positivity of \(t\), \(\tau\),
and \(G\) proves its strict increment.  The fixed signed profile and all
moment compensations have remained coupled throughout.

The fifty-one new theorem bodies are implemented in
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdMomentPackets.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdNonlinearCost.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdNonlinearComparison.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdNonlinearTransfer.lean}, and
\texttt{Assembly/CarmichaelNonlinearThreshold.lean}.
This is an explicit improvement of the complete finite-cluster observable
bound on every nonzero discrepancy.  It does not by itself evaluate a new
numerical simplicity window or improve the certified \(7/8\) boundary.
The hypothesis \(M_1>0\) remains essential to this normalization; clusters
supported entirely on the critical line still require a separate observable
estimate for their individual multiplicities.

\subsection{An arithmetic band gap beyond the real-weight moment relaxation}
\label{sec:threshold-integer-band}

The preceding nonlinear costs use real weights.  Reflection supplies an
additional restriction on actual clusters: the total order in any symmetric
band separated from the critical line is even.  Applying that restriction to
an interior band gives a computable extra cost.  The argument uses a coupled
tilt of the full cubic packet; it does not separately estimate its two sides.
The general convex-moment method is classical, as cited above.  Priority for
this particular arithmetic refinement is not asserted beyond the portfolio
comparison recorded with the proof.

\paragraph{Theorem (integer band separation).}
Retain the finite equal-moment profile, mass \(M>0\), selected mass \(K\),
energy \(E\), and \(0<d<1\) from
\eqref{eq:nonlinear-threshold-cost}.  Suppose also that
\[
 L=\sum_{i\in S}a_i\mathbf1_{d/2\le y_i<d}\in2\mathbb N.
\]
For \(K\le M\), define
\[
 z=z_+,\quad b=-\frac{(z-d)^2}{2z-d},\quad c=2z+b-d,\quad
 p=M-Kz,\quad A=\frac d2(c-d),\quad C=d-b,\quad B=K/C.
\]
Then \(p\ge0\) and \(A,C,B>0\).  Put
\[
 n=\left\lfloor\frac{p}{2d}\right\rfloor,\qquad
 \eta=\min\{p-2dn,\,2d(n+1)-p\},
\]
and define
\begin{equation}
 J_d(M,K)=
 \begin{cases}
 \eta^2/B,&K\le M,\ 2\eta\le AB,\\
 A\eta-A^2B/4,&K\le M,\ 2\eta>AB,\\
 0,&K>M.
 \end{cases}
 \label{eq:threshold-integer-band-cost}
\end{equation}
Every displayed denominator is positive on its stated branch.  One has
\begin{equation}
 E\ge\mathcal F_d(M,K)+J_d(M,K),\qquad J_d(M,K)\ge0.
 \label{eq:threshold-integer-band-bound}
\end{equation}
For \(K\le M\), the correction is strictly positive whenever
\(p\notin2d\mathbb N\).  In particular \(0<p<2d\) suffices.
No integrality or parity is assumed for an individual weight.

\paragraph{Proof of the coupled packet estimate.}
For the positive packet already proved above, the exact defect identity is
\[
 \Delta=E-\mathcal F_d(M,K)=\sum_{i\in S}a_iQ_+(y_i).
\]
The root relation gives
\[
 p=\frac{Kz(z-1)}{1-d}\ge0.
\]
Write \(I(y)=\mathbf1_{y\ge d}\) and
\(H(y)=\mathbf1_{d/2\le y<d}\).  Below the threshold,
\(Q_+(y)=y(d-y)(c-y)\).  If \(y<d/2\), then
\((d-y)(c-y)\ge(d/2)(c-d)=A\), so \(Q_+(y)\ge Ay\).
If \(d/2\le y<d\), then \(y(c-y)\ge A\), so
\(Q_+(y)\ge A(d-y)\).  Together these say
\[
 Q_+(y)\ge A|y-dH(y)|\qquad(0\le y<d).
\]
On the selected side, its exact factorization gives
\[
 Q_+(y)=(y-z)^2(y-b)\ge C(y-z)^2\qquad(y\ge d).
\]
For either \(s=1\) or \(s=-1\), and every \(0\le\lambda\le A\),
these two estimates prove the single pointwise inequality
\begin{equation}
 Q_+(y)+\frac{\lambda^2}{4C}I(y)
 -\lambda s\{y-zI(y)-dH(y)\}\ge0.
 \label{eq:threshold-integer-band-tilt}
\end{equation}
Indeed, below the threshold use \(\lambda|y-dH(y)|\le Q_+(y)\).
Above it, retain the cross term and complete the square exactly:
\[
 C(y-z)^2-\lambda s(y-z)+\frac{\lambda^2}{4C}
 =\frac{\{C(y-z)-\lambda s/2\}^2}{C}\ge0.
\]
Multiply \eqref{eq:threshold-integer-band-tilt} by \(a_i\), sum over
\(S\), and use the exact identities for \(M,K,L,\Delta\).  Choosing
\(s\) according to the sign of \(p-dL\) gives
\begin{equation}
 \lambda|p-dL|\le\Delta+B\lambda^2/4
 \qquad(0\le\lambda\le A).
 \label{eq:threshold-integer-band-joint}
\end{equation}
Thus all moment and threshold compensation terms remain present in the
same signed expression.

\paragraph{Proof of the arithmetic cost.}
The floor cell is \(2dn\le p<2d(n+1)\).  For every natural \(m\),
if \(m\le n\) the distance \(|p-2dm|\) is at least \(p-2dn\);
if \(m\ge n+1\) it is at least \(2d(n+1)-p\).  Hence
\(0\le\eta\le|p-2dm|\) for every \(m\).  Since \(L=2m\),
\eqref{eq:threshold-integer-band-joint} gives
\[
 \Delta\ge\lambda\eta-B\lambda^2/4\qquad(0\le\lambda\le A).
\]
When \(2\eta\le AB\), choose \(\lambda=2\eta/B\); otherwise choose
\(\lambda=A\).  These admissible choices give precisely the first two
branches of \eqref{eq:threshold-integer-band-cost}.  The first is positive
for \(\eta>0\); in the second branch,
\(A\eta-A^2B/4>A^2B/4>0\).  The floor cell gives \(\eta>0\) away
from \(2d\mathbb N\).  The branch \(K>M\) is the already proved
nonlinear inequality with zero added correction.  This proves
\eqref{eq:threshold-integer-band-bound} on the complete stated domain.

\paragraph{The correction is nonvacuous on feasible even weights.}
For every \(0<d<1/2\), put
\[
 u=d/2,\qquad v=\frac{1+\sqrt{1+4u(1-u)}}2>1.
\]
The two atoms with weights \((2,2)\) satisfy
\(u^2+v^2=u+v\), \(M=2(u+v)>2\), \(K=2\), and \(L=2\).
For their threshold root \(z\), the proved identity for \(p\) gives
\(p>0\).  Moreover,
\[
 \{z-(u+v-d)\}\{z+u+v-2d\}=-2(u-d)(v-d)>0.
\]
The second factor is positive, so \(z>u+v-d\), which gives \(p<2d\).
Thus \(J_d(M,2)>0\).  The Lean existence theorem establishes this
family uniformly in \(d\), together with its exact moment identity and
strict correction.  These are finite moment models, not asserted
realizations as actual zeta zero clusters.

\paragraph{Theorem (complete actual signed arithmetic gain).}
For the actual reflected same-height strip clusters in
\eqref{eq:nonlinear-threshold-actual-signed}, retain every hypothesis,
including \(M_1>0\).  The total order in the normalized interior band
\(d/2\le x_i/t<d\) is even.  At \(d=\delta\), one has
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal B_2+\tau t^3\mathcal C(\kappa,\kappa-K)
  +\tau t^3G(\kappa,K)+\tau t^3J_d(\kappa,K)\le gB(T).
 \label{eq:threshold-integer-band-actual}
\end{equation}
Its last term is strictly positive on the positive branch whenever the
canonical lattice gap is positive.

\paragraph{Proof of parity and the actual consumer.}
Filter the original finite cluster by the displayed band.  The filter
preserves reflection because its coordinate is
\((\operatorname{Re}\rho-1/2)^2/t\).  An odd total order in a reflected
strip set would force a critical-line point, by the proved odd-order
reflection theorem.  Such a point has normalized square zero and is
excluded by \(d/2>0\).  The band order is therefore even; this proves the
required condition on its total weight without assuming even individual
orders.  Apply \eqref{eq:threshold-integer-band-bound} to the normalized
coordinates.  The exact energy rescaling, unchanged kernel ledger,
remainder floor \(\tau\), and actual profile upper bound give
\[
 \mathcal B_2+\tau t^3\{\mathcal F_d(\kappa,K)+J_d(\kappa,K)\}
 \le gB(T).
\]
Insert \eqref{eq:nonlinear-threshold-exact-gain} to obtain
\eqref{eq:threshold-integer-band-actual}.  No former signed charge has
been removed or replaced.

The twenty-eight new theorem bodies are implemented in
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdBandLattice.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdBandPackets.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdBandIntegerCost.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdBandSharpness.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdBandTransfer.lean}, and
\texttt{Assembly/CarmichaelThresholdBand.lean}.
This refines the complete finite-cluster observable bound by an explicit
arithmetic cost.  The numerical simplicity window and the certified
\(7/8\) boundary are unchanged.  Clusters entirely on the critical line
still have \(M_1=0\) and lie outside this normalization; this result does
not discharge the independent area estimate in the global simplicity
criterion.

\subsection{Exactly when even integral weights preserve the nonlinear infimum}
\label{sec:nonlinear-integer-sharpness}

The arithmetic penalty raises a further question: exactly when can even
integral profiles still approach the continuous nonlinear cost?  Its answer
is a lattice condition, with an explicit converse construction preserving
both moments and the selected order.  This determines where the gap is zero;
it does not assert an exact formula for the optimal positive gap elsewhere.

\paragraph{Theorem (complete lattice sharpness classification).}
Fix \(0<d<1\), an integer \(k>0\), and \(M\ge K=2k\).  Let \(z=z_+(d,M,K)\)
and \(p=M-Kz\).  The following assertions are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
\item For every \(\epsilon>0\), there is a finite profile with nonnegative
coordinates and weights in \(2\mathbb N\), whose two moments are exactly
\(M\), selected mass is exactly \(K\), and energy is less than
\(\mathcal F_d(M,K)+\epsilon\).
\item \(p\in2d\mathbb N\).
\end{enumerate}
On the second branch, three grouped atoms with fixed even weights
\((2m,2k,2)\) suffice, where \(p=2dm\).  Off the lattice, every admissible
profile satisfies the positive gap already proved:
\[
 E\ge\mathcal F_d(M,K)+J_d(M,K)>\mathcal F_d(M,K).
\]
Even on the lattice, if \(K<M\) no finite nonnegative real-weight profile
attains \(\mathcal F_d(M,K)\) exactly.  Thus approachability and attainment
are distinct in this classification.

The background includes classical convex moment optimization and
Chebyshev-type quadrature with prescribed equal weights.
For the latter see K.-J.~F\"orster and G.-P.~Ostermeyer,
\emph{On Weighted Chebyshev-Type Quadrature Formulas},
Math. Comp. 46 (1986), 591--599.
The specific conclusion here fixes both equal moments and threshold order
and characterizes approachability by the predicted-band lattice.  Broad
priority for that conclusion is not claimed from the limited literature check.

\paragraph{Proof of necessity.}
For weights \(a_i=2b_i\), \(b_i\in\mathbb N\), the interior-band mass is
\[
 L=2\sum_{i\in S}b_i\mathbf1_{d/2\le y_i<d}\in2\mathbb N.
\]
The integer-corrected lower bound therefore applies to every such profile.
If \(p\notin2d\mathbb N\), its gap satisfies \(J_d(M,K)>0\).  Taking
\(\epsilon=J_d(M,K)/2\) contradicts approachability.  This proof is
uniform over arbitrary finite supports and arbitrary natural weights.

\paragraph{Proof of sufficiency by exact moment preservation.}
Suppose \(p=2dm\), \(m\in\mathbb N\).  The root relation and mass identity
become
\begin{equation}
 M=2(kz+md),\qquad kz(z-1)=md(1-d),\qquad z\ge1.
 \label{eq:lattice-sharpness-constraints}
\end{equation}
For a small positive parameter \(e\), define
\begin{align*}
 u_e&=d-e,& R_e&=kz+me,& S_e&=kz^2+2mde-me^2,\\
 D_e&=k\{(k+1)S_e-R_e^2\},&
 w_e&=\frac{R_e-\sqrt{D_e}}{k+1},&
 v_e&=\frac{R_e-w_e}{k}.
\end{align*}
Both denominators are positive.  At zero,
\[
 D_0=(kz)^2>0,\qquad w_0=0,\qquad v_0=z>d.
\]
Continuity consequently gives, for all sufficiently small positive \(e\),
\(0<u_e<d\), \(D_e>0\), \(w_e<d\), and \(v_e>d\).
Nonnegativity of the residual is an additional algebraic fact, rather than
a consequence of continuity at its zero value.  Indeed,
\begin{align*}
 R_e^2-kS_e&=2km(z-d)e+m(m+k)e^2\ge0,\\
 R_e^2-D_e&=(k+1)(R_e^2-kS_e)\ge0.
\end{align*}
Since \(R_e>0\), these imply \(\sqrt{D_e}\le R_e\), hence \(w_e\ge0\).
The residual root satisfies the exact quadratic identity
\[
 (k+1)w_e^2-2R_ew_e+R_e^2-kS_e=0,
\]
so the high coordinate satisfies \(kv_e^2+w_e^2=S_e\).  Together with
\eqref{eq:lattice-sharpness-constraints}, this proves both exact moments:
\begin{align*}
 2mu_e+2kv_e+2w_e&=M,\\
 2mu_e^2+2kv_e^2+2w_e^2&=M.
\end{align*}
Only \(v_e\) is selected; its weight is exactly \(2k=K\).
Let \(E_e\) be the energy of these three grouped atoms.  It is continuous
at zero, and the exact signed identity is
\[
 E_0-M(1-d)(z-1)
 =2(z+d-2)\{kz(z-1)-md(1-d)\}=0.
\]
Thus \(E_e\to\mathcal F_d(M,K)\).  For any prescribed \(\epsilon>0\),
choose \(e>0\) small enough for all the coordinate, discriminant and
energy conditions simultaneously.  The Lean construction uses a single
continuous minimum margin for this choice, and supplies a finite profile
with the exact required moments, threshold order and energy inequality.
The case \(m=0\) is included; zero weights and zero coordinates are allowed.
This proves sufficiency.

\paragraph{Proof of finite nonattainment.}
The positive cubic packet has a simple equality condition on its whole
nonnegative domain:
\[
 Q_+(y)=0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad y=z\mathbf1_{y\ge d}.
\]
Below \(d\), both remaining factors in \(y(d-y)(c-y)\) are positive,
forcing \(y=0\).  Above \(d\), \(y-b>0\), so its square factor forces
\(y=z\).  If a finite profile attained \(\mathcal F_d\), the exact
nonnegative packet sum would be zero.  Each term of positive weight would
therefore satisfy that equality condition.  Summing it gives \(M=zK\).
Substituting into the root relation gives
\(Kz(z-1)=0\), hence \(z=1\).  But \(K<M\) forces \(z>1\), a
contradiction.  Therefore \(E>\mathcal F_d(M,K)\) on that branch, for
all finite nonnegative real-weight profiles, without any parity assumption.

\paragraph{Theorem (strict complete actual signed consumer).}
Retain every actual-cluster hypothesis in
\eqref{eq:nonlinear-threshold-actual-signed}, and assume \(K<\kappa\).
Then the complete former nonlinear consumer is strict:
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal B_2+\tau t^3\mathcal F_d(\kappa,K)
 =\mathcal B_2+\tau t^3\mathcal C(\kappa,\kappa-K)
   +\tau t^3G(\kappa,K)<gB(T),\qquad d=\delta.
 \label{eq:lattice-sharpness-actual-strict}
\end{equation}
Normalize exactly as before.  The finite strict energy inequality, multiplied
by \(t^3>0\) and \(\tau>0\), remains strict.  The unchanged signed kernel
ledger, remainder floor and actual profile upper bound preserve it through
to \eqref{eq:lattice-sharpness-actual-strict}.  Thus the lattice zero-gap
cases cannot be mistaken for finite equality cases.  No other signed charge
has been removed.

The twenty-four new theorem bodies are implemented in
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdLatticeProfile.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdLatticePerturbation.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdEvenSharpness.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdNonlinearRigidity.lean}, and
\texttt{Assembly/CarmichaelThresholdSharpness.lean}.
The sharpness witnesses are moment profiles, with no claim that they occur
as zeta zero clusters.  The classification resolves exactly when parity
alone leaves this nonlinear envelope sharp; it does not settle the independent
global small-value area estimate.  The certified simplicity window and
\(7/8\) boundary are unchanged, and \(M_1>0\) remains required for the
actual normalized consumer.

\subsection{Quantitative inverse stability of near-minimal threshold profiles}
\label{subsec:threshold-transport-stability}

The nonlinear cost also determines the geometry of a nearly minimizing
profile.  The following estimate controls every Lipschitz test statistic,
without a factor depending on the total weight.  It complements the earlier
band-count estimate, whose discontinuous indicator is outside this test class.

\paragraph{Theorem (Quantitative inverse profile theorem).}
\label{thm:threshold-transport-stability}
Let \(I\) be finite, \(a_i,y_i\ge0\), and
\[
 N=\sum_{i\in I}a_i,\qquad
 M=\sum_{i\in I}a_i y_i=\sum_{i\in I}a_i y_i^2>0,\qquad
 K=\sum_{\substack{i\in I\\y_i\ge d}}a_i\le M,\qquad 0<d<1.
\]
Define
\[
 z=\frac{d+\sqrt{d^2+4(1-d)M/K}}2,\quad
 b=-\frac{(z-d)^2}{2z-d},\quad
 S=\frac d2(2z+b-2d),\quad C=d-b,
\]
and
\[
 E=\sum_{i\in I}a_i y_i(y_i-1)^2,\quad
 F=M(1-d)(z-1),\quad \Delta=E-F,\quad
 L=\frac{M-Kz}{d}.
\]
Then \(K,S,C>0\) and \(\Delta\ge0\).  For every globally
\(1\)-Lipschitz function \(\varphi:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R\), put
\[
 T_\varphi=N\varphi(0)+L\{\varphi(d)-\varphi(0)\}
                   +K\{\varphi(z)-\varphi(0)\}.
\]
For every \(0\le\ell\le S\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:threshold-transport-tilt}
 \ell\left|\sum_{i\in I}a_i\varphi(y_i)-T_\varphi\right|
 \le 2\Delta+\frac{K\ell^2}{2C}.
\end{equation}
Consequently,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:threshold-transport-inverse}
 \left|\sum_{i\in I}a_i\varphi(y_i)-T_\varphi\right|
 \le
 \begin{cases}
  2\sqrt{K\Delta/C},&\Delta\le KS^2/(4C),\\
  2\Delta/S+KS/(2C),&\Delta>KS^2/(4C).
 \end{cases}
\end{equation}
If \(K<M\), then \(L>0\) and \(N-K-L>0\).  Thus \(T_\varphi\)
is the statistic of the explicit positive three-atom measure
\[
 \mu_*=(N-K-L)\delta_0+L\delta_d+K\delta_z.
\]


\paragraph{Proof.}
Use the complete positive cubic packet \(Q_{d,z}\) established above.
Its exact moment ledger is
\[
 \sum_{i\in I}a_iQ_{d,z}(y_i)=E-F=\Delta.
\]
In particular no cutoff or moment-compensation term is omitted.
Subtract the affine interpolant of \(\varphi\) at \(0,d\):
\[
 \Psi(y)=\varphi(y)-\varphi(0)
                 -\frac{y}{d}\{\varphi(d)-\varphi(0)\}.
\]
The function \(\Psi\) vanishes at \(0,d\) and is \(2\)-Lipschitz.
Write \(A(y)=1_{\{y\ge d\}}\) and
\(J(y)=1_{\{d/2\le y<d\}}\).  The rounded coordinate is
\(zA(y)+dJ(y)\), so
\[
 |\Psi(y)-A(y)\Psi(z)|
 \le 2|y-zA(y)-dJ(y)|.
\]
The already proved complete tilted packet, at the two signs, gives
\[
 \ell|y-zA(y)-dJ(y)|
 \le Q_{d,z}(y)+\frac{\ell^2}{4C}A(y),
 \qquad 0\le\ell\le S.
\]
Multiplication by \(a_i\), summation, and the exact defect ledger
therefore yield
\[
 \ell\left|\sum_{i\in I}a_i\{\Psi(y_i)-A(y_i)\Psi(z)\}\right|
 \le2\Delta+\frac{K\ell^2}{2C}.
\]
Expanding \(\Psi\) identifies the sum on the left with
\(\sum_{i\in I}a_i\varphi(y_i)-T_\varphi\), proving
\eqref{eq:threshold-transport-tilt}.

For the atom weights, the root relation
\(Kz(z-d)=(1-d)M\) and \(K<M\) imply \(z>1\) and
\((1-d)(M-Kz)=Kz(z-1)>0\), hence \(L>0\).
The complete fixed-total packet
\(T_{d,z}(y)=(y-z)^2-(d-z)^2(1-A(y))\) has the exact identity
\[
 \sum_{i\in I}a_i T_{d,z}(y_i)
 =(2z-d)\{d(N-K)-M+Kz\}.
\]
Its strict positivity, because \(K<N\), proves \(N-K-L>0\).
Finally divide \eqref{eq:threshold-transport-tilt} by \(\ell>0\).
The unconstrained minimizing choice is
\(\ell=2\sqrt{C\Delta/K}\); use this when it is at most \(S\),
and use \(\ell=S\) otherwise.  At \(\Delta=0\) let
\(\ell\downarrow0\).  This proves \eqref{eq:threshold-transport-inverse}.
\hfill\(\square\)

The atom at \(d\) is a boundary atom approached from the unselected
side.  When \(K<M\), the limiting measure \(\mu_*\) itself has selected
weight \(K+L\), rather than \(K\), under the closed cutoff \(y\ge d\).
Thus the inverse theorem is consistent with finite nonattainment.
For fixed \(M,K,N,d\), every sequence with \(\Delta\to0\) converges
to this same three-atom measure in every Lipschitz test statistic.
The error estimate is unchanged by adding weight at \(y=0\).

\paragraph{Corollary (The full signed zero-cluster consumer).}
\label{cor:threshold-transport-zero-budget}
Let \(W\) be a finite set in the open critical strip, all at the same
height \(T\), with \(|T|\ge2\), and use the actual analytic zero orders
as weights.  Assume \(N>0\), \(M_1>0\), and \(K\le\kappa\), in the
notation of the nonlinear threshold criterion.  Set
\[
 t=\frac{M_2}{M_1},\qquad \kappa=\frac{M_1^2}{M_2},\qquad
 y_w=\frac{(\Re w-\tfrac12)^2}{t},\qquad d=\delta,
\]
and form \(z,S,C,T_\varphi\) from \(M=\kappa\) and the actual
threshold order \(K\).  With the complete signed charge \(B_2\) and
the existing budget \(gB\), put
\[
 \mathcal R=\frac{gB-B_2}{\tau t^3}-F_d(\kappa,K).
\]
Then, for every globally \(1\)-Lipschitz \(\varphi\) and
\(0\le\ell\le S\),
\[
 \ell\left|\sum_{w\in W}m(w)\varphi(y_w)-T_\varphi\right|
 \le2\mathcal R+\frac{K\ell^2}{2C}.
\]

\paragraph{Proof.}
Normalization gives equal first and second moments \(\kappa\).
The complete weighted kernel ledger, its proved remainder floor
\(\tau\), and the actual horizontal-profile upper bound give
\[
 B_2+\tau t^3 E\le gB.
\]
Since \(\tau t^3>0\), this implies \(\Delta\le\mathcal R\).
Substitute that inequality into
\eqref{eq:threshold-transport-tilt}.  The full signed term \(B_2\)
is retained throughout.
\hfill\(\square\)

The general dual-polynomial approach to moment inequalities is classical;
see Bertsimas and Popescu, \emph{Optimal Inequalities in Probability
Theory: A Convex Optimization Approach}, SIAM J.\ Optim.\ 15 (2005),
780--804, doi:10.1137/S1052623401399903.  The result here is the explicit
inverse estimate for this cutoff cost, including its boundary atom and
the full signed zero-cluster application.  No global priority claim is
made for the abstract method.

\subsection{The negative branch: exact extremizers and a finite exceptional spectrum}
\label{sec:negative-integer-phase}

The negative branch has a different attainment geometry.  Both zeros of its
selected packet, \(d\) and \(z_-\), belong to the selected set.  Consequently
its arithmetic sharp cases are exact finite extremizers.  The same calculation
also supplies the missing negative-branch charge in the full signed bound.

\paragraph{Theorem (negative arithmetic penalty).}
Retain the finite nonnegative equal-moment profile with positive mass \(M\),
selected mass \(K\), energy \(E\), and \(0<d<1\).  Suppose \(M<K\), and put
\[
 z=z_- =\frac{M(1-d)}{M-dK}\ge1,\quad g=z-d>0,\quad
 p=M-dK>0,\quad h=\frac{d+z}{2},\quad A=\frac{g^2}{4},\quad C=\frac g2,\quad B=K/C.
\]
The previously proved domain gives \(K>0\) and \(dK<M\), so every denominator
is positive.  Suppose that the upper selected mass
\(L=\sum_{i\in S}a_i\mathbf1_{y_i\ge h}\) is even integral.  Define
\[
 n=\left\lfloor\frac p{2g}\right\rfloor,\qquad
 \eta_- =\min\{p-2gn,\,2g(n+1)-p\},
\]
and
\begin{equation}
 J^-_d(M,K)=
 \begin{cases}
 \eta_-^2/B,&M<K,\ 2\eta_-\le AB,\\
 A\eta_--A^2B/4,&M<K,\ 2\eta_->AB,\\
 0,&K\le M.
 \end{cases}
 \label{eq:negative-integer-cost}
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}
 E\ge\mathcal F_d(M,K)+J^-_d(M,K),\qquad J^-_d(M,K)\ge0.
 \label{eq:negative-integer-energy}
\end{equation}
On the negative branch the added cost is positive whenever
\(p\notin2g\mathbb N\).

\paragraph{Proof with both threshold compensations retained.}
The installed negative packet gives the exact defect
\[
 \Delta=E-\mathcal F_d(M,K)=\sum_{i\in S}a_iQ_-(y_i).
\]
Write \(I_d(y)=\mathbf1_{y\ge d}\) and \(H(y)=\mathbf1_{y\ge h}\).
Below \(d\), its factorization gives
\[
 Q_-(y)=y\{(y-d)(y-2z)+z^2\}\ge z^2y\ge Ay.
\]
For \(d\le y<h\), one has \((y-z)^2\ge g^2/4=A\), hence
\(Q_-(y)\ge A(y-d)\).  Thus throughout \(0\le y<h\),
\[
 Q_-(y)\ge A\{y-dI_d(y)\},\qquad y-dI_d(y)\ge0.
\]
For \(y\ge h\), the selected factorization instead gives
\(Q_-(y)=(y-d)(y-z)^2\ge C(y-z)^2\).
For either sign \(s=\pm1\) and every \(0\le\lambda\le A\), these bounds
combine into the single nonnegative packet
\begin{equation}
 Q_-(y)+\frac{\lambda^2}{4C}H(y)
  -\lambda s\{y-dI_d(y)-gH(y)\}\ge0.
 \label{eq:negative-integer-tilt}
\end{equation}
On the upper region its proof is the exact square completion
\[
 C(y-z)^2-\lambda s(y-z)+\frac{\lambda^2}{4C}
 =\frac{\{C(y-z)-\lambda s/2\}^2}{C}\ge0.
\]
Sum \eqref{eq:negative-integer-tilt} with weights \(a_i\) and choose the
sign of \(p-gL\).  Since \(h>d\), the upper count satisfies \(0\le L\le K\).
Only the explicitly quadratic compensation is enlarged in the following bound:
\begin{equation}
 \lambda|p-gL|\le\Delta+\frac{L}{C}\frac{\lambda^2}{4}
 \le\Delta+\frac{K}{C}\frac{\lambda^2}{4},\qquad0\le\lambda\le A.
 \label{eq:negative-integer-joint}
\end{equation}
The signed linear expression and both indicator subtractions are unchanged.
Because \(L\in2\mathbb N\), the canonical gap satisfies
\(\eta_-\le|p-gL|\).  Choosing \(\lambda=2\eta_-/B\) when this is at most
\(A\), and \(\lambda=A\) otherwise, proves
\eqref{eq:negative-integer-energy}.  The previously proved floor-cell and
penalty lemmas give nonnegativity and strict positivity off the lattice.

\paragraph{Theorem (exact negative attainment spectrum).}
Fix an integer \(k>0\), \(K=2k\), and \(dK<M<K\).  Among finite profiles
with weights in \(2\mathbb N\), the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
\item With both moments fixed at \(M\) and selected order fixed at \(K\),
the continuous nonlinear cost is approachable to every positive tolerance.
\item Some such finite profile attains that cost exactly.
\item \(M-dK\in2(z_--d)\mathbb N\).
\item For an integer \(1\le\ell<k\),
\begin{equation}
 M=M_\ell(d,k):=2d(k-\ell)+\ell+
 \sqrt{\ell^2+4\ell(k-\ell)d(1-d)}.
 \label{eq:negative-exceptional-spectrum}
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
Thus the exceptional masses are given by a finite explicit list.  In every
sharp case, the two atoms at \(d,z_-\), with weights
\((2(k-\ell),2\ell)\), are exact extremizers.  No realization of these
moment profiles as actual zeta clusters is asserted.

\paragraph{Proof of the classification and finite list.}
Even natural weights force every upper threshold mass to be even integral.
If the lattice condition failed, the strictly positive penalty in
\eqref{eq:negative-integer-energy} would contradict approachability by
taking half that penalty as the tolerance.  Exact attainment implies
approachability directly.

Conversely suppose \(p=2g\ell\).  Positivity of \(p,g\) forces \(\ell>0\).
If \(\ell\ge k\), then
\(M=dK+2g\ell\ge dK+gK=zK\ge K\), contradicting \(M<K\).
Hence \(0<\ell<k\), and
\[
 2(k-\ell)d+2\ell z=M.
\]
The negative root identity \(dzK=M(z-1+d)\) then gives
\[
 2(k-\ell)d^2+2\ell z^2=M.
\]
Both atoms are selected, with total weight \(K\).  Their negative packets
vanish exactly, so the summed defect is zero and their energy equals
\(\mathcal F_d(M,K)\).  This proves all three logical implications.

Subtracting the two exact moment identities gives
\(\ell z(z-1)=(k-\ell)d(1-d)\).  Therefore
\[
 (2\ell z-\ell)^2=\ell^2+4\ell(k-\ell)d(1-d).
\]
Since \(z\ge1\) and \(\ell>0\), the left square has a nonnegative base;
taking its square root proves \eqref{eq:negative-exceptional-spectrum}.
In the reverse direction, that explicit mass identity gives
\[
 (M-dK)^2=2\ell\{M(1-d)-d(M-dK)\}.
\]
The exact identity \((M-dK)z=M(1-d)\), with \(M-dK>0\), reduces this
to \(M-dK=2\ell(z-d)\), proving the converse without selecting an
extraneous quadratic root.

Together with the preceding positive-branch theorem, this is a complete
approachability phase diagram for \(dK<M\) and fixed even \(K\).
For \(K<M\), the sharp lattice profiles are limiting profiles and finite
equality is impossible.  For \(M<K\), sharpness is equivalent to finite
attainment at the two selected boundary points.  The balanced case remains
covered by the preceding positive-branch criterion.

\paragraph{Theorem (complete two-sided actual signed bound).}
Retain the finite same-height reflected strip-cluster hypotheses, including
\(|T|\ge2\), \(N>0\), and \(M_1>0\).  Write \(J^+_d\) for the previous
positive-branch integer correction, and set
\(J_d=J^+_d+J^-_d\).  At the installed threshold \(d=\delta\), one has
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal B_2+\tau t^3\mathcal F_d(\kappa,K)+\tau t^3J_d(\kappa,K)
 =\mathcal B_2+\tau t^3\mathcal C(\kappa,\kappa-K)
     +\tau t^3G(\kappa,K)+\tau t^3J_d(\kappa,K)\le gB(T).
 \label{eq:two-sided-integer-actual}
\end{equation}
If \(\kappa<K=2k\) and \(\kappa\) avoids the finite list
\eqref{eq:negative-exceptional-spectrum}, the new negative-branch term
\(\tau t^3J^-_d(\kappa,K)\) is strictly positive.

\paragraph{Proof of the actual transfer.}
For any positive cutoff \(h\), filter the actual cluster by
\(h\le(\operatorname{Re}\rho-1/2)^2/t\).  The filter preserves reflection
and excludes the critical line.  An odd total order would therefore
contradict the proved odd-order reflection theorem.  Its total order is
even; no evenness assumption on individual zero orders is used.
Apply this to \(h=(d+z_-)/2>0\).  The negative integer-cost theorem then
applies to the normalized coordinates whenever \(\kappa<K\).  Exact
energy rescaling, the unchanged full kernel ledger, its floor \(\tau\),
and the actual profile upper bound preserve the entire cost.  On the other
branch, use the installed positive integer-cost consumer.  The inactive
correction is zero in each case, giving
\eqref{eq:two-sided-integer-actual}.  Finally, the finite-list equivalence
implies a positive canonical gap off that list, so the compiled positivity
consumer gives the stated strict charge.

The thirty-three new theorem bodies are implemented in
\texttt{Helpers/NegativeThresholdPackets.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/NegativeThresholdIntegerCost.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/NegativeThresholdSharpness.lean},
