\texttt{Helpers/NegativeThresholdTransfer.lean}, and
\texttt{Assembly/CarmichaelNegativeInteger.lean}.
The result supplies a new quantitative term on the branch where the earlier
integer correction vanished, and an exact attainment classification there.
Classical moment duality and equal-weight quadrature remain the background
cited above; the specific compensated-threshold phase diagram and full
signed application are the conclusions here.  Broad priority is not asserted
from the limited literature check.  The numerical simplicity window and
certified \(7/8\) boundary are unchanged.  Clusters supported entirely on
the critical line still require the independent small-value area estimate.

\subsection{Quantitative inverse stability on the negative branch}
\label{subsec:negative-threshold-transport}

The negative packet also controls the whole profile, with an admissible
cutoff atom.  We work first with arbitrary nonnegative real weights;
the previously established integer restrictions remain additional constraints.

\paragraph{Theorem (negative-branch Lipschitz inverse).}
Let \(S\) be finite, \(a_i,y_i\ge0\), \(0<d<1\), and
\[
 N=\sum_{i\in S}a_i,\qquad
 M=\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i=\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i^2>0,\qquad
 K=\sum_{\substack{i\in S\\y_i\ge d}}a_i>M.
\]
The moment domain gives \(dK<M\).  Put
\[
 z=\frac{M(1-d)}{M-dK},\qquad
 s_-=\frac{(z-d)^2}{4},\qquad c_-=\frac{z-d}{2},
\]
\[
 F_-=\frac{dM(1-d)(K-M)}{M-dK},\qquad
 \Delta_-=\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i(y_i-1)^2-F_-\ge0.
\]
Then \(z\ge1\), \(s_-,c_->0\), and the measure
\[
 \mu_*=(N-K)\delta_0+
       \frac{Kz-M}{z-d}\delta_d+
       \frac{M-dK}{z-d}\delta_z
\]
has nonnegative weights, with both selected weights positive.
It has total mass \(N\), selected mass \(K\), first and second
moments \(M\), and energy \(F_-\).
For every globally \(1\)-Lipschitz
\(\varphi:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R\), write
\(T_\varphi=\int\varphi\,d\mu_*\).  For every \(0\le\ell\le s_-\),
\[
 \ell\left|\sum_{i\in S}a_i\varphi(y_i)-T_\varphi\right|
 \le 2\Delta_-+\frac{K\ell^2}{2c_-}.
\]
Consequently,
\[
 \left|\sum_{i\in S}a_i\varphi(y_i)-T_\varphi\right|
 \le
 \begin{cases}
  2\sqrt{K\Delta_-/c_-},
      &\Delta_-\le Ks_-^2/(4c_-),\\[2pt]
  2\Delta_-/s_-+Ks_-/(2c_-),
      &\Delta_->Ks_-^2/(4c_-).
 \end{cases}
\]
There is no total-mass factor \(N\) in this error estimate.

\paragraph{Proof.}
Write \(I(y)=1_{\{y\ge d\}}\),
\(J(y)=1_{\{y\ge(d+z)/2\}}\), and
\[
 m_\varphi=\frac{\varphi(z)-\varphi(d)}{z-d},\qquad
 r_\varphi(y)=\varphi(y)-\varphi(0)-ym_\varphi
 -I(y)\bigl(\varphi(d)-\varphi(0)-dm_\varphi\bigr).
\]
Since \(|m_\varphi|\le1\), subtraction of this affine function
has Lipschitz constant at most \(2\).  At the anchors \(0,d,z\)
the residual vanishes.  Choosing the anchor \(0\) for \(y<d\),
\(d\) for \(d\le y<(d+z)/2\), and \(z\) above that midpoint gives
\[
 |r_\varphi(y)|
 \le2|y-dI(y)-(z-d)J(y)|.
\]
The complete negative packet is
\[
 Q_-(y)=y^3-(2z+d)y^2+(z^2+2dz)y-dz^2I(y).
\]
On the selected set it equals \((y-d)(y-z)^2\).
For \(0\le y<d\), its unselected expression is
\[
 Q_-(y)=y\bigl((y-z)^2+d(2z-y)\bigr)\ge z^2y\ge s_-y.
\]
For \(d\le y<(d+z)/2\), \(Q_-(y)\ge s_-(y-d)\);
above the midpoint, \(Q_-(y)\ge c_-(y-z)^2\).
These are the already proved negative coercivity inequalities.
Completing the square in the upper region, and using
\(0\le\ell\le s_-\) below it, proves for both \(\sigma=1,-1\)
\[
 Q_-(y)+\frac{\ell^2}{4c_-}J(y)
 -\ell\sigma\bigl(y-dI(y)-(z-d)J(y)\bigr)\ge0.
\]
Thus
\[
 \ell |r_\varphi(y)|
 \le2Q_-(y)+\frac{\ell^2}{2c_-}J(y).
\]
The exact root relation \(dzK=M(z-1+d)\), together with the two
moment equalities, gives
\(\sum_{i\in S}a_iQ_-(y_i)=\Delta_-\).
The residual sum is exactly
\[
 \sum_{i\in S}a_ir_\varphi(y_i)
 =\sum_{i\in S}a_i\varphi(y_i)
 -\left[N\varphi(0)+K(\varphi(d)-\varphi(0))
 +(M-dK)m_\varphi\right].
\]
The bracket is \(T_\varphi\).
Sum the pointwise inequality with nonnegative weights and use
\(\sum_{i\in S}a_iJ(y_i)\le K\) to obtain the asserted tilt.

For completeness, the tilt optimization is a scalar statement now shared
by both branches in Lean.  If \(x\ge0\) satisfies
\(\ell x\le2e+k\ell^2/(2c)\) for \(0\le\ell\le s\), with
\(e\ge0\) and \(k,c,s>0\), then \(e\le ks^2/(4c)\) first implies
\(cx\le ks\) by taking \(\ell=s\).
The admissible choice \(\ell=cx/k\) gives \(cx^2\le4ke\).
In the other regime, \(\ell=s\) gives the stated linear bound.

Finally \(K\le N\), and \(K>M\), \(z\ge1\) imply \(Kz-M>0\);
\(M-dK>0\) supplies the other positive weight.
Their sum is \(K\), their first moment is \(M\), and the root relation
gives their second moment \(M\) and energy \(F_-\).
All coordinates \(d,z\) are selected under the closed cutoff.
This verifies admissibility and attainment for the real-weight problem.
\hfill\(\square\)

In particular, at fixed \(d,M,K,N\), energy excess tending to zero forces
convergence of every Lipschitz statistic to this single explicit profile.
At zero excess all these statistics agree exactly.  This complements the
positive branch, where the cutoff atom is approached from the unselected
side and fails the closed selected-mass constraint at the limit.
It does not replace the separate discontinuous band-count estimates,
nor remove the integer-lattice conditions for actual order weights.

\paragraph{Corollary (complete signed zero-cluster consumer).}
Let \(W\) be a finite cluster in \(0<\Re w<1\) at one height \(T\),
\(|T|\ge2\), with weights \(a_w=\operatorname{ord}_w\zeta\).
Write \(x_w=(\Re w-\tfrac12)^2\),
\(M_1=\sum_Wa_wx_w>0\), \(M_2=\sum_Wa_wx_w^2\),
\(N=\sum_Wa_w>0\), \(t=M_2/M_1\), and
\(\kappa=M_1^2/M_2\).
Use the proved threshold \(d=\texttt{momentRoundThreshold}\) and let
\(K=\sum_{x_w/t\ge d}a_w>\kappa\).
The normalized coordinates \(y_w=x_w/t\) have equal first and
second moments \(\kappa\).
With \(B_2\), \(g\mathcal B(T)\), and \(\tau>0\) denoting the
complete signed second-order charge, profile upper budget, and proved
cubic remainder floor from the preceding kernel ledger, set
\[
 \mathcal R_-=
 \frac{g\mathcal B(T)-B_2}{\tau t^3}
       -F_-(d,\kappa,K)\ge0.
\]
The same canonical profile, with \(M=\kappa\), satisfies
\[
 \ell\left|\sum_{w\in W}a_w\varphi(y_w)-T_\varphi\right|
 \le2\mathcal R_-+\frac{K\ell^2}{2c_-},
 \qquad 0\le\ell\le s_-.
\]

\paragraph{Proof.}
The full signed ledger, the cubic remainder floor and the actual profile
upper bound give
\[
 B_2+\tau t^3\sum_{w\in W}a_wy_w(y_w-1)^2
 \le g\mathcal B(T).
\]
Divide by the positive \(\tau t^3\) and subtract the proved negative
cost to obtain \(0\le\Delta_-\le\mathcal R_-\).
Substitute in the finite weighted tilt above.
In particular, the signed term \(B_2\) is retained throughout.
No reflection assumption on \(W\) is used.
\hfill\(\square\)

\subsection{The square-root inverse exponent is optimal on the negative branch}
\label{subsec:negative-threshold-rate-sharpness}

The inverse theorem can be closed in the other direction: its square-root
exponent cannot be improved using only the real-weight moment constraints.
The parameters and the total mass remain fixed throughout the construction.

\paragraph{Theorem (fixed-moment rate optimality).}
Fix
\[
 0<d<1,\qquad 0<M<K,\qquad dK<M,\qquad N\ge K.
\]
Let \(z=M(1-d)/(M-dK)\) and let \(\mu_*\) be the negative canonical
profile of the preceding subsection.  The function
\(\varphi(y)=|y-z|\) is globally \(1\)-Lipschitz.
For every \(h>1/2\), \(C\ge0\), and \(\varepsilon>0\), there is a
nonnegative measure \(\mu\) on at most four nonnegative points such that
\[
 \int1\,d\mu=N,\qquad
 \int y\,d\mu=\int y^2\,d\mu=M,\qquad
 \mu([d,\infty))=K,
\]
and, writing
\[
 \Delta=\int y(y-1)^2\,d\mu-F_-(d,M,K),\qquad
 D=\left|\int\varphi\,d\mu-\int\varphi\,d\mu_*\right|,
\]
one has
\[
 0<\Delta<\varepsilon,\qquad D>C\Delta^h.
\]
Thus no uniform local inverse estimate \(D\le C\Delta^h\), with
\(h>1/2\), holds even at fixed \(d,M,K,N\) in this moment class.

\paragraph{Proof.}
Put
\[
 g=z-d,\qquad A=M-dK,\qquad
 B=\frac{A}{g},\qquad
 \alpha=K-B=\frac{Kz-M}{g},\qquad
 \eta=\min\left\{\frac14,\frac{\alpha}{2K}\right\}.
\]
All of \(g,A,B,\alpha,\eta\) are positive: the preceding root
inequality gives \(z\ge1>d\), and \(Kz-M\ge K-M>0\).
In particular \(0<B<K\).
For \(0<q\le\eta\), define
\[
 v=1+q^2,\qquad
 u=\frac{gq^2}{v},\qquad r=\frac{gq}{v},\qquad b=Bv,
\]
\[
 \mu_q=(N-K)\delta_0+(K-b)\delta_d+
       \frac b2\delta_{z-u-r}+\frac b2\delta_{z-u+r}.
\]
These are rational functions of the parameters and \(q\); no rationality
assumption on the parameters is needed.
Since \(q\le1/4<1\) and \(q\le\alpha/(2K)\),
\[
 Bq^2\le Kq\le\alpha/2,\qquad
 K-b=\alpha-Bq^2\ge\alpha/2>0.
\]
The two moving coordinates satisfy
\[
 z-u-r-d=\frac{g(1-q)}{1+q^2}>0,\qquad
 z-u+r-d=\frac{g(1+q)}{1+q^2}>0.
\]
Hence all coordinates and weights are nonnegative, the selected mass
under the closed cutoff is exactly \(K\), and the total mass is \(N\).
The origin has zero weight when \(N=K\), which is why the assertion
allows fewer than four atoms.

We verify the full moments before estimating any term.
The first-moment identity is
\[
 (K-b)d+b(z-u)=(K-B)d+Bz=M,
\]
because \(bg-bu=Bg\).  For the second moment, the exact cancellation is
\[
 (b-B)g^2-2bzu+b(u^2+r^2)
       +2d(b-B)g=0.
\]
Indeed \(bu=Bgq^2\), \(b-B=Bq^2\), and
\(b(u^2+r^2)=Bg^2q^2\); the left side is
\(2Bgq^2(g-z+d)=0\).
It follows that
\[
 (K-b)d^2+b\bigl((z-u)^2+r^2\bigr)
       =(K-B)d^2+Bz^2=M.
\]
Thus the moving low weight compensates both high atoms exactly.

Expand the cubic energy with all three selected contributions:
\[
\begin{split}
 &(K-b)d(d-1)^2
 +\frac b2(z-u-r)(z-u-r-1)^2\\
 &\qquad+\frac b2(z-u+r)(z-u+r-1)^2\\
 &=(K-B)d(d-1)^2+Bz(z-1)^2
   +\frac{Bg^3q^2(1-q^2)}{(1+q^2)^2}.
\end{split}
\]
The first two terms are exactly \(F_-\), so
\[
 \Delta(q)=\frac{Ag^2q^2(1-q^2)}{(1+q^2)^2}>0.
\]
For the statistic, \(r>u\ge0\).  The two moving atoms therefore
contribute \(br=Aq\) to its difference from the canonical high atom.
The change in the cutoff weight contributes
\(-(b-B)g=-Aq^2\); the origin weight is unchanged.
Keeping this compensation gives the exact signed difference
\[
 \int|y-z|\,d\mu_q-\int|y-z|\,d\mu_*
       =Aq(1-q)>0.
\]
In particular
\[
 D(q)=Aq(1-q),\qquad
 0<\Delta(q)\le Ag^2q^2,\qquad D(q)\ge Aq/2>0,
\]
and hence
\[
 \Delta(q)\le L D(q)^2,\qquad L=\frac{4g^2}{A}>0.
\]

Positive deficits can be made arbitrarily small.  For any \(e>0\),
take \(q=\min\{\eta/2,e/(2Ag^2)\}\).
Then \(0<q\le\eta\), and \(q<1\) gives
\(\Delta(q)\le Ag^2q^2\le Ag^2q\le e/2<e\).

Finally fix \(h>1/2\), \(C\ge0\), and \(\varepsilon>0\).
Since \(2h-1>0\), continuity at zero supplies \(e>0\) such that
\[
 LC^2\delta^{2h-1}<1\qquad(0<\delta<e).
\]
Choose the preceding profile with
\(0<\Delta<\min\{e,\varepsilon\}\).
If \(D\le C\Delta^h\), nonnegativity and the quadratic relation give
\[
 \Delta\le LD^2\le LC^2\Delta^{2h}.
\]
Division by the positive \(\Delta\) contradicts the choice of \(e\).
Thus \(D>C\Delta^h\).
The Lipschitz assertion for \(|y-z|\) is the reverse triangle inequality.
\hfill\(\square\)

This proves optimality of the exponent, not of the leading constant.
It concerns arbitrary nonnegative real weights in the fixed moment
problem.  Integer multiplicities, reflection, and the arithmetic
conditions on actual zeta zeros remain additional restrictions;
the construction does not assert that such zero clusters exist.
A stronger inverse theorem on those restricted classes would therefore
need to use information beyond the moment constraints alone.

\subsection{Complete feasible-mass region and the sharp inverse count}

The preceding phase diagram fixes the moment mass and the selected order.
We now determine exactly which such pairs can occur.  This is an existence
classification for finite profiles with even natural weights, together with
its necessary consequence for actual reflected zeta clusters.
Throughout this subsection the cutoff is inclusive:
\(I_d(y)=1\) when \(y\ge d\), and zero otherwise.

\paragraph{Theorem (complete threshold feasibility).}
Let \(0<d<1\), let \(k>0\) be an integer, and put \(K=2k\).  Define
\begin{equation}
 R_d(K)=d(K-2)+1+\sqrt{1+2(K-2)d(1-d)}.
 \label{eq:threshold-minimum-mass}
\end{equation}
For every real \(M\), the following statements are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
\item There is a finite profile with weights \(a_i\in2\mathbb N\),
coordinates \(y_i\ge0\), and
\[
 \sum_i a_i y_i=\sum_i a_i y_i^2=M,
 \qquad \sum_i a_i I_d(y_i)=K.
\]
\item Such a profile exists on two sites, allowing a zero weight.
\item \(M\ge R_d(K)\).
\end{enumerate}
In particular the minimum is attained, and
\[
 2\le R_d(K)\le K,\qquad dK<R_d(K).
\]
For every admissible profile one also has the inverse count bound
\begin{equation}
 K\le C_d(M):=
 \frac{M+d-\sqrt{d^2+2(1-d)M}}d.
 \label{eq:threshold-inverse-count}
\end{equation}
These are constraints on the moment data themselves, independent of a
subsequent energy estimate.

\paragraph{Proof of necessity: exact ledgers and cross terms.}
Write
\[
 x_i=(y_i-d)I_d(y_i),\quad
 X=\sum_i a_i x_i,\quad
 Y=\sum_i a_i y_i(1-I_d(y_i)),
\]
\[
 U=\sum_i a_i x_i^2,\qquad
 V=\sum_i a_i y_i^2(1-I_d(y_i)).
\]
All five quantities are nonnegative.  The first and second moments give
exactly
\begin{equation}
 M-dK=X+Y,\qquad M=d^2K+2dX+U+V.
 \label{eq:threshold-feasibility-ledgers}
\end{equation}
On every unselected site, \(0\le y_i<d\), so \(V\le dY\).
For each nonzero even weight, \(a_i\ge2\).  Consequently
\[
 2a_i x_i^2\le(a_i x_i)^2,
 \qquad
 2U\le\sum_i(a_i x_i)^2\le\Bigl(\sum_i a_i x_i\Bigr)^2=X^2.
\]
The last inequality retains all the nonnegative cross terms; it is not a
Cauchy--Schwarz relaxation.  Substituting into the full second ledger yields
\[
 2M\le2d^2K+4dX+X^2+2dY
       \le2d^2K+4d(X+Y)+(X+Y)^2.
\]
The second inequality has precisely the slack
\(2dY+2XY+Y^2\ge0\).  Hence
\begin{equation}
 (M-dK)^2+(4d-2)M-2d^2K\ge0.
 \label{eq:threshold-feasibility-quadratic}
\end{equation}
Since \(d>0\) and \(K>0\), the first ledger implies \(M>0\).
The already proved equal-moment witness theorem then gives \(dK<M\).

For completeness, set
\(D=1+2(K-2)d(1-d)\) and \(A=1+d(K-2)\).
Here \(D\ge1\), and \(R=A+\sqrt D\) is an exact root of the
quadratic in \eqref{eq:threshold-feasibility-quadratic}.  Its other root
is \(A-\sqrt D\le dK-2d<dK\).
Thus the admissible branch \(M>dK\) forces \(M\ge R\).
Also \(R\ge2\), \(R-dK=1-2d+\sqrt D>0\), and, with
\(B=1+(K-2)(1-d)\),
\[
 B^2-D=(K-2)K(1-d)^2\ge0.
\]
Since \(B\ge0\), this proves \(\sqrt D\le B\) and \(R\le K\).
Finally the quadratic can equally be written
\[
 (M+d-dK)^2\ge d^2+2(1-d)M.
\]
Its left base is positive because \(dK<M\).  Taking the nonnegative
square root and dividing by \(d>0\) proves
\eqref{eq:threshold-inverse-count}.

\paragraph{Proof of sufficiency: every mass, not just the boundary.}
For real \(m\ge0\), \(n>0\), and \(0\le u\le1\), put
\[
 v_{m,n}(u)=\frac{1+\sqrt{1+4(m/n)u(1-u)}}2,\qquad
 H_{m,n}(u)=2mu+2nv_{m,n}(u).
\]
The radical is at least one, so \(v_{m,n}(u)\ge1\).  Direct squaring,
with the displayed nonnegative root, proves the exact identity
\[
 mu^2+nv_{m,n}(u)^2=mu+nv_{m,n}(u).
\]
Thus the two sites \(u,v_{m,n}(u)\) with weights \(2m,2n\) have equal
moments \(H_{m,n}(u)\).  This function is continuous, and
\[
 H_{m,n}(0)=2n,\qquad H_{m,n}(1)=2m+2n.
\]

If \(R_d(K)\le M\le K\), take the integer weights
\(m=k-1\), \(n=1\), and vary \(u\) over \([d,1]\).
Both sites are selected throughout this interval, so their selected order
is \(2(k-1)+2=K\).  The endpoint masses are
\[
 H_{k-1,1}(d)=R_d(2k),\qquad H_{k-1,1}(1)=2k.
\]
The intermediate value theorem supplies every intervening mass \(M\).
This includes \(k=1\): the first weight is then zero and the endpoint
interval consists only of mass two.  At the minimum the two coordinates
are exactly \(d,v_{k-1,1}(d)\).

If \(M\ge K\), choose a natural number \(n>M/d\) and use the integer
weights \(2n,2k\).  Vary \(u\) over \([0,d/2]\), taking the high site
\(v_{n,k}(u)\).  The low site is strictly below \(d\), and the high site
is at least one, so the selected order is exactly \(2k=K\).
Now
\[
 H_{n,k}(0)=K,\qquad
 H_{n,k}(d/2)\ge nd+K>M.
\]
Continuity again gives the prescribed mass.  No monotonicity assumption
on either continuous family is needed.  This proves the equivalence and
attainment at the lower endpoint.

\paragraph{Theorem (actual reflection-paired inverse count).}
Let \(W\) be any finite set of strip points, stable under
\(\rho\mapsto1-\overline\rho\), with their actual analytic zeta orders
\(a_\rho\).  Assume \(N>0\) and \(M_1>0\), and retain the earlier
normalizations
\[
 t=Q/M_1>0,\qquad \kappa=M_1^2/Q,\qquad
 y_\rho=(\operatorname{Re}\rho-1/2)^2/t.
\]
For every \(0<d<1\), let \(K_d=\sum_{\rho\in W}a_\rho I_d(y_\rho)\).
Then
\begin{equation}
 R_d(K_d)\le\kappa,\qquad K_d\le C_d(\kappa).
 \label{eq:actual-threshold-feasibility}
\end{equation}
The installed compensated-order specialization follows by setting
\(d=\delta\).  No common-height or lower-height hypothesis is needed for
this structural result.

\paragraph{Proof without individual-order evenness.}
The normalized first and second moments both equal \(\kappa\), by exact
rescaling.  Positive-threshold reflection parity already proves
\(K_d=2k\); the equal-moment witness gives \(K_d>0\), hence \(k>0\).
It remains to replace the even-weight square estimate: individual orders
may be odd.  Set \(x_\rho=(y_\rho-d)I_d(y_\rho)\) and
\(X=\sum_{\rho\in W}a_\rho x_\rho\).
Whenever \(a_\rho x_\rho>0\), the point is off the critical line and
has a distinct reflected partner in \(W\).  The partner has the same
coordinate and the same positive integral order.  Both orders are at
least one, so, retaining these two contributions to the nonnegative sum,
\[
 X\ge a_\rho x_\rho+a_{1-\overline\rho}x_{1-\overline\rho}
   \ge2x_\rho.
\]
Multiplying by \(a_\rho x_\rho\ge0\) and summing, including the zero
terms, gives
\[
 2\sum_{\rho\in W}a_\rho x_\rho^2\le X^2.
\]
The same exact ledgers therefore prove
\eqref{eq:threshold-feasibility-quadratic} for \((\kappa,K_d)\).
The already established admissible-root and inverse-count implications
prove \eqref{eq:actual-threshold-feasibility}.

The thirty-six new proof bodies are maintained in
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdFeasibilityEnvelope.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdFeasibilityRoot.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdFeasibilityModels.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdFeasibilityExistence.lean}, and
\texttt{Assembly/CarmichaelThresholdFeasibility.lean}.
The previous work classified equality and approachability for an energy
cost; this theorem instead classifies existence at prescribed moment mass
and selected order.  Its exact minimum is the first member of the previous
negative exceptional-mass spectrum when that spectrum is nonempty.
Classical moment optimization and equal-weight quadrature remain the
background discussed above; broad priority is not asserted.
The two-site witnesses certify sharpness in the abstract even-weight
profile class, not realization by zeta zeros.  The actual result constrains
finite off-critical cluster data.  It supplies no independent global
small-value area estimate, and it changes neither the numerical simplicity
window nor the certified \(7/8\) boundary.

\subsection{Linear stability at the integer feasibility boundary}
\label{subsec:threshold-feasibility-linear-stability}

The feasibility envelope also has an inverse theorem.  Its discrete weight
constraint recovers a minimal-multiplicity atom and gives an error linear
in the full quadratic slack.  There is no cubic-energy hypothesis.

\paragraph{Theorem (linear feasibility inverse).}
Let \(a_i\in\mathbb N\), let \(y_i\ge0\) on a finite index set, and let
\(0<d<1\).  Write \(w_i=2a_i\) and assume
\[
 N=\sum_iw_i,\qquad
 M=\sum_iw_i y_i=\sum_iw_i y_i^2>0,\qquad
 K=\sum_{y_i\ge d}w_i.
\]
Put
\[
 A=M-dK>0,\qquad
 P=A^2+(4d-2)M-2d^2K\ge0,\qquad c=d+A/2.
\]
The displayed signs follow from the proved domain and feasibility
inequalities.  Suppose
\[
 P\le dA,\qquad P<A^2/8.
\]
Then \(2\le K\le N\), there is an index \(j\) with
\(a_j=1\) and \(y_j>d\), and the nonnegative reference measure
\[
 \nu=(N-K)\delta_0+(K-2)\delta_d+2\delta_c
\]
satisfies, for every globally \(1\)-Lipschitz
\(\varphi:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R\),
\[
 \left|\sum_iw_i\varphi(y_i)-\int\varphi\,d\nu\right|
 \le \frac Pd+\frac{4P}{A}.
\]
The reference has moments
\[
 \int1\,d\nu=N,\qquad
 \int y\,d\nu=M,\qquad
 \int y^2\,d\nu=M+P/2.
\]
In particular, at zero feasibility slack every Lipschitz statistic agrees
exactly with this explicit reference, which then satisfies both moment
constraints.  The selected atom of weight two is present in this case too.

\paragraph{Proof: the complete slack ledger.}
Retain the five quantities from the feasibility proof:
\[
 x_i=(y_i-d)I_d(y_i),\quad
 X=\sum_iw_ix_i,\quad
 Y=\sum_iw_i y_i(1-I_d(y_i)),
\]
\[
 U=\sum_iw_i x_i^2,\qquad
 V=\sum_iw_i y_i^2(1-I_d(y_i)).
\]
Here \(I_d(y)=1\) for \(y\ge d\), so the cutoff itself is selected.
The exact first and second ledgers give
\[
 A=X+Y,\qquad M=d^2K+2dX+U+V.
\]
Substituting these identities, before making any estimate, yields
\[
 P=(X^2-2U)+2XY+Y^2+2dY+2(dY-V).
\]
All five summands are nonnegative.  Indeed \(X,Y\ge0\), \(V\le dY\),
and the already proved even-atom square inequality gives \(2U\le X^2\).
Writing \(E=X^2-2U\), we therefore have
\[
 0\le E\le P,\qquad 2dY\le P.
\]
The first smallness condition implies \(Y\le A/2\), hence
\(X\ge A/2>0\).  The second gives
\[
 E\le P<A^2/8\le X^2/2.
\]

\paragraph{Proof: recovering the smallest allowed weight.}
Choose \(j\) with largest \(x_j\) among indices with positive weight.
Such an index exists because \(X>0\), and \(x_j>0\).
The complete nonnegative sum gives
\[
 U\le x_jX,\qquad w_jx_j\le X.
\]
If \(a_j\ge2\), then \(w_j\ge4\), so \(4x_j\le X\) and
\[
 E=X^2-2U\ge X^2-2x_jX\ge X^2/2,
\]
a contradiction.  Thus \(a_j=1\), \(w_j=2\), and \(y_j>d\).
Set
\[
 R=X-2x_j=\sum_{i\ne j}w_i x_i\ge0.
\]
Keeping all remaining contributions in \(X\) and \(U\) gives
\[
 XR=X^2-2x_jX\le X^2-2U=E\le P.
\]
Consequently
\[
 Y\le\frac{P}{2d},\qquad
 R\le\frac{P}{X}\le\frac{2P}{A}.
\]
This argument is formalized first in its project-independent form:
for natural weights \(m_i\), if
\(T=\sum_i m_i x_i>0\), \(Q=\sum_i m_i x_i^2\), and
\(T^2-Q<T^2/2\), a largest positive-weight coordinate has \(m_j=1\)
and
\[
 0\le T-x_j,\qquad T(T-x_j)\le T^2-Q.
\]
The even-weight argument is its exact rescaling by two.

\paragraph{Proof: the full statistic and zero slack.}
Compare the original profile with \(\nu\) by sending every unselected
atom to zero, every selected atom except \(j\) to \(d\), and the weight-two
atom at \(y_j\) to \(c\).  The first two costs are exactly \(Y\) and \(R\).
No selected weight is lost: the remaining cutoff weight is \(K-2\).
The first ledger gives the exact compensation
\[
 2(c-y_j)=Y+R\ge0.
\]
Thus the last cost is \(2|c-y_j|=Y+R\).
For a \(1\)-Lipschitz test function, summing all these contributions proves
\[
 \left|\sum_iw_i\varphi(y_i)
 -(N-K)\varphi(0)-(K-2)\varphi(d)-2\varphi(c)\right|
 \le2(Y+R)\le P/d+4P/A.
\]
The inequalities \(K\ge2\) and \(N\ge K\) follow respectively from the
selected weight-two atom and the nonnegativity of all weights.
Also \(c>d>0\), so \(\nu\) is a nonnegative measure with closed selected
mass \(K\).
Direct expansion of its three weighted contributions gives the displayed
total, first and second moments; the last excess is exactly \(P/2\).

If \(P=0\), then \(A>0\) makes both smallness conditions automatic.
The bound reduces to zero, proving the asserted equality of every
Lipschitz statistic, with the minimal selected atom retained.
\hfill\(\square\)

This is a feasibility-slack inverse theorem for even integer weights.
The earlier square-root theorem estimates cubic-energy excess for arbitrary
nonnegative real weights; its rate-optimality construction varies weights
continuously.  The new proof uses the discrete alternative \(w_i=2\) or
\(w_i\ge4\) and the entire quadratic ledger.
Its constants are explicit, but no optimal leading constant is asserted.

The finite even-weight conclusion does not by itself identify individual
zeta orders as even: reflection gives even combined order on pairs.
The exact orbit-profile construction in the next subsection transfers this
inverse theorem while retaining the full critical-line multiplicity at zero.

\subsection{The paired inverse for actual zeta orders}
\label{subsec:paired-threshold-feasibility-stability}

Reflection supplies the even weights needed by the preceding inverse theorem,
without requiring any individual zeta order to be even.  The critical-line
mass remains present throughout the construction and its compensation.

\paragraph{Theorem (reflection-paired linear inverse).}
Let \(W\) be any finite subset of \(0<\Re w<1\), closed under
\(r(w)=1-\overline w\).  Write \(a_w=\operatorname{ord}_w\zeta\), with
order zero at nonzeros, and put
\[
 N=\sum_{w\in W}a_w,\qquad
 M_1=\sum_{w\in W}a_w(\Re w-\tfrac12)^2,\qquad
 M_2=\sum_{w\in W}a_w(\Re w-\tfrac12)^4.
\]
Assume \(N>0\) and \(M_1>0\).  Then \(M_2>0\), and define
\[
 t=M_2/M_1>0,\qquad M=M_1^2/M_2>0,\qquad
 y_w=(\Re w-\tfrac12)^2/t.
\]
For any \(0<d<1\), set
\[
 K=\sum_{\substack{w\in W\\y_w\ge d}}a_w,\qquad
 A=M-dK,\qquad P=A^2+(4d-2)M-2d^2K.
\]
The proved moment and paired-feasibility inequalities imply \(A>0\)
and \(P\ge0\).  Suppose further that
\[
 P\le dA,\qquad P<A^2/8.
\]
There is \(w\in W\) with \(\Re w>1/2\) and \(y_w>d\) whose distinct
reflected partner also lies in \(W\), and
\[
 \operatorname{ord}_w\zeta=\operatorname{ord}_{r(w)}\zeta=1.
\]
Thus both members of this selected pair are simple zeros.
For every globally \(1\)-Lipschitz \(\varphi:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R\),
\[
 \left|\sum_{w\in W}a_w\varphi(y_w)
 -(N-K)\varphi(0)-(K-2)\varphi(d)
 -2\varphi(d+A/2)\right|
 \le P/d+4P/A.
\]
In particular, when \(P=0\), the selected simple pair is present and every
such statistic agrees exactly with the displayed three-point reference.
There is no common-height, lower-height or RH hypothesis.

\paragraph{Proof: a simultaneous orbit profile.}
Let \(R=\{w\in W:\Re w>1/2\}\), let
\(L=\{w\in W:\Re w<1/2\}\), and retain the full central mass
\[
 C=\sum_{\substack{w\in W\\\Re w=1/2}}a_w.
\]
The zeta functional equation gives \(a_{r(w)}=a_w\) throughout the open
strip, and \(y_{r(w)}=y_w\).  Reflection is a bijection from \(L\) to
\(R\); every central point has \(y_w=0\).  Hence, for every function
\(\varphi:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R\), without any regularity condition,
\[
 \sum_{w\in W}a_w\varphi(y_w)
 =2\sum_{w\in R}a_w\varphi(y_w)+C\varphi(0).
\]
Choose a bijection from \(R\) to \(\{0,\ldots,n-1\}\) and let
\(b_i\in\mathbb N\), \(z_i\ge0\) be the order and normalized coordinate
of its inverse image.  This single profile satisfies, simultaneously for
every \(\varphi\),
\[
 \sum_{w\in W}a_w\varphi(y_w)
 =\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}2b_i\varphi(z_i)+C\varphi(0).
\]
Each \(i\) retains its actual right-half-plane carrier in \(W\), including
both its individual order \(b_i\) and its coordinate \(z_i\).

\paragraph{Proof: transfer of all moments and compensation.}
The definitions and \(M_1,M_2>0\) give
\[
 \sum_{w\in W}a_wy_w=M_1/t=M,\qquad
 \sum_{w\in W}a_wy_w^2=M_2/t^2=M.
\]
Use the simultaneous profile with \(\varphi(y)=y\) and \(y^2\).
Since both vanish at zero, the profile has equal first and second
moments \(M\).  Use it with the closed indicator
\(I_d(y)=1_{\{y\ge d\}}\).  Because \(d>0\), \(I_d(0)=0\), so its
selected mass is exactly the original \(K\).  Finally use the constant
function one to obtain
\[
 N=N_p+C,\qquad N_p=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}2b_i.
\]
The domain inequality gives \(A>0\), and paired feasibility gives \(P\ge0\).

Apply the preceding integer feasibility inverse to the profile
\((2b_i,z_i)\).  It produces an index \(j\) with \(b_j=1\), \(z_j>d\),
and the bound \(P/d+4P/A\) relative to the reference with total mass
\(N_p\).  Its carrier \(w\in R\) has order one and \(y_w>d\).
The strip reflection identity gives order one at \(r(w)\).
It is distinct from \(w\), because \(\Re w>1/2\).

For clarity, if \(T_H(\varphi)\) denotes
\[
 T_H(\varphi)=(H-K)\varphi(0)+(K-2)\varphi(d)
                  +2\varphi(d+A/2),
\]
then the full, exact compensation is
\[
 \sum_{w\in W}a_w\varphi(y_w)-T_N(\varphi)
 =\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}2b_i\varphi(z_i)-T_{N_p}(\varphi).
\]
Indeed the \(C\varphi(0)\) contribution occurs on both sides of the
comparison after \(N=N_p+C\) is substituted.  No central order is discarded.
The profile bound now proves the stated full-cluster inequality.
When \(P=0\), \(A>0\) makes both smallness conditions automatic, and
the error is zero, while the selected simple pair persists.
\hfill\(\square\)

This gives a structural criterion on actual analytic orders, extending the
abstract even-weight inverse through its exact orbit profile.  It does not
prove that zeta clusters satisfy the small-slack assumptions independently;
it therefore supplies neither global simplicity nor a new zero-free
boundary.  The folding and finite reindexing use classical symmetry
arguments; no general priority claim is made.

\subsection{The complete region at fixed total order}

The preceding theorem allows the total weight of a profile to vary.  Fixing
it gives a second, genuinely restrictive face of the feasible region.
The upper quadratic argument is a classical two-moment dual argument,
consistent with the Bertsimas--Popescu framework cited above.  The result
here combines that face with the attained arithmetic lower face and gives
finite even-weight realizations of every permitted mass.

\paragraph{Theorem (complete fixed-total threshold region).}
Let \(0<d<1\) and let \(0<k\le n\) be integers.  Put \(N=2n\),
\(K=2k\), and define
\begin{equation}
 U_d(N,K)=d(N-K)+\frac K2+
   \sqrt{\frac{K^2}4+K(N-K)d(1-d)}.
 \label{eq:threshold-total-upper}
\end{equation}
For a real \(M\), there exists a finite profile with
\[
 a_i\in2\mathbb N,\quad y_i\ge0,\quad
 \sum_i a_i=N,\quad
 \sum_i a_i y_i=\sum_i a_i y_i^2=M,\quad
 \sum_i a_i I_d(y_i)=K
\]
if and only if
\begin{equation}
 \begin{cases}
 R_d(K)\le M\le K,&n=k,\\
 R_d(K)\le M<U_d(N,K),&k<n.
 \end{cases}
 \label{eq:threshold-total-complete-region}
\end{equation}
Three sites suffice in every feasible case, allowing zero weights.
The lower endpoint is always attained.  When \(n=k\), the upper endpoint
is attained as well.  When \(k<n\),
\[
 K<U_d(N,K)<N,
\]
and \(U_d(N,K)\) is an unattained supremum: every positive tolerance
admits an exact profile with mass strictly between
\(U_d(N,K)-\epsilon\) and \(U_d(N,K)\).
The theorem explicitly concerns positive selected order; its parameter
range is \(0<k\le n\).

\paragraph{Proof of the upper face with every compensation term.}
Write \(J=N-K\).  For \(J\ge0\) and \(K>0\), set
\[
 v=\frac{1+\sqrt{1+4(J/K)d(1-d)}}2.
\]
Then \(v\ge1>d\),
\begin{equation}
 Jd^2+Kv^2=Jd+Kv,
 \qquad U_d(N,K)=Jd+Kv.
 \label{eq:threshold-total-root-balance}
\end{equation}
The second identity follows by scaling the nonnegative square root by
\(K/2>0\); its squared radical is exactly
\(K^2/4+KJd(1-d)\).
Consider the full signed packet
\[
 P_{d,v}(y)=(y-v)^2-(d-v)^2(1-I_d(y)).
\]
If \(y\ge d\), this is \((y-v)^2\ge0\).
If \(y<d\), its exact factorization is
\[
 P_{d,v}(y)=(y-d)(y+d-2v)>0.
\]
No term is estimated separately before this cancellation.
The exact summed identity, using both moment equalities, is
\begin{align}
 \sum_i a_i P_{d,v}(y_i)
   &=(1-2v)M+v^2N-(d-v)^2(N-K)\\
   &=(2v-1)\{U_d(N,K)-M\}.
 \label{eq:threshold-total-signed-ledger}
\end{align}
The last equality uses the complete root balance
\eqref{eq:threshold-total-root-balance}.
When \(J>0\), at least one unselected site has positive weight, since
\[
 \sum_i a_i(1-I_d(y_i))=N-K=J>0.
\]
Its packet is strictly positive.  All other weighted packets are
nonnegative, so the left side of
\eqref{eq:threshold-total-signed-ledger} is positive.  Since \(2v-1>0\),
this proves \(M<U_d(N,K)\).
This argument works for arbitrary nonnegative real weights; it uses
neither evenness nor individual lower bounds on nonzero weights.

For the closed face \(N=K\), the exact variance identity
\[
 \sum_i a_i(y_i-1)^2=N-M\ge0
\]
gives \(M\le K\).  The preceding arithmetic feasibility theorem gives
\(R_d(K)\le M\) in both cases.
Finally \(U_d(K,K)=K\).  If \(J>0\), the root balance implies
\[
 U_d(N,K)-K=Jd+K(v-1)>0,
\]
\[
 N-U_d(N,K)=J(d-1)^2+K(v-1)^2>0.
\]
This proves all necessary inequalities and the scalar endpoint bounds.

\paragraph{Proof that every permitted mass is realized.}
For \(R_d(K)\le M\le K\), use the preceding bounded construction:
weights \(2(k-1),2\), coordinates \(u,v_{k-1,1}(u)\), and
\(u\in[d,1]\).  Add a zero-coordinate site of weight \(2(n-k)\).
Since \(d>0\), this padding is unselected and changes neither moment
nor selected order.  It makes the total weight exactly \(2n\).
The exact endpoint and continuity argument already proved supplies every
such \(M\), including the lower endpoint and the case \(k=1\).
The zero padding weight when \(n=k\) causes no exception.

For \(K\le M<U_d(N,K)\), use the fixed weights
\(2(n-k),2k\) and coordinates
\[
 u,\quad v_{n-k,k}(u),\qquad 0\le u\le d.
\]
Their common moment \(H_{n-k,k}(u)\) is continuous, with
\[
 H_{n-k,k}(0)=K,\qquad H_{n-k,k}(d)=U_d(N,K).
\]
The intermediate value theorem therefore gives the prescribed mass.
The chosen parameter cannot equal \(d\), because \(M<U_d(N,K)\).
Thus its low site is strictly unselected, its high site is at least one,
and its selected order is exactly \(2k\).  Two sites suffice on this
branch; padding by a zero weight gives the uniform three-site statement.
No monotonicity assumption is used.

For an explicit supremum witness, when \(k<n\) put
\[
 e=\min\{\epsilon,U_d(N,K)-K\}>0,\qquad
 M=U_d(N,K)-e/2.
\]
Then \(K\le M<U_d(N,K)\) and \(U_d(N,K)-\epsilon<M\), so the
upper-branch construction gives an exact two-site profile.
At \(n=k\), the bounded family at \(u=1\) attains mass \(K\).
This proves sufficiency, all endpoint statements, and the claimed supremum.

\paragraph{Theorem (joint actual total-order region).}
Retain a finite reflected strip cluster \(W\), its actual orders,
\(N>0\), \(M_1>0\), and the normalized data
\(t=Q/M_1\), \(\kappa=M_1^2/Q\), \(y_\rho=(\Re\rho-1/2)^2/t\).
For every \(0<d<1\), let \(K_d=\sum_{\rho\in W}a_\rho I_d(y_\rho)\).
Then the compiled actual consumer gives all of
\begin{equation}
 R_d(K_d)\le\kappa,\qquad K_d\le C_d(\kappa),\qquad K_d\le N,
 \label{eq:actual-total-region-counts}
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}
 \begin{cases}
 \kappa\le N,&K_d=N,\\
 \kappa<U_d(N,K_d),&K_d<N.
 \end{cases}
 \label{eq:actual-total-region-upper}
\end{equation}
The strict upper inequality itself does not require reflection.
In particular, no evenness assumption on the actual total order \(N\)
is made: odd total order, including central multiplicity, is covered.
The installed compensated-order specialization \(d=\delta\) is also
compiled, with every inequality in the same conclusion.

\paragraph{Proof of the actual consumer.}
Exact rescaling makes both normalized moments equal \(\kappa\), and
preserves the total weight \(N\) and selected weight \(K_d\).
The equal-moment witness theorem gives \(K_d>0\).
Since \(0\le I_d\le1\), summing against actual nonnegative orders gives
\(K_d\le N\).  If this is strict, apply the full signed packet identity
\eqref{eq:threshold-total-signed-ledger} to the actual normalized data;
the real-weight version applies directly.  If \(K_d=N\), the installed
moment feasibility inequality gives \(\kappa\le N\).
The previous reflection-paired theorem supplies both remaining inequalities
in \eqref{eq:actual-total-region-counts}.  The already proved equality
between normalized selected mass and compensated order gives the
\(d=\delta\) specialization.

The twenty-seven new proof bodies are maintained in
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdTotalPackets.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdTotalBoundary.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdTotalModels.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdTotalFeasibility.lean}, and
\texttt{Assembly/CarmichaelThresholdTotal.lean}.
Unlike the earlier kernel minimum at fixed total mass, this theorem fixes
both normalized moments and the inclusive selected order.  Its upper
quadratic mechanism belongs to classical moment optimization; the specific
joint arithmetic-weight classification and reflected-cluster consumer are
the conclusions established here.  Broad priority is not asserted.
The finite profile realizations are not asserted to be zeta-zero
realizations.  This result adds an independent total-order constraint to
the cluster geometry, not an independent analytic upper bound proving
simplicity of all zeros; the numerical simplicity window and certified
\(7/8\) boundary remain unchanged.

\subsection{Sharp stability at the fixed-total upper boundary}
\label{subsec:threshold-total-stability}

The fixed-total supremum has a quantitative inverse with a different rate
from the lower integer-feasibility boundary.  Approaching this upper face
forces two-layer concentration.  Its limiting low layer lies at the
inclusive cutoff itself.

\paragraph{Theorem (two-layer upper-boundary stability).}
Let \(S\) be finite, \(a_i\ge0\), \(y_i\in\mathbb R\), and \(0<d<1\).
Assume
\[
 N=\sum_{i\in S}a_i,\qquad
 M=\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i=\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i^2,\qquad
 K=\sum_{\substack{i\in S\\y_i\ge d}}a_i>0.
\]
There is no integrality or nonnegative-coordinate hypothesis.
Write
\[
 v=\frac{1+\sqrt{1+4((N-K)/K)d(1-d)}}2,\quad
 U=(N-K)d+Kv,\quad \Delta=U-M,\quad D=(2v-1)\Delta.
\]
Then \(K\le N\), \(v\ge1>d\), and \(\Delta,D\ge0\).
Define the low displacement and selected squared displacement by
\[
 L=\sum_{\substack{i\in S\\y_i<d}}a_i(d-y_i),\qquad
 Q=\sum_{\substack{i\in S\\y_i\ge d}}a_i(y_i-v)^2.
\]
They satisfy
\[
 2(v-d)L\le D,\qquad Q\le D.
\]
For every globally \(1\)-Lipschitz \(\varphi:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R\),
\begin{equation}
 \left|\sum_{i\in S}a_i\varphi(y_i)
       -(N-K)\varphi(d)-K\varphi(v)\right|
 \le \frac{D}{2(v-d)}+\sqrt{KD}.
 \label{eq:threshold-total-stability}
\end{equation}
The reference measure \((N-K)\delta_d+K\delta_v\) has total weight \(N\)
and equal first and second moments \(U\).  If \(K<N\), it has closed
selected weight \(N\), although the approximating profiles have selected
weight \(K\).  Thus the assertion controls Lipschitz statistics and does
not claim continuity of the inclusive threshold indicator.

\paragraph{Proof: the full signed packet.}
With \(I_d(y)=\mathbf1_{y\ge d}\), the exact pointwise decomposition is
\begin{align*}
 &(y-v)^2-(d-v)^2(1-I_d(y))\\
 &\qquad=(d-y)^2(1-I_d(y))
       +2(v-d)(d-y)(1-I_d(y))+(y-v)^2I_d(y).
\end{align*}
All three contributions on the second line are nonnegative.  In the
unselected case \(y<d\), the middle factor has \(d-y>0\); in the
selected case the two low contributions are zero.

The root balance
\[
 (N-K)d^2+Kv^2=(N-K)d+Kv
\]
and both moment constraints give, before estimating any term,
\begin{align*}
 D&=\sum_{i\in S}a_i\bigl\{(y_i-v)^2-(d-v)^2(1-I_d(y_i))\bigr\}\\
  &=\sum_{\substack{i\in S\\y_i<d}}a_i(d-y_i)^2+2(v-d)L+Q.
\end{align*}
This proves \(D\ge0\), the two layer bounds, and \(\Delta\ge0\), since
\(2v-1>0\).  When \(K<N\), the earlier strict packet argument gives
\(\Delta>0\); when \(K=N\), the same inverse remains valid on the closed
face \(U=N\).

\paragraph{Proof: the combined statistic bound.}
Put
\[
 H=\sum_{\substack{i\in S\\y_i\ge d}}a_i|y_i-v|.
\]
The complete weighted variance of the selected displacements gives
\[
 0\le\sum_{\substack{i\in S\\y_i\ge d}}
        a_i\{K|y_i-v|-H\}^2=K^2Q-KH^2.
\]
Since \(K>0\), \(H^2\le KQ\le KD\), hence \(H\le\sqrt{KD}\).
Sending every low coordinate to \(d\) and every selected coordinate to
\(v\), with every weight retained, gives the exact residual
\[
 \sum_{i\in S}a_i\{\varphi(y_i)-(1-I_d(y_i))\varphi(d)
                                      -I_d(y_i)\varphi(v)\}.
\]
Its absolute value is at most \(L+H\) by the Lipschitz hypothesis.
The layer bounds therefore prove
\eqref{eq:threshold-total-stability}.
The root balance also proves both stated moments of the reference.
\hfill\(\square\)

\paragraph{Theorem (fixed even-weight square-root lower rate).}
The square-root scale occurs already at the fixed parameters
\[
 d=\tfrac12,\qquad N=16,\qquad K=4,\qquad v=\tfrac32,\qquad U=12.
\]
For every \(\epsilon>0\), an exact three-site profile with weights
\(12,2,2\) has \(0<\Delta<\epsilon\), both moments equal to \(U-\Delta\),
closed selected weight \(4\), and, for the single fixed \(1\)-Lipschitz
test \(\varphi(y)=|y-\tfrac32|\),
\[
 \sum_{i=0}^2a_i\varphi(y_i)-12\varphi(\tfrac12)-4\varphi(\tfrac32)
 \ge\sqrt{\Delta}.
\]
Consequently these exact integer-weight profiles exhibit a square-root
lower rate at arbitrarily small gaps, while
\eqref{eq:threshold-total-stability} supplies the matching upper order.

\paragraph{Proof: every weight and cutoff stays fixed.}
For \(0<s\le1/4\), put
\[
 r=s\sqrt{2-4s^2},\qquad
 u=\tfrac12-s^2,\qquad c=\tfrac32-s^2,
 \qquad (y_0,y_1,y_2)=(u,c+r,c-r).
\]
The natural multiplicities are \(6,1,1\), hence the even weights are
\(12,2,2\).  The exact identities and bounds are
\[
 r^2=2s^2-4s^4,\qquad s\le r\le\tfrac12,\qquad
 0\le u<\tfrac12<c-r\le c+r.
\]
Thus only the two weight-two sites are selected.  Direct expansion gives
\[
 12u+4c=12-16s^2,\qquad
 12u^2+4(c^2+r^2)=12-16s^2.
\]
Therefore \(\Delta=16s^2>0\).  Since \(r\ge s\ge s^2\), the fixed test
has exactly
\[
 12|u-\tfrac32|+2|c+r-\tfrac32|+2|c-r-\tfrac32|-12
 =12s^2+4r\ge4s=\sqrt{\Delta}.
\]
To obtain arbitrarily small gaps, choose
\(s=\min\{1/4,\epsilon/32\}>0\).  Then
\(16s^2\le4s\le\epsilon/8<\epsilon\).
The boundary values \(v=3/2\), \(U=12\), the Lipschitz property of this
fixed test, and the complete arbitrary-small-gap lower bound are all
formalized.
\hfill\(\square\)

\paragraph{Actual zeta-order consumer.}
Let \(W\) be any finite subset of the open strip, with actual analytic
orders \(a_w=\operatorname{ord}_w\zeta\), positive total order and positive
horizontal first moment.  Use the already proved normalization
\[
 t=M_2/M_1>0,\qquad M=M_1^2/M_2>0,\qquad
 y_w=(\Re w-\tfrac12)^2/t.
\]
Both weighted normalized moments equal \(M\).  For every \(0<d<1\), the
selected mass is positive, so the preceding theorem applies to these
actual orders and proves both layer bounds and
\eqref{eq:threshold-total-stability} for the full cluster.
No reflection, parity, common-height, lower-height or RH premise is needed.
All critical-line orders remain in the low layer at \(y_w=0\).
The finite sharpness profiles above are not claimed to be zeta-zero
realizations.  This result gives an inverse for observable cluster
geometry, without supplying an independent global estimate for its gap.

The quadratic moment optimization background is classical, as discussed
for the preceding fixed-total theorem.  The explicit layer ledger,
finite-profile inverse, fixed integer-weight rate witness and actual-order
consumer are the results proved here; the limited literature check does
not establish general priority.

\subsection{A sharp linear inverse for one selected radial level}
\label{subsec:single-selected-linear}

The square-root loss in the preceding theorem comes from variation
\emph{within} the selected layer.  When that layer has only one level,
the moment identity retains its signed displacement and gives a linear
inverse.  Minimum positive even selected order supplies this hypothesis
automatically.

\paragraph{Theorem (exact cost and sharp linear stability).}
Let \(S\) be finite, \(a_i\geq0\), \(y_i\in\mathbb R\), and \(0<d<1\).
Write
\[
 I_d(y)=\mathbf1_{\{y\geq d\}},\quad
 N=\sum_{i\in S}a_i,\quad
 K=\sum_{i\in S}a_iI_d(y_i)>0,\quad
 \sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i=\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i^2=M.
\]
Suppose the selected weighted law is a single point mass: for some \(h\),
\[
 \sum_{i\in S}a_iI_d(y_i)\phi(y_i)=K\phi(h)
 \qquad\text{for every }\phi:\mathbb R\longrightarrow\mathbb R.
\]
Set
\[
 v=\frac{1+\sqrt{1+4(N-K)d(1-d)/K}}2,\quad
 U=(N-K)d+Kv,\quad \Delta=U-M,
 \qquad L=\sum_{\substack{i\in S\\y_i<d}}a_i(d-y_i).
\]
Then \(v\geq1>d\), \(\Delta\geq0\), and the exact globally
\(1\)-Lipschitz statistic error is
\begin{align}
 \mathcal C&:=
 \sup_{\operatorname{Lip}(\phi)\leq1}
 \left|\sum_{i\in S}a_i\phi(y_i)-(N-K)\phi(d)-K\phi(v)\right| \notag\\
 &=L+|\Delta-L|
 \leq c(d,v)\Delta,\qquad
 c(d,v)=\max\left\{1,\frac{v+d-1}{v-d}\right\}.
 \label{eq:single-selected-linear}
\end{align}
The single test \(\phi_v(y)=|y-v|\) attains this supremum.
If \(d\leq\tfrac12\), then \(c(d,v)=1\) and
\(\mathcal C=\Delta\).

For every fixed \(J=N-K>0\), \(K>0\), and \(0<d<1\), this coefficient
is optimal even arbitrarily close to the upper boundary: for every
\(c<c(d,v)\) and \(\eta>0\), a nonnegative two-site profile with these
same masses satisfies \(0<\Delta<\eta\) and
\(\mathcal C>c\Delta\).  For \(J=2m\), \(K=2\), \(m\geq1\) integral,
the witnesses have even integer weights \(2m,2\).
The positive-gap sharpness assertion requires \(J>0\).
On the diagonal \(N=K\), a single selected level instead forces
\(h=v=1\), so \(\Delta=\mathcal C=0\).

\paragraph{Proof.}
For any finite nonnegative weighted law and any \(v\geq d\), let
\[
 H=\sum_{\substack{i\in S\\y_i\geq d}}a_i|y_i-v|.
\]
Transporting each unselected coordinate to \(d\) and each selected
coordinate to \(v\), and using the Lipschitz inequality on each signed
difference, gives an error at most \(L+H\).  For the single test
\(\phi_v\), every difference has exactly that sign and magnitude:
\(\phi_v(y)-\phi_v(d)=d-y\) when \(y<d\), while
\(\phi_v(y)-\phi_v(v)=|y-v|\) when \(y\geq d\).
Thus the bound is attained, without a moment assumption.
More generally this exact cost has the signed overshoot identity
\[
 L+H=U-M+
       2\sum_{i\in S}a_i\max(y_i-v,0).
\]
This is an equality, retaining all overshoots.

Under the single-selected-level hypothesis,
\[
 L=d(N-K)-M+Kh,\qquad
 H=K|h-v|=|\Delta-L|.
\]
The full upper-boundary packet from the preceding section is
\[
 (2v-1)\Delta
 =\sum_{\substack{i\in S\\y_i<d}}a_i(d-y_i)^2
       +2(v-d)L
       +\sum_{\substack{i\in S\\y_i\geq d}}a_i(y_i-v)^2.
\]
All three terms are nonnegative, so
\(2(v-d)L\leq(2v-1)\Delta\).
If \(L\leq\Delta\), then \(L+|\Delta-L|=\Delta\).
If \(L>\Delta\), the same exact signed expression is \(2L-\Delta\),
which is at most
\[
 \left(\frac{2v-1}{v-d}-1\right)\Delta
 =\frac{v+d-1}{v-d}\Delta.
\]
This proves the coefficient without estimating selected displacement
by a square-root norm.  For \(d\leq\tfrac12\), the latter quotient
is at most \(1\).  The identity
\(L+|\Delta-L|\geq\Delta\) then proves equality.

For optimality fix \(J,K>0\) and, for \(0<e<d\), use weights \(J,K\)
at the two coordinates
\[
 u_e=d-e,\qquad
 h_e=\frac{1+\sqrt{1+4(J/K)(d-e)(1-d+e)}}2.
\]
Here \(0<u_e<d<1\leq h_e\), so the selected mass remains exactly
\(K\) at the one level \(h_e\).  The defining quadratic gives equal
first and second moments.  With \(v=h_0\), the exact identities are
\[
 K(h_e-v)(h_e+v-1)=Je(2d-1-e),\qquad
 \Delta_e=Je-K(h_e-v),\qquad
 \mathcal C_e=Je+K|h_e-v|.
\]
In particular \(0<\Delta_e\leq2Je\).  The denominators
\(h_e+v-1\geq1\) and \(v-d>0\) never vanish.
If \(d\leq\tfrac12\), the first identity forces \(h_e\leq v\);
hence \(\mathcal C_e=\Delta_e\), proving sharpness of coefficient \(1\).
If \(d>\tfrac12\), restrict also to \(e<2d-1\).  For any
\(c<(v+d-1)/(v-d)\), retain the signed difference
\[
 \mathcal C_e-c\Delta_e=eG_c(e),\qquad
 G_c(e)=J(1-c)+
       \frac{(1+c)J(2d-1-e)}{h_e+v-1}.
\]
The function \(G_c\) is continuous at \(0\), and
\[
 G_c(0)=
 \frac{2J(v-d)}{2v-1}
       \left(\frac{v+d-1}{v-d}-c\right)>0.
\]
Thus the signed excess is positive for all sufficiently small
positive \(e\).  Choosing also \(e\leq\eta/(4J)\) gives
\(0<\Delta_e<\eta\).  Specializing \(J=2m,K=2\) preserves the
integer weights throughout.
Finally, when \(N=K\), all positive weight is selected and
\(Kh^2=Kh\); since \(h\geq d>0\), this forces \(h=1\).
\hfill\(\square\)

\paragraph{Minimum selected order and actual zero clusters.}
For weights \(2a_i\) with \(a_i\in\mathbb N\), selected mass \(2\)
forces exactly one selected positive-weight index, of weight \(2\).
Indeed the selected integer weights sum to \(1\) after division by
\(2\); every other selected weight is zero.  This proves the
single-level hypothesis for every test function, rather than merely
for the first two moments.

Now let \(W\) be a finite set in \(0<\Re w<1\), closed under
\(w\mapsto1-\bar w\), weighted by the actual analytic orders of
\(\zeta\).  Assume positive total order \(N\) and positive horizontal
first moment \(M_1\), and put
\[
 t=M_2/M_1,\qquad y_w=(\Re w-\tfrac12)^2/t,\qquad
 M=M_1^2/M_2.
\]
The proved normalization gives
\(\sum_W a_wy_w=\sum_Wa_wy_w^2=M\).
The proved reflection profile gives, simultaneously for every test,
\[
 \sum_{w\in W}a_w\phi(y_w)
   =\sum_i2b_i\phi(z_i)+C\phi(0),
 \qquad b_i\in\mathbb N,
\]
where \(C\) is the full critical-line order.
If the closed selected order at \(d>0\) is \(2\), its central
contribution is zero, so the integer argument applies to the reflected
profile.  It produces a unique selected right-hand zero of order \(1\)
and its distinct reflected partner of order \(1\), at a single radial
level.  Applying \eqref{eq:single-selected-linear} to the
\emph{full original cluster} gives the sharp linear inverse with its
actual \(N\) and \(M\); for \(d\leq\tfrac12\), its exact cost is
\(\Delta\).  All central orders remain in the unselected layer.
In particular \(N\) may be odd.  No RH, common-height or lower-height
hypothesis is used.

\paragraph{Formal status and scope.}
The exact cost, overshoot identity, linear coefficient, half-cutoff
identity, arbitrarily small finite sharpness witnesses, minimum-order
quantization and actual reflected-zeta applications are maintained
Lean proofs.  The construction proves optimality among finite
moment profiles; it does not assert that its extremizing profiles
are realized by actual zeta zeros.  An independent small-gap estimate
would still be needed for a global simplicity or zero-free result.
The exact one-dimensional Lipschitz-cost mechanism is classical;
see S.~S.~Vallender, \emph{Calculation of the Wasserstein Distance
Between Probability Distributions on the Line},
Theory Probab.\ Appl.\ 18 (1974), 784--786,
\url{https://doi.org/10.1137/1118101}.
The specific coefficient and its quantized application are the
additions established here; the limited literature check does not
establish a priority claim.

\subsection{The complete stability-rate dichotomy for even profiles}
\label{subsec:even-threshold-rate-dichotomy}

The single-level theorem identifies a genuine change of regularity at the
smallest positive selected order.  We can now classify the optimal uniform
rate on every fixed even-mass face, rather than at one numerical example.
All statements in this subsection concern finite profiles with weights
\(2a_i\), \(a_i\in\mathbb N\), nonnegative coordinates, equal first and
second moments \(M\), total mass \(N=2m+2k\), and closed selected mass
\(K=2k>0\).  Fix \(0<d<1\), put
\[
 v=\operatorname{thresholdModelHigh}(2m,2k,d),\qquad
 \Delta=U_d(N,K)-M,
\]
and let \(\mathcal C\) denote the exact globally \(1\)-Lipschitz error
against \((N-K)\delta_d+K\delta_v\).

\paragraph{Theorem (classification at fixed masses and cutoff).}
For \(0\leq\Delta\leq1\), every such profile satisfies
\[
 \mathcal C\leq
 A_{d,m,k}\sqrt{\Delta},\qquad
 A_{d,m,k}=\frac{2v-1}{2(v-d)}+\sqrt{2k(2v-1)}.
\]
The constant is finite and depends only on the fixed constraints.
The optimal uniform exponents are as follows:
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
 \text{fixed mass face}&\text{behaviour near the upper boundary}\\ \hline
 k=1,\ m=0& M=2,\quad \mathcal C=\Delta=0\\
 k=1,\ m\geq1&\text{optimal linear rate, with coefficient }c(d,v)\\
 k\geq2,\ m\geq0&\text{optimal square-root rate}
\end{array}
\]
Here optimality is local and uniform over the entire profile class with
these fixed constraints.  In the last row, for every \(\varepsilon>0\)
there is an admissible profile with
\begin{equation}
 0<\Delta<\varepsilon,\qquad
 0<\mathcal C,\qquad
 \Delta\leq(2m+1)\mathcal C^2.
 \label{eq:even-threshold-rate-lower}
\end{equation}
More strongly, for every \(\alpha>\tfrac12\), \(C\geq0\), and
\(\varepsilon>0\), such a profile has
\(\mathcal C>C\Delta^\alpha\).
In the middle row the analogous assertion holds for every
\(\alpha>1\), and the preceding section already gives the optimal
linear coefficient.

\paragraph{Proof of the upper estimate and rigid face.}
The complete signed packet gives \(\Delta\geq0\) and the previously
proved bound
\[
 \mathcal C\leq
 \frac{(2v-1)\Delta}{2(v-d)}
       +\sqrt{K(2v-1)\Delta}.
\]
For \(0\leq\Delta\leq1\), one has \(\Delta\leq\sqrt{\Delta}\);
factoring the square root gives the displayed constant.
Its denominator is positive because \(v\geq1>d\).

If \(k=1\), minimum selected order forces a single selected carrier,
so the preceding linear theorem applies.  When also \(m=0\), total
order is \(2\).  Applying integer quantization at cutoff \(0\) leaves
one positive-weight coordinate \(y\), of weight \(2\).  Selection at
\(d>0\) gives \(y>0\), and the two moment equations give
\(2y=2y^2=M\).  Hence \(y=1\) and every test statistic is exactly
\(2\phi(1)\).  For \(m\geq1\), the preceding fixed-weight sharpness
family supplies arbitrarily small positive deficits with error
greater than \(\Delta/2\).
If \(\alpha>1\), continuity of
\(2C x^{\alpha-1}\) at \(0\) makes it less than \(1\) on a sufficiently
small positive interval.  The same witnesses then satisfy
\(C\Delta^\alpha<\Delta/2<\mathcal C\).

\paragraph{Uniform fixed-weight construction for \(k\geq2\).}
Write \(J=2m\), \(K=2k\), and, for small \(q>0\), set
\[
 u=d-q^2,\qquad a=\operatorname{thresholdModelHigh}(J,K,u),\qquad
 c=a-\frac{(2a-1)q^2}{1+q^2},\qquad
 R=\frac{(2a-1)q}{1+q^2}.
\]
Use the four coordinates and their fixed natural orders
\[
\begin{array}{c|cccc}
 \text{coordinate}&u&c+R&c-R&a\\
 \text{order}&m&1&1&k-2 .
\end{array}
\]
Their weights are twice these orders.  The order \(k-2\) may be zero.
The rational rotation obeys the exact identity
\[
 c^2+R^2-c=a^2-a.
\]
Consequently the two selected unit carriers preserve their contribution
to the signed second-minus-first moment.  The defining identity
\(Ju^2+Ka^2=Ju+Ka\) proves the full moment law, with every one of
the four contributions retained.  Total mass is \(J+K\).
As \(q\to0\), the three selected coordinates tend to \(v\geq1>d\);
hence they remain above \(d\) on a sufficiently small interval.
Meanwhile \(0\leq u<d\) there.  Thus the closed selected mass remains
exactly \(K\).  The value \(q=0\) is used only for continuity; the
witnesses always have \(q>0\).

Let \(\Delta_0=Je-K(a-v)\) with \(e=q^2\), the original two-site
moment deficit.  The full new deficit is exactly
\[
 \Delta_q=\Delta_0+
            \frac{4(2a-1)q^2}{1+q^2}.
\]
The previous two-site bounds give \(0\leq\Delta_0\leq2Jq^2\).
They include \(J=0\), where \(\Delta_0=0\) directly.
Also \(a\geq1\), and the model moment equation with \(K\geq4\)
gives \(4(a-1)\leq J\).  Therefore
\[
 0<\Delta_q\leq(4J+4)q^2=(8m+4)q^2.
\]
No positive rotation correction has been omitted.

The fixed test \(\phi_v(y)=|y-v|\) attains the exact error.
Expanding its signed error gives
\[
 \mathcal C_q=Jq^2+2|c+R-v|+2|c-R-v|+(K-4)|a-v|.
\]
All displayed terms are nonnegative.  The middle pair is at least
\(2|(c+R)-(c-R)|=4R\).  Since \(a\geq1\) and \(0<q\leq1\),
one has \(R\geq q/2\), and hence \(\mathcal C_q\geq2q>0\).
Combining this with the full deficit bound proves
\eqref{eq:even-threshold-rate-lower}.  Taking \(q\) sufficiently small
also gives \(\Delta_q<\varepsilon\), preserving the same cutoff,
total order and selected order.

Finally put \(L=2m+1>0\).  If \(\alpha>\tfrac12\), then
\(L C^2x^{2\alpha-1}\to0\) as \(x\downarrow0\).
Choose a witness with
\(L C^2\Delta_q^{2\alpha-1}<1\).
An assumed bound \(\mathcal C_q\leq C\Delta_q^\alpha\) would imply
\[
 0<\Delta_q
 \leq L\mathcal C_q^2
 \leq L C^2\Delta_q^{2\alpha}
 <\Delta_q,
\]
a contradiction.  This proves the optimal exponent on every stated
fixed face, including \(m=0\).
\hfill\(\square\)

\paragraph{Meaning and formal scope.}
The classification separates what quantized moment data alone can force:
minimum positive selected order enforces linear stability, while a second
selected unit carrier allows a moment-preserving split of square-root size.
The maintained Lean statements include the finite family, its cutoff window,
the quantitative deficit and statistic bounds, arbitrarily small witnesses,
both no-better-H\"older assertions, the rigid face, and the uniform
half-power upper estimate.  The sharpness examples are finite profiles;
their realization by actual zeta zeros is not asserted.  The general
moment and transport mechanisms are classical background, and the
literature comparison establishes no priority claim for this particular
fixed-weight classification.

\subsection{A sharp radial collision threshold}
\label{subsec:quantized-radial-collision}

Let \(S\) be finite, let \(a_i\ge0\), and suppose
\[
 0<d<1,\qquad
 N=\sum_{i\in S}a_i,\quad
 M=\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i=\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i^2,\quad
 K=\sum_{i\in S}a_i I_d(y_i)>0.
\]
Write \(v=\operatorname{thresholdModelHigh}(N-K,K,d)\),
\(U=d(N-K)+Kv\), and \(\Delta=U-M\).  The proved upper ledger gives
\(v\ge1>d\) and \(\Delta\ge0\).  Define the selected mean and
unnormalized variance by
\[
 \mu=\frac1K\sum_{i\in S}a_i I_d(y_i)y_i,\qquad
 V=\sum_{i\in S}a_i I_d(y_i)(y_i-\mu)^2.
\]
All sums in this subsection have the displayed finite support \(S\).

\paragraph{Theorem (global signed variance budget).}
Set
\[
 {\cal B}_{d,v,K}(\Delta)
 =(2v-1)\Delta-\frac{\Delta^2}{K}
   +\frac{\max\{\Delta-K(v-d),0\}^2}{K}.
 \tag{VC1}\label{eq:radial-collision-budget}
\]
Then \(V\le{\cal B}_{d,v,K}(\Delta)\).  This holds for real
nonnegative weights and requires no small-deficit assumption.
If \(\Delta\le K(v-d)\), equivalently \(M\ge dN\), the maximum term
vanishes, leaving the quadratic improvement
\(V\le(2v-1)\Delta-\Delta^2/K\).

\paragraph{Proof.}
Retain both low-layer quantities
\[
 L=\sum_{i\in S}a_i(d-y_i)(1-I_d(y_i)),\qquad
 Q=\sum_{i\in S}a_i(d-y_i)^2(1-I_d(y_i)).
\]
They are nonnegative, including when some coordinates are negative.
The first-moment decomposition and the selected parallel-axis identity give
\[
 \Delta-L=K(v-\mu),\qquad
 \sum_{i\in S}a_iI_d(y_i)(y_i-v)^2=V+K(\mu-v)^2.
\]
Substitution in the complete upper packet yields the exact identity
\begin{equation}
 (2v-1)\Delta
 =V+Q+2(v-d)L+\frac{(L-\Delta)^2}{K}.
 \tag{VC2}\label{eq:radial-collision-exact}
\end{equation}
Put \(t=\Delta-K(v-d)\).  Multiplying by \(K>0\) and completing
the square, without deleting the mean drift, gives
\[
 K\{V-(2v-1)\Delta\}+\Delta^2
 =-KQ-L^2+2tL\le\max\{t,0\}^2.
\]
For \(t\ge0\) the last inequality is \((L-t)^2\ge0\);
for \(t<0\) use \(L\ge0\).  Division by \(K\) proves
\eqref{eq:radial-collision-budget}.

\paragraph{Theorem (quantized collision rigidity and linear recovery).}
Suppose now that \(a_i=2n_i\), with \(n_i\) nonnegative integers.
Assume distinct positive-weight selected coordinates are separated by
\(g\ge0\).  If the selected law has at least two radial levels, then
\begin{equation}
 V\ge\frac{2(K-2)}K g^2.
 \tag{VC3}\label{eq:radial-collision-lower}
\end{equation}
Consequently
\begin{equation}
 {\cal B}_{d,v,K}(\Delta)<\frac{2(K-2)}K g^2
 \tag{VC4}\label{eq:radial-collision-rigidity}
\end{equation}
forces every positive-weight selected coordinate to have the same
value \(h\).  Its selected law is exactly \(K\delta_h\).
The previously proved single-level theorem therefore gives, for every
globally \(1\)-Lipschitz real test \(\phi\),
\[
 \left|\sum_{i\in S}a_i\phi(y_i)
       -\{(N-K)\phi(d)+K\phi(v)\}\right|
 \le
 \max\left\{1,\frac{v+d-1}{v-d}\right\}\Delta.
\]
Here \(v-d>0\) and \(K>0\).  The interesting separated nonconstant
case has \(K\ge4\); the earlier minimum-order theorem already handles
\(K=2\).

\paragraph{Proof.}
Choose a positive-weight selected level \(z\), let \(A\) be its
entire selected weight, and let \(B=K-A\).  If the selected law is
nonconstant, both \(A\) and \(B\) are at least two.  On the selected
sites outside this level put
\[
 R=\sum_{\substack{i\in S\\y_i\ge d,\ y_i\ne z}}a_i(y_i-z),
 \qquad
 T=\sum_{\substack{i\in S\\y_i\ge d,\ y_i\ne z}}a_i(y_i-z)^2.
\]
All sites at \(z\) contribute zero to these two sums.  The retained
parallel-axis identity gives \(KV=KT-R^2\).  The weighted square
inequality gives \(R^2\le BT\), and separation gives \(T\ge Bg^2\).
Thus
\[
 KV\ge AT\ge ABg^2\ge2(K-2)g^2,
 \qquad
 AB-2(K-2)=(A-2)(B-2)\ge0.
\]
This proves \eqref{eq:radial-collision-lower}.  Combining it with
\eqref{eq:radial-collision-budget} contradicts
\eqref{eq:radial-collision-rigidity} unless the selected law is a
Dirac.  Applying the maintained sharp single-level inverse proves
the final bound.

\paragraph{Sharpness inside the equal-moment law.}
For every fixed integer \(k\ge2\) and fixed \(0<d<1\), put
\(\rho=k-1\), \(K=2k=2(\rho+1)\), and choose
\[
 0<q\le\min\left\{1,\frac{1-d}{2(\rho+1)}\right\}.
\]
Take two sites of weights \(2,2\rho\) at
\[
 x=\frac{1+\rho q}{1+\rho q^2},\qquad
 z=\frac{1-q}{1+\rho q^2}.
\]
Then \(d<z<x\), so both sites are selected, \(N=K\), and
\[
 M=\frac K{1+\rho q^2},\quad
 \Delta=\frac{K\rho q^2}{1+\rho q^2}>0,\quad
 g=x-z=\frac{(\rho+1)q}{1+\rho q^2}>0.
\]
Direct expansion proves equal first and second moments and
\[
 V=\frac{K\rho q^2}{(1+\rho q^2)^2}
   ={\cal B}_{d,1,K}(\Delta)
   =\frac{2(K-2)}K g^2.
\]
For completeness, \(d<z\) follows from \(q^2\le q\) and
\(2(\rho+1)q\le1-d\); the selected mean is
\(1/(1+\rho q^2)\ge d\), so the maximum correction in
\({\cal B}_{d,1,K}\) is zero.  The other identities follow by
clearing the positive denominator.
For any prescribed \(\eta>0\), the explicit choice
\[
 q=\frac12\min\left\{1,\frac{1-d}{2(\rho+1)},
                           \frac{\eta}{2K\rho}\right\}
\]
also gives \(0<\Delta<\eta\): use
\(\Delta\le K\rho q^2\le K\rho q\le\eta/2\).
Thus the constant in \eqref{eq:radial-collision-lower} and the strict
inequality in \eqref{eq:radial-collision-rigidity} are sharp, even
inside the admissible moment class.  These examples have \(N=K\);
no sharpness assertion on every fixed face \(N>K\) is needed here.

\paragraph{Actual zeta orders and odd total mass.}
The maintained theorem \texttt{zeta\_selected\_collision\_linear}
applies this criterion to any finite open-strip set closed under
horizontal reflection, with actual analytic orders and coordinates
\(y_w=(\Re w-\tfrac12)^2/t\), \(t>0\), whose first two normalized
moments agree.  The previously proved normalization supplies such
\(t\) when the horizontal moment is positive.
Fold the off-line pairs to the right half of the strip.  Their
weights are even; the full central weight \(C\) stays at coordinate
zero.  Since \(d>0\), every test \(I_d\phi\) vanishes at zero.
Hence selected mass, selected mean, selected variance, and the
selected law transfer exactly to the even profile.  Apply
\eqref{eq:radial-collision-lower} there and
\eqref{eq:radial-collision-budget} to the \emph{full} set, using its
full \(N\), including \(C\).  This proves the Dirac conclusion and
the full linear inverse without an even-total assumption, RH, or
a common-height hypothesis.  One radial level need not mean one
zero: different heights can share a squared horizontal coordinate.

This supplies a quantitative criterion for upgrading the general
square-root inverse to a linear inverse.  It does not independently
supply radial separation or the required deficit for actual zeros.
Variance identities, finite moment optimization, and one-dimensional
transport are classical; priority for this particular combined
quantized criterion is not asserted.\footnote{For the general moment
inequality framework, see D.~Bertsimas and I.~Popescu,
\emph{Optimal Inequalities in Probability Theory: A Convex Optimization
Approach}, SIAM J.~Optim.~15 (2005), 780--804,
DOI 10.1137/S1052623401399903.}

\subsection{Sharp packing at every radial support size}
\label{subsec:all-r-radial-packing}

The collision threshold extends to every number of distinct selected levels.
For an integer \(r\ge1\), define
\[
 C_r=\frac{r(r^2-1)}{12},\qquad
 \epsilon_r=\begin{cases}1/4,&r\text{ even},\\0,&r\text{ odd},\end{cases}
 \qquad
 F(r,q,K)=qC_r+\frac{qr(K-qr)}K\epsilon_r.
 \tag{RP1}\label{eq:all-r-variance-floor}
\]
Here \(q>0\) is a lower bound on each atom weight and \(K\ge qr>0\).

\paragraph{Theorem (sharp separated weighted variance).}
Let \(y_0\le\cdots\le y_{r-1}\) be real sites with
\(y_{i+1}-y_i\ge g\ge0\), and let \(w_i\ge q>0\). Put
\(K=\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}w_i\) and
\(\mu=K^{-1}\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}w_i y_i\). Then
\begin{equation}
 \sum_{i=0}^{r-1}w_i(y_i-\mu)^2\ge g^2 F(r,q,K).
 \tag{RP2}\label{eq:all-r-packing}
\end{equation}
For every \(r\ge1\), \(q>0\), \(K\ge qr\), and \(g\ge0\), equality
is attained by consecutive sites of step \(g\), weight \(q\) at every
site, and all surplus weight \(K-qr\) at a middle site.
For \(g=0\) the attaining coordinates coincide; the statement then
concerns indexed sites, with zero floor.

\paragraph{Proof.}
The complete pairwise identity is
\[
 K\sum_iw_i(y_i-\mu)^2
 =\frac12\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}\sum_{j=0}^{r-1}w_iw_j(y_i-y_j)^2.
\]
Summing adjacent gaps shows
\(\lvert y_i-y_j\rvert\ge g\lvert i-j\rvert\).
Since every pair coefficient is nonnegative, compression to
\(u_i=i-(r-1)/2\) can only decrease this numerator, by a factor \(g^2\).
The consecutive grid satisfies
\[
 \sum_i u_i=0,\qquad \sum_i u_i^2=C_r,\qquad
 u_i^2\ge\epsilon_r,
\]
with equality in the last inequality at a middle site.
Write \(e_i=w_i-q\ge0\), \(E=\sum_i e_i=K-qr\),
\(T=\sum_i e_i u_i^2\), and \(H=\sum_i e_i u_i\).
The full grid numerator is exactly
\[
 K\sum_iw_i u_i^2-\Bigl(\sum_iw_i u_i\Bigr)^2
   =qKC_r+qrT+(ET-H^2).
\]
The last term is
\(\frac12\sum_{i,j=0}^{r-1}e_i e_j(u_i-u_j)^2\ge0\);
it includes the entire squared-mean subtraction. Also
\(T\ge E\epsilon_r\). Thus the numerator is at least
\(qKC_r+qrE\epsilon_r=KF(r,q,K)\), proving
\eqref{eq:all-r-packing}. Concentrating all \(e_i\) at a middle
site makes both inequalities equalities.

\paragraph{Corollary (the complete selected-level obstruction).}
Use the equal-moment profile and full deficit notation of
\eqref{eq:radial-collision-budget}, with even natural weights \(a_i=2n_i\).
Let \(r\) be the number of distinct coordinates with
\(a_iI_d(y_i)>0\), and suppose distinct such coordinates are separated
by \(g\ge0\). Then \(r\ge1\), \(K\ge2r\), and
\begin{equation}
 g^2\Phi(r,K)\le {\cal B}_{d,v,K}(\Delta),\qquad
 \Phi(r,K)=\frac{r(r^2-1)}6+
 \begin{cases}\dfrac{r(K-2r)}{2K},&r\text{ even},\\0,&r\text{ odd}.\end{cases}
 \tag{RP3}\label{eq:all-r-selected}
\end{equation}
Indeed, combine all positive selected weights at the same coordinate.
This preserves every finite weighted statistic, including mass, mean
and variance. Each resulting atom has weight at least two.
Enumerate the resulting finite support increasingly, apply
\eqref{eq:all-r-packing} with \(q=2\), and then use the full budget.
Thus a proposed support size is excluded whenever its left side in
\eqref{eq:all-r-selected} exceeds the budget. For example
\[
 \Phi(1,K)=0,\quad \Phi(2,K)=2(K-2)/K,\quad
 \Phi(3,K)=4,\quad \Phi(4,K)=12-16/K.
\]
The case \(r=0\) is excluded by \(K>0\). The \(r=1\) case has zero
variance; in the all-selected equal-moment law its positive coordinate
must be one. These are radial-level statements, not simplicity claims.

\paragraph{Actual reflected zeta orders.}
The maintained theorem \texttt{zeta\_selected\_radial\_packing}
proves \eqref{eq:all-r-selected} for the same finite reflected
open-strip zeta profiles as the preceding collision theorem.
All selected statistics transfer to the even off-line profile.
Equality of all finite weighted statistics also preserves the
positive-weight radial support: test each coordinate by its singleton
indicator. Central weight lies at zero and is unselected because \(d>0\).
Apply the lower floor to the folded selected profile and the upper
budget to the full set. Its full \(N\), including every central order,
is retained, so odd total order is allowed.

\paragraph{A normalization tool for moment-law sharpness.}
The separately compiled \texttt{Finset.centered\_moment\_normalization}
gives the following exact construction. On a finite support \(S\), suppose
\(\sum_{i\in S}w_i=K>0\), \(\sum_{i\in S}w_i z_i=0\), and
\(\sum_{i\in S}w_i z_i^2=F>0\). For any real \(p\), set
\[
 D=1+(F/K)p^2>0,\qquad y_i=(1+pz_i)/D.
\]
Then
\[
 \sum_{i\in S}w_i y_i=\sum_{i\in S}w_i y_i^2=K/D,\qquad
 \sum_{i\in S}w_i(y_i-1/D)^2=(p/D)^2F.
\]
\paragraph{Theorem (complete moment-law attainment range).}
Fix an integer \(k\ge1\), an integer \(r\ge0\), \(0<d<1\), and
\(\eta>0\). Put \(K=2k\). There exist positive natural orders
\(n_i\), coordinates \(y_i>d\) indexed by \(0\le i<r\), and
\(g,M>0\), such that
\[
 \sum_{i=0}^{r-1}2n_i=K,\qquad
 \sum_{i=0}^{r-1}2n_i y_i
 =\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}2n_i y_i^2=M,\qquad
 y_j-y_i=g(j-i),
\]
with exactly \(r\) positive-weight selected radial levels and
\begin{equation}
 0<g<\eta,\qquad 0<\Delta=K-M<\eta,\qquad
 V=g^2\Phi(r,K)={\cal B}_{d,1,K}(\Delta),
 \tag{RP4}\label{eq:all-r-moment-attainment}
\end{equation}
if and only if \(2\le r\le k\).
All sites are selected, so \(N=K\) and the diagonal reference is \(v=1\).
Thus the variance floor and the complete deficit budget can be attained
simultaneously with both gap and positive deficit arbitrarily small,
at every fixed admissible support size and even total mass.

\paragraph{Proof of sufficiency.}
Let \(2\le r\le k\), put \(u_i=i-(r-1)/2\), and choose a middle
index \(\ell\) with \(u_\ell^2=\epsilon_r\). Set
\[
 n_i=1+(k-r)\mathbf1_{i=\ell},\qquad
 w_i=2n_i,\qquad E=K-2r,\qquad
 H=Eu_\ell,\qquad z_i=u_i-H/K.
\]
These orders are positive natural numbers and \(\sum_iw_i=K\).
The exact grid identities and the full centered-variance calculation give
\[
 \sum_iw_i z_i=0,\qquad
 F:=\sum_iw_i z_i^2=\Phi(r,K)>0,\qquad z_j-z_i=j-i.
\]
Put \(\rho=F/K>0\) and
\(A=1+\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}|z_i|\), so \(|z_i|\le A\).
Choose the explicit positive parameter
\[
 p=\frac12\min\left\{1,\frac{1-d}{2(A+\rho+1)},
                       \frac{\eta}{2K\rho},\eta\right\},
 \quad D=1+\rho p^2,\quad
 y_i=\frac{1+pz_i}{D},\quad
 g=\frac pD,\quad M=\frac KD.
\]
Every displayed denominator is positive. Since \(p\le1\),
\[
 1+pz_i-dD
 \ge1-d-p(A+\rho)
 \ge\frac{1-d}{2}+p>0.
\]
Thus \(y_i>d\) at every site. Moreover \(D\ge1\) and \(0<p<\eta\),
so \(0<g\le p<\eta\). Exact normalization gives equal first and
second moments \(M\), mean \(1/D\), and variance \(g^2F\).
The coordinates are spaced by \(g>0\), hence have exactly \(r\)
distinct values and satisfy the selected separation condition.
The deficit is
\[
 \Delta=\frac{K\rho p^2}{D}>0,\qquad
 \Delta\le K\rho p^2\le K\rho p\le\eta/2<\eta.
\]
Because all positive weights have coordinates above \(d\), their mean
\(1/D\) is above \(d\). Hence
\(\Delta-K(1-d)\le0\), so the maximum term in the full budget is
proved to vanish. Keeping its quadratic mean correction gives
\[
 {\cal B}_{d,1,K}(\Delta)
   =\Delta-\Delta^2/K
   =K\rho p^2/D^2
   =g^2F.
\]
This proves every assertion in \eqref{eq:all-r-moment-attainment}.

\paragraph{Necessity and small cases.}
Positive natural orders give \(K=\sum_i2n_i\ge2r\), hence \(r\le k\).
For \(r=0\), the total is zero, contrary to \(K=2k>0\).
For \(r=1\), the sole positive coordinate \(c>d>0\) obeys
\(Kc^2=Kc\); hence \(c=1\), \(M=K\), and the deficit is zero.
More generally this is the maintained one-radius rigidity theorem.
Thus a positive-deficit attainer requires \(r\ge2\).
The Lean declaration
\texttt{thresholdRadialPacking\_attainers\_iff} proves this exact
necessary-and-sufficient range. Its attainer predicate includes the
admissibility law, positive orders, strict cutoff, total mass,
\(0<g<\eta\), exact pairwise spacing, separation, exact selected
radial cardinality, \(0<\Delta<\eta\), and both variance equalities.
The result concerns the all-selected face \(N=K\), with the gap chosen
by the construction.

Spacing arguments and moment inequalities are classical. For comparison,
L.~Moser's \emph{Notes on Number Theory IV: On the Multiplicity of
Representations of Numbers by Sums}, Canad.~Math.~Bull.~6 (1963),
163--166, DOI 10.4153/CMB-1963-014-9, uses spacing with an upper
multiplicity cap. Here a lower atom quantum gives the displayed
parity-dependent floor and its selected-profile consequence.
Priority for the combined criterion is not asserted.

\subsection{Sharp radial support and exact two-layer reconstruction}
\label{sec:radial-support-inverse}

Fix a finite index set \(S\), nonnegative weights \(a_i\), nonnegative
coordinates \(y_i\), and \(0<d<1\).  Throughout this subsection put
\[
 N=\sum_{i\in S}a_i,\qquad
 M=\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i=\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i^2>0,\qquad
 K=\sum_{i\in S}a_i I_d(y_i),\qquad I_d(y)=\mathbf1_{y\ge d}.
\]
The radial support is the finite set
\[
 \mathcal R(S,a,y)=\{y_i:i\in S,\ a_i>0\}.
\]
It includes zero whenever a positive-weight site has coordinate zero.
Thus a radial level always means a value of positive weight, not necessarily
a strictly positive coordinate.  No sites of weight zero are counted.

\paragraph{Lemma (rigidity and the third radial level).}
Always \(K\le N\) and \(\#\mathcal R\ge1\).  If all positive-weight
selected sites have the same coordinate \(v\), then \(v\ge1\) and
\(K\le M\).  Consequently \(M<K\) forces two distinct selected radial
levels, and \(M<K<N\) forces at least three radial levels.  If
\(\#\mathcal R\le1\), then \(M=N=K\).

\paragraph{Proof.}
The inequality \(0\le I_d\le1\) gives \(K\le N\); positive first
moment supplies a positive-weight site.  The equal-moment witness lemma
supplies a positive-weight coordinate above every \(r\in(0,1)\).
In particular selected sites exist.  If their common value \(v\) were
less than one, then \(d\le v<r=(v+1)/2<1\); the witness at \(r\)
would be a selected site with coordinate strictly greater than \(v\),
a contradiction.  Hence every selected contribution satisfies
\(a_i I_d(y_i)=a_i\le a_i y_i\), whereas every unselected contribution
satisfies \(0\le a_i y_i\).  Summation gives \(K\le M\).
Its contrapositive supplies two distinct selected values if \(M<K\).
If also \(K<N\), some positive weight is unselected; its coordinate is
less than \(d\), different from both selected values.  Finally, if the
whole support has at most one value \(v\), the moment identity gives
\(M=vM\).  Since \(M>0\), \(v=1\), so both total and selected mass
are \(M\).  This also proves the claimed one-level rigidity.

\paragraph{Theorem (complete sharp radial-support classification).}
Suppose now that the weights are even nonnegative integers, \(N=2n\),
\(K=2k\), where \(n,k\) are integers and \(0<k\le n\).  Use the
previously established endpoints
\begin{align*}
 R_d(K)&=d(K-2)+1+\sqrt{1+2(K-2)d(1-d)},\\
 U_d(N,K)&=d(N-K)+\frac K2+
   \sqrt{\frac{K^2}{4}+K(N-K)d(1-d)}.
\end{align*}
Within the complete feasible region
\begin{equation}
 R_d(K)\le M,\qquad
 \begin{cases}M\le K,&N=K,\\M<U_d(N,K),&K<N,\end{cases}
 \label{eq:radial-support-region}
\end{equation}
the exact minimum number of radial levels is
\begin{equation}
 s_{\min}(M,N,K)=
 \begin{cases}
 1,&M=N=K,\\
 3,&M<K<N,\\
 2,&\text{otherwise in the feasible region}.
 \end{cases}
 \label{eq:radial-support-minimum}
\end{equation}
Every minimum is attained by a finite profile with the prescribed moments,
total weight and selected weight.  More precisely, for every integer
\(L\ge0\), such a profile with at most \(L\) radial levels exists if
and only if \eqref{eq:radial-support-region} holds and
\(s_{\min}(M,N,K)\le L\).

\paragraph{Proof.}
The preceding lemma gives all lower bounds: one level requires
\(M=N=K\), three are necessary when \(M<K<N\), and otherwise at
least two are necessary.  For \(M=N=K\), one site of weight \(K\)
at coordinate one attains the bound.  If \(N=K\) and \(M<K\), use
the preceding two-site arithmetic feasibility theorem.  Its selected
weight is \(K\), and its total is at least \(K\).  Were its total
strictly greater than \(K\), the preceding three-level necessity would
contradict its having only two sites.  Its total is therefore exactly
\(N=K\), giving the required two-level realization.  If \(K<N\)
and \(M\ge K\), the preceding fixed-total upper-branch construction
uses two sites and realizes every \(K\le M<U_d(N,K)\).  Finally, if
\(M<K<N\), the preceding bounded construction uses at most three
sites, including its zero-coordinate padding, and fixes the total at
\(N\).  Three levels are necessary, so its support has exactly three.
These constructions exhaust \eqref{eq:radial-support-region}, including
its closed lower face, its closed \(N=K\) upper face and the strict
\(K<N\) upper face.  The old feasibility theorem supplies necessity
of the region; the lower bounds and attaining constructions prove the
assertion for every \(L\), with no finite-domain restriction.

\paragraph{Theorem (unique two-layer inverse and its exact domain).}
For this theorem the weights and totals may be real.  Suppose \(0<K<N\)
and write \(J=N-K>0\).  A two-layer profile is a pair satisfying
\begin{equation}
 0\le u<d\le v,\qquad Ju+Kv=Ju^2+Kv^2=M.
 \label{eq:two-layer-profile}
\end{equation}
Such a pair exists if and only if
\begin{equation}
 K\le M<U_d(N,K).
 \label{eq:two-layer-domain}
\end{equation}
When it exists, \(0<M<N\), \(v\ge1\), and the pair is unique:
\begin{align}
 u&=\frac{M-\sqrt{KM(N-M)/(N-K)}}{N},
 \label{eq:two-layer-low-inverse}\\
 v&=\frac{M+\sqrt{(N-K)M(N-M)/K}}{N}.
 \label{eq:two-layer-high-inverse}
\end{align}
Every denominator in these formulas is strictly positive on the stated
domain.  The case \(N=K\) belongs to the support classification above
and is excluded from this inverse theorem.

\paragraph{Proof of necessity and inversion.}
The moment balance is
\[
 K v(v-1)=J u(1-u)\ge0.
\]
Since \(v\ge d>0\), it follows that \(v\ge1\), hence
\(M=Ju+Kv\ge K>0\).  Keeping both layers gives
\[
 N-M=J(u-1)^2+K(v-1)^2>0;
\]
the strict inequality follows from \(J>0\) and \(u<d<1\).
The previously proved strict fixed-total upper inequality applies to these
two real-weight sites and gives \(M<U_d(N,K)\).
For the inverse, expansion of both moments, retaining the mixed term, gives
\begin{equation}
 NM-M^2=JK(v-u)^2.
 \label{eq:two-layer-exact-variance}
\end{equation}
Since \(v-u>0\), the two positive square roots occurring in
\eqref{eq:two-layer-low-inverse}--\eqref{eq:two-layer-high-inverse}
are respectively \(K(v-u)\) and \(J(v-u)\).  Substitution into
\(M=Ju+Kv\), with \(N=J+K\), gives the displayed formulas and
uniqueness.  No mixed contribution has been discarded.

\paragraph{Proof of existence.}
For \(0\le u\le d\), use the continuous high root
\[
 v(u)=\frac12+\sqrt{\frac14+\frac JK u(1-u)}\ge1
\]
and the common moment \(F(u)=Ju+Kv(u)\).  This is the same
root-balance construction as the fixed-total models, now with arbitrary
positive real \(J,K\); equivalently it is one half of the installed
model mass with parameters \(J,K\).  Its endpoint values are
\[
 F(0)=K,\qquad F(d)=U_d(N,K).
\]
The intermediate value theorem supplies every mass in
\eqref{eq:two-layer-domain}; because \(M<U_d(N,K)\), a chosen
parameter realizing \(M\) cannot equal \(d\).  Thus \(u<d\)
and \(v(u)\ge1>d\), proving \eqref{eq:two-layer-profile}.
The already proved inverse gives uniqueness, including the lower endpoint
\(M=K\).

\paragraph{Finite-profile adapter.}
Suppose all positive-weight coordinates in a finite profile belong to
\(\{u,v\}\), where \(u<d\le v\).  For every function \(f\)
on the real line, the exact finite identity is
\begin{equation}
 \sum_{i\in S}a_i f(y_i)=(N-K)f(u)+Kf(v).
 \label{eq:two-layer-function-adapter}
\end{equation}
