Indeed, at a site of positive weight the threshold indicator distinguishes
\(u\) from \(v\); at a site of weight zero both sides of the
sitewise decomposition vanish.  Summing this decomposition gives
\eqref{eq:two-layer-function-adapter}.  Taking \(f(y)=y\) and
\(f(y)=y^2\) gives both equations in \eqref{eq:two-layer-profile}.
This adapter retains all finite coefficients, including zero-weight sites,
and is not an approximation of the underlying distribution.

\paragraph{Theorem (actual five-point and six-point forcing).}
Let \(W\) be a finite reflected subset of the open critical strip, with
actual orders \(a_\rho=\operatorname{ord}_\rho\zeta\), total
\(N=\sum_{\rho\in W}a_\rho>0\), and positive horizontal moment
\(M_1=\sum_{\rho\in W}a_\rho(\Re\rho-1/2)^2>0\).
Put
\[
 Q=\sum_{\rho\in W}a_\rho(\Re\rho-1/2)^4,\quad
 t=Q/M_1>0,\quad \kappa=M_1^2/Q>0,\quad
 y_\rho=(\Re\rho-1/2)^2/t,\quad
 K_d=\sum_{\rho\in W}a_\rho I_d(y_\rho).
\]
If \(\kappa<K_d<N\), then \(W\) contains at least five distinct
points of positive analytic order.  If every positive-order point of
\(W\) is off the critical line, it contains at least six such points.
No common-ordinate assumption and no evenness assumption on \(N\) or
on individual orders is required.

\paragraph{Proof.}
Exact rescaling gives
\[
 \sum_{\rho\in W}a_\rho y_\rho
 =\sum_{\rho\in W}a_\rho y_\rho^2=\kappa.
\]
The three-level lemma therefore supplies two distinct selected values
and a third unselected value.  The selected coordinates are at least
\(d>0\), so each corresponding point is offcritical and differs from
its reflection \(\rho\mapsto1-\overline\rho\).  Reflection preserves
both the radial coordinate and the actual order.  The two selected values
therefore give two disjoint reflection pairs, accounting for four distinct
positive-order points.  A point at the third radial value differs from all
four and gives the fifth.  If it too is offcritical, its reflection is a
sixth distinct positive-order point.  The finite cardinality argument keeps
all these distinctions explicitly; the fifth point may instead be central.
The conclusion counts distinct points, not their total multiplicity.

\paragraph{Theorem (actual two-layer reconstruction).}
Reflection is unnecessary for the inverse statement.  Retain a finite strip
set with the positive moments above.  Suppose its positive-order radial
coordinates lie in \(\{u,v\}\), with \(0\le u<d\le v\), and
\(K_d<N\).  Then \(0<K_d\le\kappa<N\), and
\eqref{eq:two-layer-low-inverse}--\eqref{eq:two-layer-high-inverse}
recover \(u,v\) with \(M=\kappa\) and \(K=K_d\).
Indeed the equal-moment witness gives \(K_d>0\), the exact adapter gives
both layer equations, and the inverse theorem applies with the actual total
order.  Thus odd total orders and possible critical-line points are covered.
All-critical clusters have \(M_1=0\) and are outside these hypotheses.

The thirty-two proof bodies are maintained in
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdRadialWitness.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdRadialCard.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdRadialMinimal.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/ReflectedRadialCard.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdTwoLayerInverse.lean}, and
\texttt{Assembly/CarmichaelRadialSupport.lean}.
The elementary two-point mean--variance inverse is classical.  The specific
conclusions here are the complete arithmetic support classification and its
observable-conditioned actual zero-cluster geometry; broad priority is not
asserted.  Arithmetic feasibility witnesses are not asserted to occur as
zeta-zero configurations, and the hypotheses \(\kappa<K_d<N\) are not
asserted unconditionally.  These results supply geometric restrictions, not
an independent global observable estimate proving simplicity.  The numerical
simplicity window and the certified \(7/8\) boundary are unchanged.

\subsection{Fixed total order and sharp arithmetic energy}
\label{sec:fixed-total-energy-sharpness}

The preceding fixed-total feasibility theorem does not automatically increase
the sharp nonlinear energy lower bound.  We now determine exactly when that
bound remains approachable at a prescribed total order.  The issue is a fixed
parameter problem: the total order stays fixed as the approximation error
tends to zero.

Fix \(0<d<1\), integers \(k>0\), \(n\ge0\), and \(M\ge K=2k\).
For finite nonnegative even weights and nonnegative coordinates write
\[
 N=\sum_{i\in S}a_i=2n,\qquad
 \sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i=\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i^2=M,\qquad
 \sum_{i\in S}a_i I_d(y_i)=K.
\]
Their energy and the positive-branch root are
\[
 E=\sum_{i\in S}a_i y_i(y_i-1)^2,\qquad
 z=\frac{d+\sqrt{d^2+4(1-d)M/K}}{2}\ge1.
\]
The installed lower bound is
\(E\ge F_d(M,K)=M(1-d)(z-1)\).
Call the bound approachable at total \(2n\) if, for every
\(\epsilon>0\), a finite profile with exactly these \(M,N,K\)
has \(E<F_d(M,K)+\epsilon\).

\paragraph{Theorem (complete fixed-total sharpness criterion).}
The energy bound is approachable at total \(2n\) if and only if some
integer \(m\ge0\) satisfies
\begin{equation}
 M-2kz=2dm,\qquad
 \begin{cases}
 n\ge k,&m=0,\\
 n\ge k+m+1,&m>0.
 \end{cases}
 \label{eq:fixed-total-energy-criterion}
\end{equation}
For \(m=0\), one has \(z=1\), \(M=K\), and an exact zero-energy
profile with at most two sites exists.  For \(m>0\), every admissible
profile has \(E>F_d(M,K)\), but profiles with at most four sites approach
this bound at every permitted fixed total.  More strongly, on each lattice
row \(M-2kz=2dm\), fixed-total feasibility and fixed-total
approachability are equivalent.  Thus the prescribed total introduces an
exact feasibility threshold, but no positive additive energy correction on
any feasible lattice row.

\paragraph{Proof of the signed order threshold.}
The root relation and lattice identity give
\begin{equation}
 M=2(kz+md),\qquad kz(z-1)=md(1-d).
 \label{eq:fixed-total-lattice-moments}
\end{equation}
When \(m>0\), these imply \(M>K\).  The equal-moment variance identity
\(N-M=\sum_{i\in S}a_i(y_i-1)^2\ge0\) then gives \(N>K\),
so there is a positive-weight unselected site.  Keep the complete signed
cutoff packet
\[
 T_{d,z}(y)=(y-z)^2-(d-z)^2(1-I_d(y)).
\]
Its selected branch is \((y-z)^2\ge0\).  On the unselected branch,
\(y<d<z\) gives
\(T_{d,z}(y)=(y-d)(y+d-2z)>0\).
Consequently its full weighted sum is strictly positive.  Both moment
constraints give the exact ledger
\begin{align}
 0<&\sum_{i\in S}a_i T_{d,z}(y_i)\notag\\
  ={}&(1-2z)M+z^2N-(d-z)^2(N-K)\notag\\
  ={}&d(2z-d)\bigl(N-2(k+m)\bigr).
 \label{eq:fixed-total-energy-signed-order}
\end{align}
The last equality uses \eqref{eq:fixed-total-lattice-moments}; it retains
the product subtraction and all mixed terms.  Since \(d(2z-d)>0\),
we obtain \(N>2(k+m)\).  With \(N=2n\), integrality is exactly
\(n\ge k+m+1\).

If \(m=0\), the second identity in
\eqref{eq:fixed-total-lattice-moments}, together with \(k>0\) and
\(z\ge1\), forces \(z=1\); the first gives \(M=K\).
Selected weight never exceeds total weight, hence \(n\ge k\).
Conversely the sites of weights \(2k,2(n-k)\) at coordinates \(1,0\)
give the required exact profile, with energy and nonlinear cost both zero.
This includes \(n=k\), where the zero-coordinate padding has weight zero.

\paragraph{Proof of fixed-total approachability.}
Retain a lattice row with \(m>0\).  The existing three-site perturbation
has fixed weights \(2m,2k,2\).  Explicitly, for sufficiently small
\(e>0\), put
\begin{align*}
 R_e&=kz+me,& S_e&=kz^2+2mde-me^2,\\
 D_e&=k\bigl((k+1)S_e-R_e^2\bigr),&
 w_e&=\frac{R_e-\sqrt{D_e}}{k+1},\\
 v_e&=\frac{R_e-w_e}{k},&u_e&=d-e.
\end{align*}
The proved perturbation theorem gives
\(0\le u_e<d<v_e\), \(0\le w_e<d\), and \(D_e>0\).
The first moment is identically
\[
 2m u_e+2k v_e+2w_e=2(kz+md)=M.
\]
The quadratic identity defining \(w_e\), combined with the second
identity in \eqref{eq:fixed-total-lattice-moments}, gives
\[
 2m u_e^2+2k v_e^2+2w_e^2=M.
\]
Only the high site is selected, so its selected weight is exactly \(2k\).
The total weight is exactly \(2(k+m+1)\), independent of \(e\).
As \(e\) tends to zero from above, the three coordinates tend to
\(d,z,0\), and continuity of the cubic energy gives
\begin{align*}
 E_e&\longrightarrow 2md(d-1)^2+2kz(z-1)^2\\
    &=2(kz+md)(1-d)(z-1)=F_d(M,K).
\end{align*}
The final equality uses the root balance.  The limiting low site is at the
inclusive cutoff and is not itself asserted admissible.  Every approximating
low site remains strictly below the cutoff; the selected weight is constant.

If \(n\ge k+m+1\), add one site at coordinate zero with weight
\(2(n-k-m-1)\).  Its first moment, second moment, selected weight and
energy are all zero.  It raises the total to precisely \(2n\), leaving
all other identities and the limiting energy unchanged.  Thus for every
\(\epsilon>0\) there is a four-site witness at the prescribed total.
The weights, including the padding weight, are fixed independently of
\(\epsilon\).  The case of zero padding causes no exception.

\paragraph{Proof that these are all the cases.}
Forgetting the total constraint in a fixed-total approaching family gives
an unrestricted arithmetic approaching family.  The previously proved
lattice-necessity theorem therefore supplies an integer \(m\ge0\)
with \(M-2kz=2dm\).  Selecting any one admissible member of the family
and applying the signed order threshold gives exactly the budget in
\eqref{eq:fixed-total-energy-criterion}.  Conversely the constructions
above supply approaching families for every permitted budget.  This proves
the full criterion for arbitrary \(n\), without a preassigned inequality
\(k\le n\).  On a fixed lattice row the same threshold applies to any
admissible profile, independently of its energy; hence feasibility alone
already implies approachability.  The reverse implication follows by taking
any positive approximation tolerance.

For \(m>0\), the lattice identities give \(M>K\), so the previously
proved strict nonlinear energy theorem applies to every finite profile and
gives \(E>F_d(M,K)\).  Together with fixed-total approachability this
identifies the exact, unattained infimum.  In particular, a proposed uniform
correction \(c(d,M,N,K)>0\) would fail on a witness with energy below
\(F_d(M,K)+c/2\).

The twelve new proof bodies are maintained in
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdFixedTotalLattice.lean} and
\texttt{Helpers/ThresholdFixedTotalSharpness.lean}.
The nonattainment assertion uses the existing strict-energy theorem.
Classical moment optimization supplies the general background; the specific
conclusion here classifies the interaction of fixed even total order, the
inclusive threshold and the arithmetic lattice.  Broad priority is not
asserted.  In the signed zero-cluster consumer, this result identifies the
optimal \(F_d\) contribution at fixed \(N\); all other proved signed
corrections retain their original hypotheses and values.  A zeta-specific
improvement would require information beyond these finite moment and total
constraints.  This optimality theorem does not enlarge the numerical
simplicity window or change the certified \(7/8\) boundary.

\subsection{Sharp block response and residual zero-copy certificates}
\label{sec:residual-block}

The global logarithmic multiplicity envelope is not an intrinsic height
limit of the compensated trace.  The following finite certificate retains
the actual trace, subtracts independently certified contributions of known
zeros, and charges any remaining zero copies.  It makes the numerical
input needed to enlarge a simplicity window explicit; it does not supply
that input by itself.

Write
\[
 K(x,y)=\frac{18}{5}D_{2,3}(x,y),\qquad
 q(H)=\frac{36}{(4+H^2)(9+H^2)},
\]
where \(D_{2,3}\) is the full paired resolvent difference, including its
\(2/3\) compensation.  Thus neither reflection nor compensation terms
are discarded.

\paragraph{Sharp block theorem.}
For every \(0\le H\le1\), \(|x|\le1/2\), and \(|y|\le H\),
\begin{equation}
 K(x,y)\ge q(H)>0.
 \label{eq:sharp-block-charge}
\end{equation}
The constant is optimal, with equality at \((x,y)=(0,H)\).  In particular,
\(q(0)=1\) and \(q(1)=18/25\).  Equivalently, a real constant \(c\)
is a valid lower bound throughout this rectangle if and only if
\(c\le q(H)\).

\paragraph{Proof.}
Put \(v=x^2\), \(w=y^2\), so that \(0\le v\le1/4\) and
\(0\le w\le1\), and set
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 A_2&=(4+w)^2+(2w-8)v+v^2,\\
 A_3&=(9+w)^2+(2w-18)v+v^2,\\
 G&=(4+w)(9+w)-(13+6w)v+v^2,\\
 F&=G(4+w)(9+w)-A_2A_3.
 \end{aligned}
\]
The exact paired-resolvent formula gives
\(K(x,y)=36G/(A_2A_3)\).  Both \(A_2\) and \(A_3\) are positive:
they are respectively the products
\(((r-x)^2+y^2)((r+x)^2+y^2)\) for \(r=2,3\), and \(|x|<r\).
The signed defect has the exact identity
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 F=v\bigl[&\tfrac7{64}
 +(1-w)(418+127w+10w^2)
 +vw(39-5w)\\
 &+4v^2(1-w)
 +(\tfrac14-v)(v^2-\tfrac{87}{4}v+\tfrac{3193}{16})\bigr].
 \end{aligned}
\]
Every term in the brackets is nonnegative on the stated domain.  For the
last factor, \(v^2-87v/4+3193/16\ge3193/16-87/16>0\).
Consequently \(F\ge7v/64\ge0\).  Dividing by the positive denominators
yields
\[
 K(x,y)\ge\frac{36}{(4+y^2)(9+y^2)}\ge q(H).
\]
The second inequality uses \(y^2\le H^2\).  At \(x=0\), the first
inequality is equality, so \(K(0,H)=q(H)\), proving optimality.
The formulas at \(H=0,1\) follow directly.  The denominators defining
\(q(H)\) are positive for every real \(H\). \(\square\)

Let \(\mathcal I\) be the existing xi divisor index, which includes
\emph{one index for each multiplicity copy}.  For \(p\in\mathcal I\),
write \(\rho_p=\beta_p+i\gamma_p\).  The exact compensated trace is
\[
 \mathcal T(t)=\frac{18}{5}
 \left(\operatorname{Tr}_{\xi}(2,t)
       -\frac23\operatorname{Tr}_{\xi}(3,t)\right)
 =\sum_{p\in\mathcal I}K(\beta_p-\tfrac12,t-\gamma_p).
\]
Here the trace parameters \(2,3\) correspond to evaluation points
\(5/2+it,7/2+it\), respectively.  The full open critical strip implies
the positivity hypotheses of the existing exact trace theorem.  Thus,
unconditionally, every finite \(W\subset\mathcal I\) satisfies
\[
 \sum_{p\in W}K(\beta_p-\tfrac12,t-\gamma_p)\le\mathcal T(t).
\]
In particular, no RH or QRH assumption is needed for this application.

\paragraph{Residual certificate.}
Fix \(t\in\mathbb R\), \(0\le H\le1\), a finite known-copy set
\(S\subset\mathcal I\), and real lower weights \(\ell_p\) on \(S\).
Suppose the independently validated inputs satisfy
\[
 \mathcal T(t)\le U,\qquad
 \ell_p\le K(\beta_p-\tfrac12,t-\gamma_p)\quad(p\in S).
\]
For every finite \(P\subset\mathcal I\setminus S\) with
\(|t-\gamma_p|\le H\) for \(p\in P\), one has
\begin{equation}
 q(H)|P|\le U-\sum_{p\in S}\ell_p.
 \label{eq:residual-copy-budget}
\end{equation}
If the right side is strictly less than \(q(H)\), every copy in the
block belongs to \(S\).  If, in addition, the map
\(p\mapsto\rho_p\) is injective on \(S\), then
\[
 \operatorname{ord}_w\zeta\le1
 \qquad(0<\Re w<1,\ |t-\Im w|\le H).
\]
If all values \(\rho_p\), \(p\in S\), lie on the critical line,
then every positive-order point in this block lies on that line.
The latter conclusion does not require injectivity.

\paragraph{Proof.}
Disjointness of \(S\) and \(P\), with all supports retained, gives
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \sum_{p\in S}\ell_p+q(H)|P|
 &\le\sum_{p\in S}K(\beta_p-\tfrac12,t-\gamma_p)
      +\sum_{p\in P}K(\beta_p-\tfrac12,t-\gamma_p)\\
 &=\sum_{p\in S\cup P}K(\beta_p-\tfrac12,t-\gamma_p)
 \le\mathcal T(t)\le U.
 \end{aligned}
\]
This proves \eqref{eq:residual-copy-budget}.  If an unaccounted block
copy existed, taking its singleton as \(P\) would contradict the strict
budget.  For each \(w\) in the open strip, the existing finite
multiplicity fiber has cardinality \(\operatorname{ord}_w\zeta\).
All its copies belong to \(S\).  Injectivity of the value map on \(S\)
forces that fiber to have cardinality at most one.  If the order is
positive, the fiber is nonempty, and any one of its copies transfers
the critical-line condition on \(S\) to \(w\). \(\square\)

The certificate does not assume the known zeros are simple.  It asks for
one chosen copy per distinct certified value; the residual argument rules
out further copies.  The lower bounds may use zeros outside the target
block, but each chosen copy is subtracted only once.  It is also valid
with a lower weight below zero; positivity is needed for the full kernel
and the block charge, not for the supplied lower weights.

The completeness method through explicit formulas has classical precedents,
including B\"uthe's 2013 method (arXiv:1308.6704).  No priority claim is made
for that general principle.  The contribution here is the specific sharp
rational block charge, its signed polynomial proof, and its formal consumer
on the actual xi multiplicity-copy index.  These results permit a larger
window to be tested using certified trace and witness bounds at each height.
They do not yet verify such bounds, enlarge the current numerical simplicity
window, or change the certified \(7/8\) boundary.

The 21 proof bodies are maintained in the following four owners and exported
through the multiplicity facade:
\begin{itemize}
\item \texttt{Helpers/ResolventBlockPolynomial.lean}: two signed defect proofs;
\item \texttt{Helpers/ResolventBlockKernel.lean}: eleven kernel and sharpness proofs;
\item \texttt{Helpers/PositiveTraceResidual.lean}: two finite residual proofs;
\item \texttt{Assembly/CarmichaelResidualCertificate.lean}: six actual xi/zeta consumers.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{A finite arithmetic certificate with height-independent error}
\label{sec:finite-euler-trace}

The abstract upper bound \(U\) in Section~\ref{sec:residual-block} can be
replaced by a finite signed arithmetic expression.  The number of Euler
terms needed for a fixed analytic error does not increase with the center
height.  This statement concerns truncation accuracy; it does not assert
that the remaining signed budget is favorable at every height.

For \(N\ge6\), define
\[
 R_N=\frac{\frac23\log N+\frac49}{N\sqrt N}.
\]
Put \(\Lambda(0)=0\) in the finite sums, with the zeroth summand interpreted
as zero, and write
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 A_N(t)&=-\sum_{n=1}^{N}
   \frac{\Lambda(n)(1-\frac{2}{3n})}{n^{5/2+it}},\\
 e_N(t)&=\frac{\Re A_N(t)+\Re A_N(-t)}2.
 \end{aligned}
\]
These are the finite complex-power definitions maintained in Lean.
Their usual real expansion is
\[
 e_N(t)=-\sum_{n=1}^{N}
  \Lambda(n)\left(1-\frac{2}{3n}\right)n^{-5/2}\cos(t\log n).
\]
Thus every phase in the retained finite packet is kept.  The real expansion
is a mathematical rewriting of the finite complex-power definition; the
consumer uses that definition directly.

Retain the complete compensated archimedean corrections
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 G(u)&=\log(1+\tfrac{81}{4}u)
       -\tfrac23\log(1+\tfrac{121}{4}u),\\
 P(y)&=\frac{3/2}{(3/2)^2+y}
       -\frac23\frac{5/2}{(5/2)^2+y},\\
 J(y)&=\frac{9/4}{(9/4)^2+y/4}
       -\frac23\frac{11/4}{(11/4)^2+y/4}.
 \end{aligned}
\]
For \(|t|\ge2\), set
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 M_N(t)={}&\frac35\log|t|-\frac35\log(2\pi)
  +\frac9{10}G(1/t^2)+\frac{18}{5}P(t^2)\\
 &-\frac9{10}J(t^2)+\frac{18}{5}e_N(t),\\
 U_N(t)={}&M_N(t)+\frac{70}{1089}+\frac{18}{5}R_N.
 \end{aligned}
\]

\paragraph{Finite trace theorem.}
Unconditionally, for every integer \(N\ge6\) and every real \(t\),
\begin{equation}
 \left|\frac{\zeta'}{\zeta}(\tfrac52+it)
       -\frac23\frac{\zeta'}{\zeta}(\tfrac72+it)-A_N(t)\right|
 \le R_N.
 \label{eq:finite-euler-error}
\end{equation}
For every \(|t|\ge2\), the actual trace from the preceding section satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal T(t)\le U_N(t).
 \label{eq:finite-euler-trace}
\end{equation}
Consequently every \(w\) in the open critical strip with \(|\Im w|\ge2\)
satisfies
\[
 \operatorname{ord}_w\zeta\le U_N(\Im w).
\]
In particular, \(U_N(\Im w)<2\) certifies order at most one without requiring
any list of zero witnesses.

\paragraph{Proof.}
The logarithmic derivative series converges absolutely on both evaluation
lines.  Its complete compensation has coefficient
\(\Lambda(n)(1-2/(3n))\), since the second evaluation line is one unit to
the right of the first.  For \(n\ge1\),
\[
 0\le1-\frac{2}{3n}\le1,\qquad 0\le\Lambda(n)\le\log n.
\]
Only after subtracting the entire signed finite head do we take the norm
of the omitted series.  Its norm is at most
\(\sum_{n>N}\log n\,n^{-5/2}\), uniformly in \(t\).

The function \(f(x)=\log x\,x^{-5/2}\) is nonnegative and decreasing for
\(x\ge6\), because
\(f'(x)=x^{-7/2}(1-\frac52\log x)\le0\) there.  Its primitive is
\[
 F(x)=-x^{-3/2}\left(\frac23\log x+\frac49\right).
\]
For every finite tail length \(L\), monotonicity gives
\[
 \sum_{j=1}^{L}f(N+j)
 \le\int_N^{N+L}f(x)\,dx
 =F(N+L)-F(N)\le-F(N).
\]
The log-series summability theorem justifies the limit \(L\to\infty\),
which proves \eqref{eq:finite-euler-error}.  In particular, this is not
a numerical check at selected heights.

Use the previously proved exact xi logarithmic-derivative identity on the
two lines, and its compensated real digamma estimate at
\(9/4+it/2\) and \(11/4+it/2\).  Substitute the finite Euler upper bound,
retaining the pole term \(P\), the inverse correction \(J\), and both
gamma logarithms in \(G\).  Normalize by \(18/5\), then average the
identities at \(t\) and \(-t\).  The signed finite Euler part becomes
\(e_N(t)\), and the existing gamma remainder is \(70/1089\).
This gives \eqref{eq:finite-euler-trace}.  Combining it with the existing
full-strip multiplicity lower charge gives the order bound; integrality
gives the simplicity test. \(\square\)

\paragraph{Enclosed-witness certificate.}
Let \(S\) be any finite set of xi multiplicity copies whose values are on
the critical line.  Suppose \(a_p\in\mathbb R\), \(d_p\ge0\), and
\[
 |\gamma_p-a_p|\le d_p\qquad(p\in S).
\]
The contribution of each chosen copy has the explicit lower bound
\[
 K(0,t-\gamma_p)\ge
 \ell_p(t):=q\bigl(|t-a_p|+d_p\bigr).
\]
This holds even when the witness lies outside the target block:
the center identity \(K(0,y)=q(y)\) and monotonicity of \(q\) in
\(|y|\) hold for all real \(y\).  Indeed,
\[
 |t-\gamma_p|\le |t-a_p|+|\gamma_p-a_p|\le |t-a_p|+d_p.
\]
Therefore, for \(0\le H\le1\), the entirely explicit inequality
\begin{equation}
 U_N(t)-\sum_{p\in S}\ell_p(t)<q(H)
 \label{eq:finite-euler-enclosed-budget}
\end{equation}
implies that every zero copy in the block \(|t-\gamma|\le H\) belongs
to \(S\).  If the values of the chosen copies are distinct, every zeta
order in that block is at most one.  This is the actual residual theorem
with its former abstract upper-bound hypothesis discharged by
\eqref{eq:finite-euler-trace} and its individual lower-weight hypotheses
discharged by the enclosure inequality.

\paragraph{A fixed 100-term budget.}
The following strict rational allowances are proved:
\[
 \frac{18}{5}R_{100}<\frac1{50}q(1),\qquad
 \frac{70}{1089}+\frac{18}{5}R_{100}<\frac3{25}q(1),
 \qquad q(1)=\frac{18}{25}.
\]
For the first inequality, \(\log100<5\), \(\sqrt{100}=10\), and the
tail formula give \(R_{100}<1/250\); multiplication by \(18/5\)
finishes the comparison.  The second is then rational arithmetic.
For completeness, the logarithm certificate uses
\[
 \log5-\log6\le-\frac16,\quad
 \log6=\log2+\log3<\frac95,\quad
 \log100=2(\log2+\log5)<5,
\]
with the existing explicit bounds on \(\log2,\log3\).

It follows that
\begin{equation}
 M_{100}(t)-\sum_{p\in S}\ell_p(t)
    <\frac{22}{25}q(1)
 \label{eq:hundred-euler-budget}
\end{equation}
certifies simplicity throughout the unit block, under the same distinctness
and enclosure hypotheses.  The Euler tail alone consumes less than 2\%
of one-copy charge; the tail and the retained gamma allowance together
consume less than 12\%.  These percentages are uniform for every
\(|t|\ge2\).  Increasing height therefore does not force more Euler terms
for this analytic allowance, although argument-evaluation precision and
the work of covering a whole height range remain separate costs.

The remaining condition is \eqref{eq:hundred-euler-budget} itself:
the favorable sign and size of the finite main residual have not been
proved at arbitrary heights.  Neither the 88\% allowance nor the
height-independent truncation error establishes a new numerical window,
a simplicity theorem for all zeros, or a QRH improvement.  Certified
transcendental evaluations and zero witnesses are still needed to apply
the finite certificate to a larger range.

Dirichlet-series truncation and the general completeness principle cited
in the preceding section are classical.  The specific maintained result
is their explicit coupling to the sharp rational block charge and the
actual xi divisor-copy index, including the fixed 100-term allowance.
Broad priority for this general strategy is not asserted.
The 23 new proof bodies are maintained in:
\begin{itemize}
\item \texttt{Helpers/EulerUniformTail.lean}: five universal tail proofs;
\item \texttt{Helpers/CompensatedEulerPacket.lean}: seven signed-packet proofs;
\item \texttt{Assembly/CarmichaelFiniteTraceCertificate.lean}: eleven actual
trace, enclosure and multiplicity consumers.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Normalization by zero count without diluting multiplicity}
\label{sec:zero-count-normalization}

The fixed-width trace counts a growing number of neighboring zeros.
Dividing that trace by a zero count divides an extra-copy signal by the
same count.  To retain an integer multiplicity signal, change the
coordinate and amplitude together.  For a center \(c\in\mathbb C\)
and density \(\lambda>0\), put
\begin{equation}
 \phi_{c,\lambda}(z)=c+\frac z\lambda,
 \qquad
 P_{c,\lambda}(z)=\frac1\lambda P(\phi_{c,\lambda}(z)).
 \label{eq:count-normalized-carrier}
\end{equation}
Here the actual probability carrier is
\[
 P(s)=-\frac{L'}{L}(s,\chi)+E(s),\qquad
 E(s)=\sum_{n\ge1}\bigl(a(n)-\chi(n)\Lambda(n)\bigr)n^{-s}.
\]
The coefficient construction already proved in the repository supplies
one \(a\), before any choice of center or density, with constants
\(n_0\ge100\) and \(C>0\) uniform in the character and accuracy order.

\paragraph{Uniform microscopic approximation.}
Suppose the accuracy order is an integer \(k\ge3\),
\(a(n)=\chi(n)\Lambda(n)\) for \(n<n_0\), and
\[
 |a(n)-\chi(n)\Lambda(n)|\le Cn^{-(k-1)}\qquad(n\ge1).
\]
For every \(\Re\phi_{c,\lambda}(z)\ge0\), the complete correction satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \left|P_{c,\lambda}(z)+\frac1\lambda
       \frac{L'}{L}(\phi_{c,\lambda}(z),\chi)\right|
 \le \frac{C}{\lambda(n_0-1)}.
 \label{eq:count-normalized-error}
\end{equation}
Indeed, on this half-plane the norm of each correction term is at most
\(C/n^2\).  The initial terms vanish exactly, and telescoping gives
\[
 \sum_{n\ge n_0}\frac1{n^2}
 \le\sum_{n\ge n_0}\left(\frac1{n-1}-\frac1n\right)
 =\frac1{n_0-1}.
\]
This bounds the analytic correction only; the signed logarithmic
derivative is retained in full.  There is no RH or zero-free-strip
hypothesis, no bound on \(\Im c\), and no resampling as \(\lambda\) varies.
The accuracy order \(k\) is the coefficient-reconstruction parameter,
not a polynomial input substituted into a family observable.

\paragraph{The multiplicity signal is unchanged.}
If \(\rho\ne1\), \(\Re\rho\ge0\), is an \(L\)-zero of multiplicity \(m\), set
\(z_\rho=\lambda(\rho-c)\).  Locally write
\(L(s,\chi)=(s-\rho)^m g(s)\), where \(g(\rho)\ne0\).  Then
\[
 L(\phi_{c,\lambda}(z),\chi)
 =\lambda^{-m}(z-z_\rho)^m g(\phi_{c,\lambda}(z)).
\]
Its analytic multiplicity is still \(m\).  The chain rule, including
its factor \(1/\lambda\), yields
\[
 P_{c,\lambda}(z)
 =-\frac{d}{dz}\log L(\phi_{c,\lambda}(z),\chi)
   +\frac{E(\phi_{c,\lambda}(z))}{\lambda}.
\]
Thus the residue at \(z_\rho\) is \(-m\), while the reconstruction error
is reduced by \(1/\lambda\).  The maintained proofs check the exact
chain identity, preservation of analytic multiplicity and meromorphic
pole order, and the unconditional zero test in the normalized coordinate.

\paragraph{Choosing the density from the actual zero count.}
Let \(N(T)\) count nontrivial zeta zeros with multiplicity, and put
\[
 n_T=N(2T)-N(T),\qquad \lambda_T=\frac{n_T}{T}>0.
\]
The classical Riemann--von Mangoldt formula gives
\(\lambda_T\sim\log T/(2\pi)\).  Thus unit distances in \(z\) correspond
to the mean-spacing scale in the original height, and
\eqref{eq:count-normalized-error} is \(O(1/\log T)\) with the same
height-independent coefficient constant.  For the xi logarithmic
derivative centered at \(c=1/2+iT\), its leading gamma background becomes
\(\log(T/(2\pi))/(2\lambda_T)\to\pi\), while each zero retains its
integer residue.  This counting asymptotic is a classical interpretation
of the proved arbitrary-positive-density theorem, not a new Lean
formalization of the zero-counting formula.

This normalization does not supply a uniform upper bound for the
normalized logarithmic derivative, remove clustered-zero contributions,
or establish a new simplicity window.  The independent arithmetic
estimate must control that retained reference term.  The new estimate is
that the actual probability-carrier correction stays uniformly small
on the changing scale, with an undiluted multiplicity signal.

\paragraph{Two-scale inverse regularization.}
Let \(f(s)=(s-\rho)^m g(s)\) in a neighborhood of \(\rho\), where
\(m\) is a natural number, \(g\) is continuous at \(\rho\), and
\(g(\rho)\ne0\). For every nonzero complex \(c\),
\[
 \lim_{\substack{h\to0\\h\ne0}}
 h^m\left(\frac1{f(\rho+h)}-\frac1{f(\rho+ch)}\right)
 =\frac{1-c^{-m}}{g(\rho)}.
\]
Indeed, for sufficiently small nonzero \(h\), the expression on the
left equals
\(g(\rho+h)^{-1}-c^{-m}g(\rho+ch)^{-1}\); continuity gives the limit.
This identity retains both inverse terms and the scale factor.
It is formalized by
\texttt{Complex.tendsto\_pow\_mul\_inverse\_difference\_of\_factor} in
\texttt{Mathlib/Analysis/Complex/TwoScaleInverse.lean}.
It supplies a local analytic interface, with no RH premise.
It does not give an upper bound on the inverse difference or on
multiplicity, and no inverse-carrier estimate is inferred from the
additive logarithmic-derivative error bound above.

\paragraph{Three-scale quotient regularization.}
Under the same local factorization, the complete quotient satisfies
\[
 \lim_{\substack{h\to0\\h\ne0}}
 \frac{f(\rho+h)\,f(\rho+3h)^3}{f(\rho+2h)^4}
 =\left(\frac{27}{16}\right)^m.
\]
For sufficiently small nonzero \(h\), its denominator is nonzero and
the quotient equals
\[
 \left(\frac{27}{16}\right)^m
 \frac{g(\rho+h)\,g(\rho+3h)^3}{g(\rho+2h)^4}.
\]
The last factor tends to one by continuity and \(g(\rho)\ne0\).
This classical local calculation is formalized by
\texttt{Complex.tendsto\_three\_scale\_ratio\_of\_factor} in
\texttt{Mathlib/Analysis/Complex/TwoScaleInverse.lean}, for every
natural \(m\), including zero, without an RH premise.
The independent arithmetic upper bound on this quotient remains open;
no absolute multiplicity bound is claimed.

\subsection{Signed two-dimensional control of a zero cluster}
\label{sec:cluster-multiplicity}

The exact-ordinate restriction can be removed while retaining the horizontal
charge.  Let \(W\) be any finite set of distinct points in the open critical
strip, let \(T\) be a real center height, and define
\[
 N_W=\sum_{w\in W}m(w),\qquad
 H_W=\sum_{w\in W}m(w)(\Re w-\tfrac12)^2,\qquad
 V_{T,W}=\sum_{w\in W}m(w)(T-\Im w)^2.
\]
Here \(m(w)=\operatorname{ord}_w\zeta\), including order zero at nonzeros.
Retain \(g>0\), \(c_3\), \(C_3\), and
\(B(T)=c_3\log|T|+C_3\) from the preceding results.

\paragraph{Theorem.}
Unconditionally, for every such \(W\) and every \(|T|\ge2\),
\begin{equation}
 gN_W+\frac{13}{2}H_W-48V_{T,W}\le gB(T).
 \label{eq:cluster-budget}
\end{equation}
Equivalently, with \(\lambda=13/(2g)\) and \(\nu=48/g\),
\[
 N_W+\lambda H_W-\nu V_{T,W}\le B(T).
\]
If \(N_W\ge(1-\eta)B(T)\), the retained moments satisfy
\[
 \lambda H_W\le\eta B(T)+\nu V_{T,W}.
\]
Thus near-saturation forces horizontal concentration up to the explicitly
measured vertical spread.  No equality of the ordinates in \(W\) is assumed.

If \(h\ge0\), \(|T-\Im w|\le h\) for every \(w\in W\), and
\(48h^2<g\), then
\begin{equation}
 (g-48h^2)N_W+\frac{13}{2}H_W\le gB(T),\qquad
 N_W\le\frac{g}{g-48h^2}B(T).
 \label{eq:cluster-window}
\end{equation}
In particular, for the whole physical height interval of half-width
\(1/16\),
\begin{equation}
 N_W\le\frac{4625194854}{28457195785}\log|T|+C_{\rm win},\qquad
 C_{\rm win}=\frac{g}{g-3/16}C_3\ge0.
 \label{eq:cluster-sixteenth}
\end{equation}
Its logarithmic coefficient is \(0.162531645385733\ldots\).
Every sum here has the stated finite support \(W\), and every order is the
actual zeta multiplicity.

\paragraph{The complete signed polynomial.}
For \(x=u^2\), \(y=v^2\), write
\[
 d_j(x,y)=(a_j^2+y-x)^2+4xy,\qquad D(x,y)=\prod_{j=1}^3d_j(x,y),
\]
\[
 N(x,y)=\sum_{j=1}^3 c_ja_j(a_j^2+y-x)\prod_{k\ne j}d_k(x,y).
\]
The product in the final expression is over
\(k\in\{1,2,3\}\setminus\{j\}\).
The exact earlier kernel identity gives \(K_3(u,v)D(x,y)=N(x,y)\).
The required new difference is the full polynomial
\[
 P(x,y)=N(x,y)-(g+\tfrac{13}{2}x-48y)D(x,y).
\]
In particular, the negative signed resolvent coefficient and every product
cross term remain inside \(P\).

\paragraph{Exact tensor Bernstein certificate.}
Set \(w_0=9/64\), \(y_0=1/24\), and
\[
 F(X,Y)=P(w_0X,y_0Y)=\sum_{k,\ell=0}^{10}f_{k\ell}X^kY^\ell,
\]
with zero coefficients where necessary.  For \(0\le i,j\le10\), put
\[
 b_{ij}=\sum_{k=0}^i\sum_{\ell=0}^j
 f_{k\ell}\frac{\binom{i}{k}}{\binom{10}{k}}
             \frac{\binom{j}{\ell}}{\binom{10}{\ell}},\qquad
 A_{ij}=S\binom{10}{i}\binom{10}{j}b_{ij},
\]
where the exact positive integer scale is
\[
 S=26759767523328000000000000000000000000000.
\]
The complete polynomial identity is
\begin{equation}
 S F(X,Y)=\sum_{i=0}^{10}\sum_{j=0}^{10}
 A_{ij}X^i(1-X)^{10-i}Y^j(1-Y)^{10-j}.
 \label{eq:cluster-bernstein}
\end{equation}
Exact rational arithmetic gives \(A_{00}=0\); the other 120 coefficients
are positive integers.  The following table records the smallest positive
coefficient in each row.  Its finite column support is
\(\mathcal J_0=\{1,\ldots,10\}\) and
\(\mathcal J_i=\{0,\ldots,10\}\) for \(1\le i\le10\).
\[
 \begin{array}{r|r|r}
 i & |\mathcal J_i| & \min_{j\in\mathcal J_i} A_{ij}\\\hline
0 & 10 & 1435847826288335768930567676541692542976 \\
1 & 11 & 17386041839141102685814931482663059456 \\
2 & 11 & 657650302994114246236600720014550331904 \\
3 & 11 & 3976432714679260747692425618268304194816 \\
4 & 11 & 11183214666950463932447398711242303533454 \\
5 & 11 & 18170199061297069792264357664937309720381 \\
6 & 11 & 18438698474708211172663650782479899051831 \\
7 & 11 & 11909751902478525826565523601745700696354 \\
8 & 11 & 4767611498538405883252717447699100918316 \\
9 & 11 & 1078752928337332422699563211742274310129 \\
10 & 11 & 105347705440134657307317366740398145631 \\
 \end{array}
\]
All coefficients, rather than sampled values of \(X,Y\), are checked in
the maintained Lean certificate.  The universal identity is proved by
ring normalization and its nonnegativity by the signs of the displayed
Bernstein factors.  Consequently \(P(x,y)\ge0\) throughout
\(0\le x\le9/64\), \(0\le y\le1/24\).

For \(|u|\le3/8\), every factor of \(D(u^2,v^2)\) is strictly positive.
On \(v^2\le1/24\), division of the preceding certificate therefore proves
\[
 K_3(u,v)\ge g+\tfrac{13}{2}u^2-48v^2.
\]
For \(v^2>1/24\), the same right side is negative, because
\(g+(13/2)(9/64)<2=48/24\), while the full joint kernel is positive.
Thus the inequality holds for every real \(v\), with no truncation or
unestimated tail.

\paragraph{Full signed consumer and physical-window proof.}
For any real function \(q\) on the finite set \(W\), complete disjoint
multiplicity-fiber transport gives
\[
 \sum_{w\in W}m(w)q(w)
 =\sum_{p\in S_W}q(\rho_p),
\]
where \(S_W\) is the finite union of all canonical multiplicity fibers.
This exact identity permits signed \(q\).  Apply it to
\(q(w)=g+(13/2)(\Re w-1/2)^2-48(T-\Im w)^2\).
The new all-frequency kernel bound charges the resulting finite sum.
The full kernel is nonnegative on the actual canonical seven-eighths
strip, so its finite sum is at most the full absolutely convergent
signed xi trace.  The already proved signed trace estimate is
\(B_3(T)\le gB(T)\).  These facts prove
\eqref{eq:cluster-budget} without taking absolute values of its main terms.
The rigidity inequality follows by subtracting the assumed lower bound
for \(N_W\).

Under the physical-window hypothesis, each
\((T-\Im w)^2\le h^2\), and multiplication by \(m(w)\ge0\) and summation
give \(V_{T,W}\le h^2N_W\).  Substitution into the same signed inequality
proves the first part of \eqref{eq:cluster-window}.  Since \(H_W\ge0\)
and \(g-48h^2>0\), division gives its second part.
For \(h=1/16\), the loss is \(48h^2=3/16<g\); exact rational
simplification yields \eqref{eq:cluster-sixteenth}.

\paragraph{Comparison and formal status.}
The preceding theorem required an exact common ordinate.  The new theorem
allows arbitrary heights and measures their effect with the retained
negative vertical second moment.  Its physical-window corollary has a
fixed explicit additive constant independent of \(T\) and \(W\).
The global coefficient \(c_3\), the numerical simplicity window, and the
zero-free boundary remain unchanged.  The fifteen new declarations have
real Lean proofs, including the complete tensor identity, signed finite
transport, unconditional moment budget, strict denominator conditions,
and the explicit interval coefficient.  No finite sample of zeta zeros
or numerical completion of an unresolved term is used.  External priority
for this quantitative refinement is not asserted.

\subsection{The exact window profile and every minimizing point}
\label{sec:exact-window-profile}

The preceding quadratic envelope can be replaced by the exact rectangle
minimum of the fixed signed three-line kernel.  Define
\[
 q(h)=K_3(0,h)=\sum_{j=1}^3\frac{c_ja_j}{a_j^2+h^2},\qquad
 c(h)=\frac{\sum_{j=1}^3c_j}{2q(h)},\qquad
 C(h)=\frac{g}{q(h)}C_3.
\]
The charge \(q(h)\) is positive for every real \(h\).  The geometric
minimum theorem below is asserted on \(0\le h\le3/20\).

\paragraph{Exact minimum theorem.}
For every \(0\le h\le3/20\), \(|u|\le3/8\), and \(|v|\le h\),
\begin{equation}
 K_3(u,v)\ge q(h),\qquad
 K_3(u,v)=q(h)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad u=0\ \hbox{and}\ v^2=h^2.
 \label{eq:exact-window-minimum}
\end{equation}
Thus the minimizing points are precisely \((0,h)\) and \((0,-h)\),
coinciding when \(h=0\).  In particular, for every real candidate charge
\(b\),
\[
 \bigl(\forall |u|\le3/8,\ |v|\le h:\ b\le K_3(u,v)\bigr)
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad b\le q(h).
\]
This is optimality of the uniform pointwise charge for this fixed kernel
and these rectangles.  No optimization over all signed filters, or over
widths beyond \(3/20\), is asserted.

\paragraph{Unconditional continuous window theorem.}
Let \(|T|\ge2\), and let \(W\) be any finite set in the open critical
strip with \(|T-\Im w|\le h\) for every \(w\in W\), where
\(0\le h\le3/20\).  Then
\begin{equation}
 N_W=\sum_{w\in W}m(w)\le c(h)\log|T|+C(h).
 \label{eq:exact-window-count}
\end{equation}
Here \(m(w)\) is the actual zeta order, including order zero at nonzeros.
The constant is fixed explicitly before \(T\) and \(W\) vary.
Three compiled specializations have the following exact coefficients:
\[
 \begin{array}{c|c|c}
 h & c(h) & \text{decimal value}\\\hline
\frac{1}{16} & \frac{24507727000183581493}{180840288877455431220} & 0.1355213882\ldots \\
\frac{1}{8} & \frac{3307467502813082321}{22628332539800442840} & 0.1461648797\ldots \\
\frac{3}{20} & \frac{319946206530171}{2096481612731132} & 0.1526110244\ldots \\
 \end{array}
\]
The \(3/20\)-window has \(12/5\) times the width of the earlier
\(1/16\)-window.  Nevertheless, for every \(|T|\ge2\), its entire
right-hand side satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \frac{319946206530171}{2096481612731132}\log|T|+C(3/20)
 \le\frac{4625194854}{28457195785}\log|T|+C_{\rm win}.
 \label{eq:exact-window-domination}
\end{equation}
The comparison includes the additive constants, not only the logarithmic
coefficient.

\paragraph{Strict signed axis decrease.}
Write \(r(y)=\sum_jc_ja_j/(a_j^2+y)\) and
\[
 L(y)=\prod_{j=1}^3(a_j^2+y)=d+py+sy^2+y^3,
\]
where \(d=\prod_ja_j^2\), \(p=\sum_{i<j}a_i^2a_j^2\), and
\(s=\sum_ja_j^2\).  With \(\tau=\sum_jc_ja_j\), set
\[
 a_0=gd,\qquad a_1=s\tau-\sum_jc_ja_j^3,\qquad a_2=\tau.
\]
Exact common-denominator algebra gives
\(r(y)L(y)=a_0+a_1y+a_2y^2\).  Put
\[
 b_0=a_0p-a_1d,\qquad b_1=a_0s-a_2d,\qquad
 b_2=a_0+a_1s-a_2p.
\]
All six rational numbers \(a_0,a_1,a_2,b_0,b_1,b_2\) are strictly
positive for the stated filter.  The complete signed divided difference is
\begin{align*}
 &(r(y)-r(z))L(y)L(z)=(z-y)H(y,z),\\
 &H(y,z)=b_0+b_1(y+z)+a_0(y^2+z^2)+b_2yz
       +a_1yz(y+z)+a_2y^2z^2.
\end{align*}
For \(y,z\ge0\), both denominators and \(H\) are positive.
Consequently \(r\) is strictly decreasing on the whole nonnegative
axis.  This proves \(K_3(0,v)\ge K_3(0,h)\) when \(v^2\le h^2\),
with equality exactly when \(v^2=h^2\).  The negative second weight
is retained until the full divided difference has been formed.

\paragraph{Strict transverse difference.}
Retain \(d_j(x,y)\), \(D(x,y)\), and \(N(x,y)\) from the preceding
subsection.  Define the polynomial
\[
 Q(x,y)=L(y)\sum_{j=1}^3c_ja_j(a_j^2-x-3y)
       \prod_{k\in\{1,2,3\}\setminus\{j\}}d_k(x,y)(a_k^2+y).
\]
The full coordinate identity is
\begin{equation}
 (K_3(u,v)-K_3(0,v))D(u^2,v^2)D(0,v^2)
   =u^2Q(u^2,v^2).
 \label{eq:exact-window-transverse}
\end{equation}
It includes every signed numerator, denominator, and cross term.
On \(|u|\le3/8\) all denominator factors are positive.  We prove the
strict polynomial inequality
\[
 Q(x,y)>0\qquad(0\le x\le9/64,\ 0\le y\le9/400)
\]
by the following finite exact certificate.

Set \(F(X,Y)=Q((9/64)X,(9/400)Y)=\sum f_{k\ell}X^kY^\ell\).
Its degree is at most five in \(X\) and ten in \(Y\).  For
\(0\le i\le5\), \(0\le j\le10\), define
\[
 \beta_{ij}=\sum_{k=0}^i\sum_{\ell=0}^j f_{k\ell}
  \frac{\binom{i}{k}}{\binom{5}{k}}
  \frac{\binom{j}{\ell}}{\binom{10}{\ell}}.
\]
There are exactly 66 such rational coefficients, and all are positive.
Let \(\mu=\tfrac12\min_{i,j}\beta_{ij}\).  For the exact positive
integers
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathcal S&=524288000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000,\\
 \mathcal R&=40321255493226389739445755074329909385682279296615625.
 \end{aligned}
\]
we have \(\mathcal R=\mathcal S\mu\), and the integers
\(A_{ij}=\mathcal S(\beta_{ij}-\mu)\binom{5}{i}\binom{10}{j}\)
are all positive.  The full identity is
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal S F(X,Y)=\mathcal R+
 \sum_{i=0}^{5}\sum_{j=0}^{10}A_{ij}
 X^i(1-X)^{5-i}Y^j(1-Y)^{10-j}.
 \label{eq:exact-window-transverse-certificate}
\end{equation}
For completeness, the smallest integer coefficient in each full row
\(j\in\{0,\ldots,10\}\) is:
\[
 \begin{array}{r|r}
 i & \min_{0\le j\le10}A_{ij}\\\hline
0 & 2870406609453477111160584812007246766270563877749384375 \\
1 & 9947799725668726895996096356930895890586117186239621875 \\
2 & 12990638596670728413070009382405025451020679898015268750 \\
3 & 7629558311328276010151636439797677080787054426043325000 \\
4 & 1757676171139975286866002944292490643097126717377409375 \\
5 & 40321255493226389739445755074329909385682279296615625 \\
 \end{array}
\]
The 66 explicit coefficients and the positive constant reserve appear in
the maintained Lean definition.  Ring normalization checks the whole
identity, and the signs of the Bernstein factors prove positivity on the
entire closed unit square.  No grid of values of \(u,v,h\) is used.

It now follows from \eqref{eq:exact-window-transverse} that
\(K_3(u,v)\ge K_3(0,v)\), strictly when \(u\ne0\), throughout the
stated physical box.  Combining this with strict axis decrease proves
\eqref{eq:exact-window-minimum} and both equality conditions.  Testing
any proposed uniform charge at \((0,h)\) proves its optimality statement.

\paragraph{Complete multiplicity consumer.}
The proved unconditional canonical strip places every actual divisor
index at \(|\Re\rho-1/2|\le3/8\).  Form the finite union of every
multiplicity fiber above \(W\), retaining all copies.  The physical
window hypothesis and \eqref{eq:exact-window-minimum} charge each copy
by \(q(h)\).  All other terms in the full joint kernel are nonnegative.
The complete signed trace estimate therefore gives
\[
 q(h)N_W\le B_3(T)\le
 \frac{\sum_jc_j}{2}\log|T|+gC_3.
\]
Division by the proved positive charge yields
\eqref{eq:exact-window-count}.  Exact rational evaluation gives the
three displayed coefficients.  The new \(3/20\) coefficient is smaller
than the old \(1/16\) coefficient, and
\[
 \frac{g}{q(3/20)}\le\frac{g}{g-3/16}.
\]
Since \(C_3\ge0\) and \(\log|T|\ge0\), these two inequalities prove
\eqref{eq:exact-window-domination} through the full signed consumer.

\paragraph{Scope and formal status.}
The continuous family, strict minimum classification, arbitrary-candidate
optimal-charge equivalence, all three numerical specializations, and the
comparison of entire right-hand sides have real Lean proofs.  All 28 new
declarations are maintained in the repository.  The global pointwise
coefficient \(c_3\), numerical simplicity window, and zero-free boundary
are unchanged.  This exact profile is established on the declared width
range only; no largest possible width or optimality among all filters
is claimed.  External priority for the refinement is not asserted.

\subsection{Effective constants and a numerical simplicity window}
\label{sec:effective-multiplicity-window}

The additive constant in \eqref{eq:sharp-global-multiplicity} can be
made explicit. Put \(B(a)=|\zeta'(a)/\zeta(a)|\),
\(h=(a-1)/2\), and
\begin{align*}
 H(a)&=\frac1{a-1}+B(a)+\frac{|\log\pi|}{2}
       +\frac{a+8+|\log3|+|\gamma_E|}{2},\\
 L(a)&=\frac{2B(1+h)}h+\frac1{(a-1)^2}
           +\frac14\sum_{n\ge0}\frac1{(n+1)^2}.
\end{align*}
For the radii \(r=r_\varepsilon\), \(R=R_*\) selected above,
\(a=1/2+r\), the maintained explicit witness is
\[
 C_\varepsilon^{\rm coarse}
       =\frac{rR}{r+R}\bigl(H(a)+rL(a)\bigr).
\]
This witness is greater than two. That fact concerns this particular
witness, not the existence of a better constant. A direct signed
estimate at fixed radii gives the following different tradeoff.

\paragraph{Effective multiplicity and simplicity window.}
For every \(w\) with \(0<\Re w<1\), writing
\(T=|\Im w|\) and \(m(w)=\operatorname{ord}_w\zeta\),
\begin{align*}
 T\ge2&\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
       m(w)\le\frac35\log T+\frac45,\\
 T\ge6&\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
       m(w)\le\frac35\log T+\frac{11}{100}.
\end{align*}
In particular, every nontrivial zero with
\[
                   2\le T<e^{63/20}\approx23.34
\]
is simple. The two inequalities hold without assuming RH and do not
require the narrower \(7/8\) strip.

\paragraph{Proof.}
We retain the two-line compensation at \(r=2\), \(R=3\).
The positive finite-strip kernel is valid on the entire critical strip,
and its same-height charge is \(g=1/r-r/R^2=5/18\).
Thus the proved zero-fiber inequality gives
\[
 m(w)\le\frac{18}{5}\left(A_2(\Im w)-\frac23A_3(\Im w)\right).
\]
We give every independent analytic input to the upper bound.

First, the real Euler bound is
\(B(5/2)\le3/10\). Its certificate uses the convergent logarithmic
Dirichlet series and the exact von Mangoldt convolution:
\[
 B(5/2)\,|\zeta(5/2)|
     =\left|\sum_{n\ge1}\frac{\log n}{n^{5/2}}\right|.
\]
The following rational bounds are proved with infinite tails retained:
\[
 \sum_{n=1}^{6}\frac{\log n}{n^{5/2}}
   +6^{-3/2}\left(\frac23\log6+\frac49\right)
       \le\frac{159}{400},\qquad
 \sum_{n=1}^{12}n^{-5/2}\ge\frac{53}{40}.
\]
Indeed \(f(x)=x^{-5/2}\log x\) decreases for \(x\ge6\), and
\(-x^{-3/2}(\frac23\log x+\frac49)\) is its exact primitive.
The finite sum--integral inequality bounds every partial tail by its
value at six; summability then passes the inequality to the full tail.
The rational certificate uses
\(\log2<0.693148\), \(\log3<1.098613\),
\(\log5\le\log6-1/6\), and rational square-root enclosures.
The ratio \((159/400)/(53/40)\) is exactly \(3/10\).

The common-height Euler comparison is essential. For \(\Re s=a>1\)
and \(0\le\lambda\le1\), the entire compensated quotient satisfies
\[
 \left|\frac{\zeta'}{\zeta}(s)
       -\lambda\frac{\zeta'}{\zeta}(s+1)\right|\le B(a).
\]
Before estimation, its von Mangoldt coefficient is
\(\Lambda(n)n^{-s}(1-\lambda/n)\), up to the common minus sign.
The factor \(1-\lambda/n\) lies in \([0,1]\); no separate bounds
on the two Euler quotients are substituted. At \(a=5/2\),
\(\lambda=2/3\), the normalized Euler allowance is therefore
\((18/5)(3/10)=27/25\).

For the gamma contribution the maintained remainder theorem proves
\[
 \left|\Re\psi(z)-\log|z|+\frac12\Re\frac1z\right|
       \le\frac1{8(\Re z)^2}\qquad(\Re z\ge1).
\]
For completeness, set \(f_z(x)=\Re(1/(z+x))\). The finite
trapezoid identity retains its endpoint term, and on each unit interval
its error is bounded by
\[
 \frac1{8(u+n)^2}-\frac1{8(u+n+1)^2},\qquad u=\Re z.
\]
This follows from the Peano weight \(x(1-x)/2\) and
\(|f_z''(x)|\le2/(u+x)^3\). Summing telescopes; the digamma series
and harmonic logarithm limit give the displayed remainder. In
particular, the inverse endpoint correction is retained when taking
upper and lower bounds on the two gamma terms.

Put \(q=1/T^2\), \(y=T^2\), and define
\begin{align*}
 D(q)&=\log(1+81q/4)-\frac23\log(1+121q/4),\\
 P(y)&=\frac{3/2}{9/4+y}-\frac{5/3}{25/4+y},\\
 J(y)&=\frac{9/4}{81/16+y/4}-\frac{11/6}{121/16+y/4}.
\end{align*}
Using the exact identity
\[
 \frac{\xi'}{\xi}(s)=\frac{\zeta'}{\zeta}(s)+\frac1{s-1}
             -\frac12\log\pi+\frac12\psi(s/2+1),
\]
the complete normalized signed bound is
\begin{align*}
 m(w)\le{}&\frac35\log T+\frac{27}{25}-\frac35\log(2\pi)
              +\frac{70}{1089}\\
          &+\frac9{10}D(1/T^2)+\frac{18}{5}P(T^2)
                                      -\frac9{10}J(T^2).
\end{align*}
The remainder \(70/1089\) is the sum of the two explicit gamma
errors at real parts \(9/4\) and \(11/4\), after compensation
and division by \(g\). The same bound applies at both signs of the
height, so averaging gives the required paired trace.

All remaining inequalities are rational or elementary logarithmic
inequalities, proved on their complete ranges:
\begin{align*}
 0\le q\le1/4&:\quad D(q)\le1/2,\\
 0\le q\le1/36&:\quad \frac9{10}D(q)\le\frac{17043}{449440},\\
 y\ge4&:\quad \frac{18}{5}P(y)\le\frac7{25},\\
 y\ge36&:\quad P(y)\le0,\qquad
 y\ge0:\quad J(y)\ge0.
\end{align*}
For example, \(3D(q)\) is the logarithm of
\((1+81q/4)^3/(1+121q/4)^2\). On the first range this ratio is at
most \(49/16=(7/4)^2\), so \(\log x\le x-1\) proves
\(D(q)\le1/2\). On the second range the ratio is at most
\(50625/44944\); the same logarithmic inequality gives the stated
bound. These ratio bounds are polynomial inequalities on the full
intervals, not finite samples.

Finally \(\log(2\pi)>9/5\), so the Euler allowance is canceled
by the retained constant \(-(3/5)\log(2\pi)\). The remaining
allowances are
\[
 \frac{70}{1089}+\frac9{20}+\frac7{25}<\frac45,
 \qquad
 \frac{70}{1089}+\frac{17043}{449440}<\frac{11}{100}.
\]
This proves both multiplicity bounds. For \(2\le T\le6\),
\(\log6<2\) makes the first bound strictly less than two.
For \(6\le T<e^{63/20}\), the second is strictly less than two.
Integrality of the analytic order completes the proof.

The effective constant \(4/5<2\) uses logarithmic coefficient
\(3/5\), not a coefficient arbitrarily close to \(c_*\).
The near-optimal asymptotic theorem is retained separately.
The maintained declarations are
\texttt{zeta\_multiplicity\_effective\_low},
\texttt{zeta\_multiplicity\_effective\_high}, and
\texttt{zeta\_simplicity\_window} in
\texttt{Assembly/CarmichaelEffectiveMultiplicity.lean}.
The independent analytic inputs are in
\texttt{Helpers/RealDigammaRemainder.lean},
\texttt{Helpers/EulerRealHalfIntegerBounds.lean}, and
\texttt{Helpers/CompensatedXiConstants.lean}.
This finite window does not establish simplicity at unbounded heights;
the positive logarithmic term still grows. No improvement of the
certified zero-free boundary is asserted.

\subsection{A probability-carrier criterion for simplicity of all nontrivial zeros}

The numerical window above is now established. The corresponding global
question admits an exact criterion in terms of small values of a positive
probability Dirichlet series. Define
\[
 a_n=\frac{\varphi(n)}n\quad(n\ge1),\qquad a_0=0,\qquad
 \mathcal P(s)=\frac{\zeta(s)}{\zeta(s+1)}.
\]
The number \(a_n\in[0,1]\) is the probability that a uniform residue modulo
\(n\) is coprime to \(n\). For \(\Re s>1\),
\[
 \mathcal P(s)=\sum_{n\ge1}a_n n^{-s}.
\]
For \(w\) in the open critical strip, let
\[
 A_{w,R}(\varepsilon)=\operatorname{Area}
 \{z\in\mathbb C:|z-w|<R,\ |\mathcal P(z)|\le\varepsilon\},
\]
where Area denotes two-dimensional Lebesgue measure.

\paragraph{Global simplicity criterion.}
All nontrivial zeros of \(\zeta\) are simple if and only if
\[
 \forall w\ (0<\Re w<1)\quad\exists R>0\quad
 A_{w,R}(\varepsilon)=o(\varepsilon)
       \qquad(\varepsilon\downarrow0).
\]
Explicitly, after choosing \(R\), for every \(c>0\) there is
\(\delta>0\) such that
\(0<\varepsilon<\delta\) implies
\(A_{w,R}(\varepsilon)\le c\varepsilon\).
The radius and constants may depend on \(w\). This is a local condition
at every point of the strip; no uniformity in height is assumed.

\paragraph{Proof of the carrier identity and order transfer.}
The exact arithmetic convolution
\(\sum_{d\mid n}\varphi(d)=n\) gives
\[
 \left(\sum_{n\ge1}\varphi(n)n^{-(s+1)}\right)\zeta(s+1)=\zeta(s).
\]
All products and sums here converge absolutely when \(\Re s>1\):
\(0\le\varphi(n)\le n\) bounds the first series by the ordinary
zeta series at \(\Re s\). Dividing by the nonzero \(\zeta(s+1)\)
and shifting each term proves the probability-series identity.
If \(\Re w>0\) and \(w\ne1\), then \(\zeta\) is analytic at \(w\),
while \(\zeta(w+1)\) is analytic and nonzero because
\(\Re(w+1)>1\). Thus \(\mathcal P\) is analytic there and
\[
 \operatorname{ord}_w\mathcal P=\operatorname{ord}_w\zeta.
\]
These orders are finite, as follows also from the existing nonzero entire
xi function and its local unit factorization. No RH hypothesis enters.

\paragraph{Proof of the area threshold.}
For an analytic function \(f\) of finite order \(m\) at \(w\), write
\(f(z)=(z-w)^m g(z)\) near \(w\), where \(g\) is analytic and
\(g(w)\ne0\). If \(m\le1\), shrink a ball of radius at most one
so that \(|g(z)|\ge c>0\). Then
\(|f(z)|\ge c|z-w|\), including the case \(m=0\). Hence
\[
 \operatorname{Area}\{|z-w|<R,\ |f(z)|\le\varepsilon\}
       \le\pi(\varepsilon/c)^2=o(\varepsilon).
\]
Conversely, if \(m\ge2\), shrink a ball of radius at most one so
that \(|g(z)|\le C\), with \(C>0\). On that ball,
\(|f(z)|\le C|z-w|^2\). For every sufficiently small positive
\(\varepsilon\), the disk of radius \(\sqrt{\varepsilon/C}\)
is contained in both that ball and any proposed ball in the criterion.
Its area is \(\pi\varepsilon/C\), so the sublevel area cannot be
\(o(\varepsilon)\). This proves the equivalence. Applying it to
\(\mathcal P\) and using exact order transfer proves the assertion.
The distinction between planar area and length along a line is essential.

\paragraph{What the numerical window already supplies.}
The area condition is proved for every \(w\) with
\(0<\Re w<1\) and \(2\le|\Im w|<e^{63/20}\). Consequently global
simplicity is equivalent to the same condition only on the complement
of this window, namely \(|\Im w|<2\) or \(|\Im w|\ge e^{63/20}\).
The low-height complement has not been silently removed.

The three maintained modules
\texttt{ZeroSmallValueArea}, \texttt{CoprimalityDirichletCarrier}, and
\texttt{CarmichaelSimplicityCriterion} contain the full proofs, including
the probability interpretation, convergence, both geometric implications,
and the window specialization. The criterion does not itself establish
the independent global small-value estimate. Positive coefficients alone
do not imply such an estimate, and the logarithmic multiplicity bound
still grows with height. No proof of global simplicity or improvement
of the certified \(7/8\) zero-free boundary is claimed. The local analytic
mechanism is standard; novelty priority for this formulation is unestablished.

\subsection{Exact Mellin action on general windows}
\label{sec:general-window-mellin}

Let
\[
 \mathcal M W(s)=\int_0^\infty x^{s-1}W(x)\,dx,\qquad
 \widetilde W(x)=\frac{W(x)-3W(2x)+2W(4x)}6.
\]
For every \(s\in\mathbb C\) where the first integral is absolutely
convergent, the filtered integral is also absolutely convergent and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:general-window-mellin}
 \mathcal M\widetilde W(s)
 =\frac{(1-2^{-s})(1-2^{1-s})}{6}\,\mathcal M W(s).
\end{equation}
This is an integral identity for a general window, not only a calculation
on the formal power response \(D^s\).

\emph{Proof.}
For any positive real \(a\), the substitution \(u=ax\) gives
\[
 \mathcal M[W(a\,\cdot)](s)=a^{-s}\mathcal M W(s)
\]
and preserves Mellin convergence. Linearity applied to the three
convergent integrals gives the coefficient
\((1-3\cdot2^{-s}+2\cdot4^{-s})/6\), which factors as in
\eqref{eq:general-window-mellin}. \(\square\)

If \(0<\beta=\Re s<1\), the existing multiplier estimate now applies
directly to the actual integral:
\[
 \frac{(1-2^{-\beta})(2^{1-\beta}-1)}6\,|\mathcal M W(s)|
 \le |\mathcal M\widetilde W(s)|.
\]
Its coefficient is positive. Thus a nonzero base Mellin value survives
the filter. The base value itself is still present in this bound;
no uniform lower bound for it in height is asserted.
The maintained proofs are
\texttt{GaussianMellinWavelet.window\_hasMellin},
\texttt{window\_mellin\_ne\_zero}, and
\texttt{window\_mellin\_norm\_lower}, in
\texttt{Assembly/DetectorPackets/GaussianMellinWavelet.lean}.
The construction of a responsive radial bandlimited base window
remains a separate obligation.

\subsection{An exact band-limited Type I estimate}
\label{sec:bandlimited-type-i}

A different window class removes the principal Type I block exactly.
Here \emph{Gaussian} refers to the lattice \(\mathbb Z[i]\).
An exponential Gaussian test function is not band-limited.

Let \(g\) be a complex Schwartz function on \(\mathbb C\), with
\(\widehat g(0)=0\) and \(\widehat g(\xi)=0\) for \(|\xi|\ge1\).
For every real \(q>0\), \(X>0\), \(q^2\le X\), and \(r\in\mathbb C\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bandlimited-coset-zero}
 \sum_{n\in\mathbb Z^2}
   g\!\left(\frac{qn+r}{\sqrt X}\right)=0.
\end{equation}
The sum is absolutely convergent. Identifying \(n=n_1+in_2\),
affine Poisson summation gives
\[
 \frac{X}{q^2}\sum_{k\in\mathbb Z^2}
   e^{2\pi i\, k\cdot r/q}\,
   \widehat g\!\left(\frac{\sqrt X}{q}k\right).
\]
The zero frequency vanishes by hypothesis. Every other frequency has
norm at least \(\sqrt X/q\ge1\), and vanishes by support.
Every shift phase remains in this calculation.
For an integer \(q\ge1\), the complete combination of all \(q^2\)
cosets with weights \(\chi(r_1^2+r_2^2)\) therefore also vanishes,
for any Dirichlet character modulo \(q\). The theorem includes
\(q=1,2\) and composite moduli.

Suppose \(g(z)=V(|z|^2)\) and \(V(0)=0\). For nonzero ideals and
a nonzero ideal \(H\), write
\[
 S_H(V;X)=\sum_{\substack{C\ne0\\(C,H)=1}}V(N(C)/X).
\]
The four-unit reindexing and complete divisor mask give
\[
 S_H(V;X)=\sum_{E\mid H}\mu(E)
          \sum_{C\ne0}V\bigl(N(C)/(X/N(E))\bigr).
\]
Hence \(S_H(V;X)=0\) whenever \(N(H)\le X\). For any finite sets
\(A,B\) of nonzero ideals, the entire signed block satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bandlimited-type-i-zero}
 \sum_{a\in A}\sum_{b\in B}\mu(a)\mu(b)
       S_H\!\left(V;\frac{D}{N(a)N(b)}\right)=0
\end{equation}
if \(D>0\) and \(N(H)N(a)N(b)\le D\) for every retained pair.
All divisor terms, including ramified and repeated factors, remain.
No norm estimate is applied to this main block.

The existing three-scale wavelet supplies both origin conditions.
If \(f(z)=W(|z|^2)\) and \(\widehat f(\xi)=0\) for \(|\xi|\ge1/2\),
then \(g=\operatorname{wavelet}(f)\) has Fourier support inside the
unit disk, \(g(0)=\widehat g(0)=0\), and
\(V=\operatorname{window}(W)\). The dilation identity
\[
 \widehat{f(\sqrt a\,\cdot)}(\xi)
       =a^{-1}\widehat f(\xi/\sqrt a)
\]
proves the support statement for \(a=2,4\).
The maintained owner is
\texttt{Assembly/DetectorPackets/GaussianBandlimitedTypeI.lean},
including \texttt{wavelet\_typeI\_zero} and
\texttt{character\_coset\_sum\_zero}.

\paragraph{Its complete consumer and remaining corrections.}
The earlier generic estimate was
\[
 |T_I-\mathrm{main}_I|
       \le (81/4)C_f\,\tau(H)\,UV.
\]
On the new window class the entire block is zero under
\(N(H)UV\le D\). Fixed masks thus permit \(U,V\) of order
\(\sqrt D\), rather than imposing \(UV\le D^{3/4}\) merely to
fit a \(D^{3/4}\) allowance. This is a component estimate.

Finite support remains explicit. If \(L_{\le Y}\) is the low-low
block in a finite ideal prefix, the theorem concerns its full
cofactor counterpart \(L_\infty\). Set
\(L_{\rm out}=L_\infty-L_{\le Y}\). After the required reindexing,
the two-cutoff identity retains
\[
 P_{\le Y}=E_U+E_V-L_{\le Y}+H_{\le Y}
          =E_U+E_V+H_{\le Y}+L_{\rm out},
 \qquad L_\infty=0.
\]
The outside correction has a plus sign. A total-ideal coprimality
restriction also restricts \(a,b\); a cofactor-only mask cannot
replace it. The endpoint, outside-tail and high-high bounds, their
energy cross terms, and the twisted-ideal adapter remain separate.
Removing the physical origin from the character coset sum also
uses \(g(0)=0\).

The wavelet multiplier is nonzero throughout the critical strip.
Applying this window class to zero exclusion additionally requires
a nonzero base Mellin response at the hypothetical zero; Fourier
support alone does not establish that condition. This section
proves component vanishing, not a completed band-limited RH
criterion. Novelty priority is unestablished. The certified
boundary remains \(7/8\).
